# Earth Online: the native build

`native/` is a Cargo workspace that builds Earth Online as a desktop
program: a GPU-driven renderer on [wgpu](https://wgpu.rs) that draws the
whole of San Francisco from open data, with streets, buildings, terrain,
water, sky and weather, simulated traffic and people, a walking and driving
player, sound, an interface and an online server.

The project is early and in active development. Many systems are first
versions, some content is missing, and the program changes quickly. Nothing
in this document is a statement about how a frame looks: the project's
recorded visual-quality verdict is REJECT (see
[Docs/VISUAL_QUALITY_GATE.md](../Docs/VISUAL_QUALITY_GATE.md)), and only a
new blind review can change it.

## Crates

| Crate | What it holds |
| --- | --- |
| `eo-data` | Loaders for the city files in `public/data/sf/` (streets, footprints, elevation, land cover, roof colours) and the California street standards registry. No GPU. |
| `eo-assets` | Hash-verified model loading (GLB), the binary model cache, the building kit's material registry and map bake, placement of the hand-built buildings, the scanned surface sets. |
| `eo-kit` | The procedural building planner: classification, facade layout, storeys and bays, shop fronts, roofs, and the mesh emitters for each level of detail. No GPU. |
| `eo-street` | The street surface: cross sections, junctions, corners, kerb ramps, crossings, lane paint, tile meshes, and the building floor and foundation rules. No GPU. |
| `eo-look` | The lighting model as pure functions: sun position, analytic sky, fog, the light rig, exposure, tone curve and shadow cascade fitting. No GPU. |
| `eo-sky` | Weather schedule, clouds, marine layer, rain and wetness, moon and stars, street-lamp photometry. No GPU. |
| `eo-water` | The shoreline field, the FFT ocean spectrum, wind, water optics, surf and the open-sea grid. No GPU. |
| `eo-landmarks` | Bridges, the quay wall, piers, seawalls, plazas, landmark towers, the Ferry Building, the corridor's streetcar rails and quay railing, and the open-space ground as plain meshes. No GPU. |
| `eo-farshore` | The land beyond the city out to the horizon, cooked from elevation tiles. No GPU. |
| `eo-structures` | Grade-separated roads (freeways, ramps, road bridges, tunnels): their vertical profiles, deck, support and portal meshes and collision triangles, behind `--structures`; the joins a host builds the street again with (`build_joined`: junction legs, capped, raised and lowered stubs), the tunnels' bores, lights and terrain cuts (`--tunnels`). It reads the street plan and never edits the street surface itself (eo-street draws the joins). No GPU. |
| `eo-props` | Street furniture, traffic and pedestrian signals, signs and sign atlases, street trees, parked cars, placed per tile by rule. No GPU. |
| `eo-sim` | The headless world simulation: traffic, signals, pedestrians, population and level of detail, tile-keyed and multi-threaded. No GPU. Its `traffic_lab` example writes an offline top-down replay of the traffic rules on synthetic junctions ([Docs/TRAFFIC-LAB.md](../Docs/TRAFFIC-LAB.md)). |
| `eo-agents` | Vehicle bodies and people cooked into one GPU-ready library (wheel rigs, lamps, skeletal motion, atlases), and the feed from the simulation to the renderer. No GPU. |
| `eo-player` | The on-foot controller, walk and drive cameras, the player's car, input mapping and the streamed collision world, on Rapier. No GPU. |
| `eo-render` | Scene build and the wgpu renderer: GPU culling and LOD, occlusion, streaming and uploads, and every render pass. |
| `eo-ui` | Title screen, menus, settings, map and HUD, drawn with wgpu over the 3D frame. |
| `eo-audio` | Sound bank, real-time 3D mixer, output devices and the city's sound director. |
| `eo-net` | Online foundation: UDP transport, handshake, snapshots, client prediction and interpolation. No GPU. |
| `eo-server` | The headless authoritative server binary, scripted bots and network benches. No GPU. |
| `eo-app` | The game binary: window, fly-through, `--play`, `--ui`, `--connect`, and the offscreen bench. |

Other folders:

- `assets/` holds small tracked inputs: manifests of the [optional
  assets](#optional-assets), the sound credits, the roof-colour and ground
  tables, the fallback font, and the placement modules of the hand-built
  buildings.
- `tools/` holds the Node scripts that made the optional assets'
  provenance manifests from their sources. Inside the repository they
  download only into `external/<set>`; the manifests they rewrite are the
  tracked ones in `assets/` and `eo-props/data/`.
- `*/data/` folders hold reference answers and tables that the crates and
  their tests read. Some were exported from the earlier browser
  implementation of the game, whose exporters are no longer in this
  repository; treat them as fixed test fixtures.
- `vendor/wgpu-hal/` is wgpu-hal 30.0.2 with one change to its Metal
  backend: a shader module whose label contains `[relaxed-math]` is compiled
  in Metal's relaxed math mode instead of fast math (the agents' draw shader
  with the cinematic look; every other shader as upstream). The workspace
  uses it through `[patch.crates-io]`; its `EARTH-ONLINE.md` records the
  source, the change and how to carry it to a new wgpu.
- `bench-out/`, `cache/` and `target/` are generated per machine and
  ignored by Git.

## Requirements

- A stable Rust toolchain that supports edition 2024 and let chains (Rust
  1.88 or newer), installed with [rustup](https://rustup.rs).
- A GPU and driver that wgpu supports: Vulkan or DirectX 12 on Windows,
  Vulkan on Linux, Metal on macOS. The project is developed and tested on
  Windows. macOS (Metal) builds, runs the bench and runs the tests, but is
  not a reference platform; Linux builds are untested.
- A C/C++ compiler for the crates that build native code (`meshopt` and
  others). On Windows that is the MSVC build tools; on Linux, `gcc` or
  `clang`.
- On Linux, the ALSA development package (for example `libasound2-dev`) for
  the audio output.
- Disk space: the first run cooks models, building tiles and textures into
  `native/cache/`, which grows to several gigabytes.
- Python 3 (its standard library only) for `scripts/setup-assets.py`, the
  one-time download of the [optional assets](#optional-assets) into
  `external/` at the repository root (about 2 GB more disk).
- Node.js only if you use the scripts in `tools/` or the asset-store scripts
  at the repository root.

## Build and run

First-time setup, once, from the repository root: download the optional
assets (people, motions, scanned surfaces, sound, sign art, far-shore
elevation; about 2 GB) into the git-ignored `external/` folder. Run it again
after a pull that changes a manifest; files already present and verified
are skipped, and an interrupted run resumes.

```sh
python3 scripts/setup-assets.py
```

The game runs without them too, with those features off (see
[Optional assets](#optional-assets)). Run every other command from
`native/`.

```sh
cargo build --release
cargo run --release -p eo-app                  # windowed fly-through
cargo run --release -p eo-app -- --play        # walk and drive
cargo run --release -p eo-app -- --ui          # title screen, HUD and pause menu over the fly-through
```

The first start is slow: the city is read, models and building tiles are
cooked and cached, and shaders are built. Later starts read the cache.

The release profile keeps line-level debug information (`debug = 1`), and
dependencies are optimised in the dev profile too, so a debug build is
usable but a release build is the one to measure.

### Controls

Fly-through (default):

| Input | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Right mouse button | Toggle mouse look |
| W A S D | Move |
| Q / E | Down / up |
| Shift / Ctrl | Faster / slower |
| F | Print the camera position |
| Esc | Release the mouse; press again to quit |

`--play`:

| Input | On foot | Driving |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Click the window | Capture the mouse | Capture the mouse |
| W A S D | Move (camera relative) | W accelerates, S holds the service brake, A/D steer |
| R | Sleep when beside the bed | Switch Drive/Reverse at a stop |
| Shift | Run; held straight ahead, sprint | |
| C or Ctrl | Crouch | Look behind (C) |
| Space | Jump | Handbrake |
| F | Enter a car within reach | Get out |
| E | At a landmark's lift stop: ride up to its deck, or back down | |
| V | Place a new car ahead (a test helper) | |
| F8 | Save the physics location, nearest named street and player state | Save the location, vehicle state, load and wheel contacts |
| `[` / `]` | Step the time of day back / forward 30 minutes | Step the time of day back / forward 30 minutes |
| `\` | Sync the clock to current San Francisco local time | Sync the clock to current San Francisco local time |
| K | Cycle weather presets and the dynamic weather schedule | Cycle weather presets and the dynamic weather schedule |
| P | Photo mode (below) | Photo mode |
| Esc | Release the mouse; press again to quit | |

Interactive `--play` enables the full Metal-safe game feature set by default:
`--quality high` (with smooth real-time solar progression), grade-separated
roads and tunnels (`--structures --tunnels`), diveable coastal habitats and
reefs, the furnished 21 Beaver Street terrace house (keeping the downtown
spawn unless `--beaver-house` or a `--house-*` switch is passed), street PBR,
kit facade glass/weathering/patina/window depth, indirect light and soft
shadows, wet reflections, vehicle detail, crowds and pedestrian shading,
night photometry, foliage/nature shading, and the cinematic camera model.
Every feature still honours `--quality <tier>` and its `--no-<feature>` kill
switch; `--bench --play` stays on the default `medium` budget tier.

The title bar shows the frame rate, and in `--play` the clock time, weather,
mode, speed and the player step time; at a lift stop it starts with the
lift's prompt (in `--connect` the prompt is the HUD's).

**Photo mode** (`--play` and `--connect`, P; `eo-app/src/photo.rs`). The
player's input is frozen (in `--play` the local world holds still too; in
`--connect` the server's simulation runs on). A free camera flies or orbits
within 30 m of the player, swept as a ball against the collision world: mouse
look, W A S D fly (orbit: W S nearer and farther), Q E down and up, Shift
faster, Ctrl slower, O orbit or fly. The lens runs 14 to 200 mm (35 mm
equivalent: the field of view across the 36 mm width; the wheel), Z X roll.
The panel has three pages (Tab: Camera, Look, Frame; Up and Down choose a
setting, Left and Right change it, H hides it; it fades back while the camera
moves and docks left or right). Camera: focus (on your eyes, at an AF point,
the centre or a rule-of-thirds crossing, F, or a distance; the depth of
field's near and far limits are shown, a reticle marks where the focus is
taken and G turns focus peaking on, never saved), aperture (f/1.4 to f/22,
or off) and the iris (9 rounded blades, 7 or 6 straight blades, an ideal
circle). Look: exposure compensation (plus or minus 3 EV), a look (Neutral;
Warm film, an 81A warming filter; Cool, an 82B cooling filter; Monochrome,
Tri-X 400 through a yellow, deep yellow, orange, red or green contrast
filter) at a strength, fade, vignetting and grain. Frame: a letterbox
(2.39:1, 1.85:1, 4:3, 1:1) whose opening can be moved up or down, the
rule-of-thirds guides, a local time of day (only you see it), the capture
size and the panel's side. Enter saves a PNG at the window's size or rendered
at twice it, with its settings beside it, into the photos folder
(`EO_PHOTOS_DIR`, else `photos` in the application-data folder). The HUD is
hidden while photo mode is on; P or Esc leaves. The game's own keys (E, R,
V, F and the rest) do nothing while it is on. The depth of field and finish
are `eo-render/src/photo_mode.rs`; post's display (the filmic display or the
camera look, `--blender-camera`, `--photo-finish`, `--look cinematic`) takes
its output in place of the frame, as before, and the frame's bloom is added
in photo mode's finish instead of in post.

### Online

```sh
./target/release/eo-server --bind 127.0.0.1:47800                      # the server
./target/release/eo-app --connect 127.0.0.1:47800 --name ada           # a client (--play controls)
./target/release/eo-app --connect 127.0.0.1:47800 --name bo --spawn 1549,-366,3.14
./target/release/eo-app --connect local                                # a server inside the client process
./target/release/eo-server bots --connect 127.0.0.1:47800 --count 16   # scripted bots
```

`--net-sim RTT,JITTER,LOSS` (round trip in ms, jitter in ms, loss in
percent) impairs a client's link in both directions. `eo-server bench`,
`eo-server bandwidth` and `eo-server predict` measure the server, the
bandwidth on a busy street and client prediction. Clients and bots must
use the server's `--ground-scale` and its `--structures` / `--tunnels`;
the handshake refuses one whose world differs and says why.

### Packaged builds

`scripts/release/package.sh <label> [out-dir]` (after
`cargo build --release -p eo-app -p eo-server`) packs a playable copy into
`dist/EarthOnline-<version>-<label>.zip`: `eo-app`, `eo-server`, the
platform's launcher from `scripts/release/launchers/` (it starts `--play`),
[Docs/PLAYING.md](../Docs/PLAYING.md), and the files tracked at `HEAD` that
the game reads at run time: `public/`, `native/assets/`, each crate's
`data/`, the street-standards table and the licences. The folder keeps the
repository's layout. A packaged build finds its files as a checkout does:
the crates' tables, `native/assets/` and the model cache are under
`eo_data::native_dir()`, which is the `native/` folder the binary was built
in while that folder exists, and otherwise the `native/` folder beside the
data root found from the working directory or the executable. The cache is
`native/cache/` inside the game's folder unless `EO_NATIVE_CACHE` names
another. Optional off-repository assets are never packed.

[`.github/workflows/release.yml`](../.github/workflows/release.yml) builds
and packs Windows x64, macOS arm64 and Linux x64 for every pull request into
`main` that touches the game (the zips are the run's artifacts for a week),
and for a pushed `v*` tag publishes them as a GitHub Release:

```sh
git tag v0.1.0
git push origin v0.1.0
```

The builds are not code-signed; PLAYING.md tells players how to open them.

Presence at landmarks (`eo_net::presence`): the server owns the hour, how
fast it runs and the weather, and every snapshot carries them (a client
eases toward the server's clock, so friends see the same sunset;
`--time-of-day`, `--day-rate` and `--weather` set a local server's).
Other players are drawn as Rocketbox people in their account's body,
moved from their replicated speed and state (walk; stop with the feet
closing together; step through a turn on the spot; emotes), on a smooth
path through the snapshots, and no street person is drawn in a player's
body. The emotes are edited at the bake (`eo_agents::pose_edit`): a
one-arm wave with the forearm swinging, a point with the index finger out
and the head turned along the arm, a selfie with a phone in the hand
(`eo_agents::rig`), and a crouch. The ground under each body darkens
where the body takes light from it (`eo_agents::rig::contacts`): each
shoe, each shin and thigh and a crouch's seat is a box, and a patch on
the ground around it takes away the share of the sky the box hides (its
exact view factor) and, a few centimetres from a sole, the sun it blocks,
out of the light the renderer puts there (eo-render `agents_draw.wgsl`
`ag_contact`, in the agents' glass pass; none in a tunnel's bore, where the
bore's own light draws the body). Name tags hang over each head as it is
drawn, fade with distance (opaque to 12 m, 0.35 at 25 m, gone at 40 m),
hide behind buildings and, from a GPU depth probe of the frame, behind
anything nearer than the head (a passer-by, a pole, another player), and
step aside or up with a leader line rather than cover another tag or a
face. In the window, 1 to 4 wave, point, crouch and take a selfie, 0
stops, and T fast-travels to the next landmark viewpoint
(`assets/landmark-viewpoints.json`; the server checks the request and
lands the player there half a second later).

```sh
./target/release/eo-server bots --connect 127.0.0.1:47800 --count 12 --landmarks   # bots that walk up to the viewpoints, emote, travel on
./target/release/eo-app --connect local --net-shots scenes.json --out DIR --width 1600 --height 900 [--pair-before DIR2]   # landmark captures (eo-app/src/net.rs); DIR2: the same instants with --no-remote-bodies --no-name-tags
```

## Command-line switches

`eo-app --help` prints the main bench and streaming options. The most useful
switches are below; each mode (`--play`, `--ui`, `--connect`) rejects a
switch it does not take.

### Modes

| Switch | What |
| --- | --- |
| (none) | Windowed fly-through |
| `--start-underwater` | Start the fly camera just beneath the Bay, looking up toward the skyline |
| `--beaver-house` / `--no-beaver-house` | Opt in to the furnished three-storey terrace at 21 Beaver Street (off by default). With `--play --beaver-house`, start outside the shut front door. Press E at the handle to open or close it from either side. Walk up the stairs to the bedroom and press R beside the bed to wake at 07:00. The top floor is a workroom. The [Blender builder](../scripts/blender-buildings/build_beaver_terrace.py) accepts `-- --blend PATH --parts DIR` to save the editable scene and individual house and furniture GLBs outside the checkout. |
| `--house-finishes` / `--no-house-finishes` | With `--beaver-house`, use the Southern California courtyard design at the same Beaver Street address. It has a private path with clay-tiled steps and a planted gravel bed, a deep arched entry, a rounded stair bay, clay-tile roofs, pale photographed floorboards and locally reflected kitchen lighting. The architectural reference is the [SurveyLA Mediterranean and Indigenous Revival survey](https://planning.lacity.gov/odocument/097f6db5-feee-43f5-a448-fd140763de90/MediterraneanandIndigenousRevivalArchitecture_1893-1948.pdf). Rebuild the [floor scan atlas](../scripts/blender-buildings/prepare_beaver_floor.py), run the Blender builder with `-- --house-finishes` and the desired scene/component output paths, then run [the Blender reflection baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_reflections.py) against the exported house. |
| `--house-patina` / `--no-house-patina` | With `--beaver-house`, opt in to the furnished courtyard with scan-derived plaster, gravel, fired clay, painted timber and linen. Adds a trained bougainvillea, a wrought-iron stair rail, casement sashes, projecting sills and rainwater fittings. The front glass reflects day and night captures of the actual block. The upstairs linen quilt is settled against a mattress in Blender and exported at rest. Turning it off with `--house-finishes` keeps the previous courtyard. The [surface builder](../scripts/blender-buildings/prepare_beaver_surfaces.py) verifies its photographic inputs from an external cache. Pass `-- --house-patina` to the Blender house builder and kitchen reflection baker. The [glazing baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_glazing.py) takes an external capture directory and `--capture` to regenerate the two street panoramas. |
| `--house-furniture` / `--no-house-furniture` | Opt in to the furnished 21 Beaver house with sewn sofa cushions, a wool carpet, a six-seat dining table, a sideboard, and a wood coffee table. Furniture and its collision use physical metres inside the surveyed footprint. The indoor camera follows the standing or crouching character’s eye height in play mode. Use `--no-house-eye-camera` to retain the chase camera. An `EO_CAPTURE_POSES` entry can include `"player": {"feet": [x, y, z], "yaw": 0}` to pose the loaded game avatar as a scale reference. Reuse the previous sitting room with `--house-patina --no-house-furniture`. The [material builder](../scripts/blender-buildings/prepare_beaver_furniture.py) verifies the rug photograph and timber scans. Pass `-- --house-furniture` to the house builder and kitchen reflection baker. Run [the furniture exporter](../scripts/blender-buildings/export_beaver_furniture_parts.py) against the saved Blender file to export the fifteen loose furniture and prop GLBs and the hinge-pivoted front door with embedded materials. The closed door measures 0.96 × 2.05 m on site and opens with E from the recessed landing or hall. |
| `--house-photo` / `--no-house-photo` | Load the furnished 21 Beaver reference house with interlocking tile, the surveyed frontage tree, a 285 mm stair going, garden beds, sewn bedding and waxed boards. The camera meters the glazing on the occupied floor. For comparison with a shifted architectural lens, an `EO_CAPTURE_POSES` camera may specify `projection_shift_ndc: [x, y]`. Zero centres the optical axis. The courtyard includes the source scene’s citrus and grasses. Run the [planting exporter](../scripts/blender-buildings/export_beaver_planting.py) in Blender with the finished editable scene open to rebuild its portable meshes. The terrace, kitchen reflection, surface and floor builders accept `--house-photo`. After building the terrace and kitchen reflections, run the [glazing baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_glazing.py) with an external capture directory and `--capture --house-photo`, once for the street and again with `--rear`. The baker uses `--no-house-glazing` to exclude reflections of the probe being baked. |
| `--house-table-light` / `--no-house-table-light` | Opt in to the measured pendant on the dining table, fruit and crockery. Rebuild from the packed house with the [table light baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_table.py). A separate receiver just above the wood resolves dish shadows. The place settings carry light measurements on both sides of their surfaces. |
| `--house-oak-finish` / `--no-house-oak-finish` | Use the packed dining boards’ grain relief, scanned roughness, thin wax and measured room reflections. [Blender bake](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_oak.py). |
| `--house-oak-joinery` / `--no-house-oak-joinery` | Use the Blender cut-face material at both ends of each dining plank and the installed fixtures measured on its rounded edges. Combine with `--house-oak-finish` for the waxed tops and `--house-table-light` for the place settings. [Source bake](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_table.py). |
| `--house-front-daylight` / `--no-house-front-daylight` | Light the fitted front garden with directional sky and reflected sunlight measured in the editable house. Six positions follow the sloping bed. Daytime fields follow the city clock and blend toward a separately measured overcast response. The game still calculates direct solar shadows. This option selects the street garden. Generate solar samples with `cargo run --release -p eo-look --example site_daylight`, run the [Blender survey](../scripts/blender-buildings/survey_beaver_front_daylight.py) on the packed garden house, then run the [daylight cook](../scripts/blender-buildings/cook_beaver_front_daylight.py). |
| `--house-fired-stoop` / `--no-house-fired-stoop` | Use the individually modeled terracotta landing and stair at 21 Beaver Street. Six clay courses follow the installed stair height. The two arch piers extend to the local grade. With the fitted porch lamp, the clay receives the [measured light on the steps](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_stoop_light.py) under the current sky. [Blender build](../scripts/blender-buildings/build_beaver_stoop.py) · [Surface preparation](../scripts/blender-buildings/prepare_beaver_stoop.py) · [Runtime cooker](../scripts/blender-buildings/cook_beaver_stoop.py). Disabled by default. |
| `--house-porch-lamp` / `--no-house-porch-lamp` | Light the entrance from the fitted six-watt opal shade. The measured field includes the fixture hood, bracket and arch. Five surface charts carry diffuse light on the plaster. The swinging door casts a live shadow. With the site and Blender cameras, the approach uses the source exposure and retains the lamp color at night. Rebuild from the editable house with the [visibility survey](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_porch_light.py), [surface bake](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_porch_surfaces.py) and [atlas cooker](../scripts/blender-buildings/cook_beaver_porch_light.py). Time the approach with `--bench --segments house_porch`. |
| `--house-joinery-light` / `--no-house-joinery-light` | Use the measured diffuse light on the actual casement timber, putty, ironwork and painted entrance. The live material supplies the specular highlight. |
| `--house-window-optics` / `--no-house-window-optics` | Trace reflections from either side of the front and rear windows through the furnished Blender scene. This enables the photo house and uses its glazing. Measure the moving reflection with `--bench --segments house_windows`. |
| `--house-wall-daylight` / `--no-house-wall-daylight` | Opt into the Cycles diffuse light measured on the 21 Beaver street and yard walls, both returns, round stair bay and porch. The sun and its shadows follow the live city. Measure the approach with `--bench --segments house_frontage`. |
| `--house-site-camera` / `--no-house-site-camera` | With `--blender-camera`, use the packed scene’s daylight exposure near the house exterior. The approach and entrance blend into the city and room meters. |
| `--house-street-garden` / `--no-house-street-garden` | Fit the sloped street planter with separate agaves, deergrass, mineral mulch and weathered render. The native materials use the UV surfaces from the [editable Blender garden](../scripts/blender-buildings/build_beaver_street_garden.py). Measure the approach with `--bench --segments house_garden`. |
| `--house-window-linen` / `--no-house-window-linen` | Fit the editable five-pleat flax blind, woven lift tapes, cords and brass rings at the rear dining window. The cloth uses separate Cycles measurements for its indoor and outdoor faces under the current lamps, sky and sun. [Blender assembly](../scripts/blender-buildings/build_beaver_window_linen.py) · [Light bake and portable export](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_window_linen.py) · [Native cooker](../scripts/blender-buildings/cook_beaver_window_linen.py). |
| `--house-kitchen-light` / `--no-house-kitchen-light` | Use measured room reflections on the fitted kitchen, light from the two hood spots on the backsplash, and the authored oak on the chopping board. With `--blender-camera`, the source lens blends in at the ground-floor entrance. Bake the [kitchen lighting](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_kitchen_light.py) and [six board faces](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_kitchen_board.py) in the packed fitted scene, both with `-- PORTABLE_DIR EXTERNAL_SURVEY_DIR`. Run the [kitchen cooker](../scripts/blender-buildings/cook_beaver_kitchen_light.py) with `EXTERNAL_SURVEY_DIR/kitchen-light-survey.npz PORTABLE_DIR/fitted-room.glb`. |
| `--house-fitted-finishes` / `--no-house-fitted-finishes` | Opt into the fitted dining room with the source walnut varnish, ceramic glaze, chair and table finishes. Full-sphere Cycles measurements supply local reflections. Dedicated diffuse charts preserve the lighting on the bowl, folded linen and individual boards. The negative switch restores the fitted room’s previous material response. Rebuild in the packed fitted scene using the [surface baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_fitted_finishes.py) with `-- PORTABLE_DIR EXTERNAL_SURVEY_DIR`, then the [finish cooker](../scripts/blender-buildings/cook_beaver_fitted_finishes.py) with `SURVEY.npz PORTABLE_DIR/fitted-room.glb`. |
| `--house-fitted-dining` / `--no-house-fitted-dining` | Fit the Blender dining table, six chairs, kitchen, stair, and rear window at 21 Beaver. Uses their authored UVs and matching collision. For the calibrated daytime view, also enable the dining meter, Blender camera, downstairs light, dining light and floor reflections. From the fitted scene, run the [room baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_bedroom.py) with `-- meter --scene "Dining · site daylight" --exterior-blend ORIGINAL.blend --output-dir LIGHTS`. Install the portable meshes and measured lighting using the [fitted room cooker](../scripts/blender-buildings/cook_beaver_fitted_room.py) with `PORTABLE --lighting LIGHTS`. Opt-in. |
| `--house-floor-reflections` / `--no-house-floor-reflections` | Reproduce the ground-storey oak strips and sample the furnished room's directional reflections under the current sky, sun and installed lights. [Source bake](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_floor_gloss.py). |
| `--house-dining-meter` / `--no-house-dining-meter` | Meter daylight on the ground floor using its measured sky return and visible window area. It corrects the dome-estimate meter; with the scene meter on (the default) the frame's own reading sets the exposure, and `--no-scene-meter` selects this meter. With `--blender-camera`, the furnished source camera and the calibrated white card set the interior exposure ceiling. [Dining measurements](../public/assets/buildings/beaver-terrace/finishes/patina/furniture/photo/downstairs-daylight.provenance.json). |
| `--house-downstairs-light` / `--no-house-downstairs-light` | Opt in to the furnished ground-floor lighting survey. The ceiling, stairs and furniture share the Cycles window and fixture measurements. Rebuild with the [room baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_bedroom.py) and the `downstairs` product. Combine with `--house-dining-light` to resolve chair shadows on the oak. |
| `--house-dining-light` / `--no-house-dining-light` | Opt in to the furnished house with Cycles lighting on the ground-floor boards. The 19 W pendant casts the shadows of the installed chairs. Window light follows the city sky and sun. Bake the two pages in the packed house with the [floor light baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_dining.py), passing an external output directory, `--install` and `--reference-key R G B` from a clear-noon capture. Measure with `--bench --segments house_dining`. |
| `--house-mission-roof` / `--no-house-mission-roof` | Fit scanned, two-piece clay tiles to the gable and rounded stair bay at 21 Beaver. This opts into the photo house. Rebuild the mesh and material pages with the [Blender roof builder](../scripts/blender-buildings/build_beaver_mission_roof.py) on the staged house, passing an external output directory, the verified source-scan directory and `--runtime`. The roof timing path is `--bench --segments house_roof`. Disabled by default. |
| `--blender-camera` / `--no-blender-camera` | Display scene-linear light through the measured sRGB response of the furnished house’s Blender camera. Applies to the full view in play and captures, including water optics, in place of the filmic display and the camera look (the `--look cinematic` and `--photo-finish` passes keep their own). The existing exposure meter and night adaptation remain active. Its shader code is compiled out without it (`SF_BLENDER_CAMERA`). Calibrate with the [camera chart](../scripts/blender-buildings/calibrate_beaver_camera.py). Disabled by default. |
| `--house-blender-bedroom` / `--no-house-blender-bedroom` | Opt in to the Blender bedroom on the photo house. Its quilt uses the cutting pattern and baked wrinkle normals. The upstairs diffuse light uses six hemispheres per probe under a unit sky and four solar directions. The game keeps the sun, weather, fixtures and shadows live. Rebuild both pages with the [bedroom baker](../scripts/blender-buildings/bake_beaver_bedroom.py) on the editable scene, once with `-- quilt` and once with `-- light`. Measure the occupied room with `--bench --segments house_bedroom` and the photo house loaded. Disabled by default. |
| `--house-photo-lod` / `--no-house-photo-lod` | With the optional furnished house enabled, use a reduced mesh for material parts more than 120 m from the camera. Foliage and architectural detail retain their full geometry at the house. Disabled by default. |
| `--no-interior-lighting` | Disable the baked window and pendant light contribution in the Beaver Street house. |
| `--play` | The player instead of the free camera |
| `--test-character ID` / `--no-test-character` | Opt in to a demo avatar in `--play`, or restore the standard avatar. See [Demo characters](#demo-characters). |
| `--test-clip NAME`, `--list-test-clips` | Preview a named animation on the selected demo avatar, or print the roster and clip names. |
| `--car-door-animation` / `--no-car-door-animation` | In local play with a main demo avatar, use the paired entry and exit clips and the sedan's driver-door hinge when pressing F. |
| `--ui` | The interface shell: title, loading, HUD, pause menu |
| `--connect ADDR\|local` | Online client (with `--name`, `--spawn x,z,heading`) |
| `--bench` | Offscreen timing run over scripted camera paths; prints and writes JSON to `bench-out/` |
| `--bench --play` | Scripted walk and drive paths with the player |
| `--vehicle sedan\|hatchback\|van\|catalogue-sedan` | Vehicle body and handling for `--play` and `--connect`. The default is the sedan. |
| `--occupants-kg N`, `--cargo-kg N` | Occupant and cargo mass on the selected car, in whole kilograms. |
| `--physics-location FILE` | Reopen an F8 location in local play and restore the saved collision-world settings. |
| `--record-physics`, `--replay-physics FILE` | Record or verify each fixed-step input, weather sample, wheel contact and body position on a scripted walk or drive with `--play --bench`. |
| `--no-contact-physics` | In local play, use scripted nearby traffic colliders and disable on-foot collision with rendered pedestrians. `--contact-physics` enables dynamic traffic and on-foot contacts. |
| `--traffic-bend-braking`, `--no-traffic-bend-braking` | Preview earlier braking for mapped lane bends in the game and server. Off by default while city flow is being verified. |
| `--traffic-contact`, `--no-traffic-contact` | Opt-in preview for `eo-server` and `eo-app --connect local`. A collision moves both the player's vehicle and the fleet car. The server sends the resulting pose to every client. Off by default. |
| `--pedestrian-physics`, `--no-pedestrian-physics` | In local play, allow nearby vehicles to displace a supported pedestrian and show the resulting fall and recovery. Requires contact physics. Off by default. The `pedestrian_crossing` play bench includes a repeatable car and pedestrian contact. |
| `--capture-ui SCREEN` | Writes a PNG of one screen: `title`, `loading`, `settings`, `pause`, `map`, `hud` or `all`; also (not in `all`) `settings-hyper` (the settings panel's presets with Hyper real chosen and the "Lighting and detail" row with its note), `settings-world` (its World block), `pause-settings` (the pause sheet's graphics rows), `photo` (the photo mode panel's Camera page over a frame through its pipeline, focus peaking on) and `photo-look` (its Look page, monochrome) |
| `--ui-bench` | Interface cost over the street walk and each full-screen menu |
| `--audio-render all --out DIR` | Offline sound mixes; no GPU, no audio device |

### World and view

| Switch | What |
| --- | --- |
| `--time-of-day H` | Hour of the day, 0 to 24 (default 12) |
| `--weather KIND` | `clear`, `overcast`, `fog`, `drizzle`, `light-rain` (`little-rain`), `sun-rain` (`sunshower`), `rain` (`medium-rain`), `heavy-rain` (`big-rain`, `downpour`), `storm`, `damp` (a street an hour after rain: the film and puddles, drying patches, no rain, a clearing deck), or `schedule` for the changing weather |
| `--weather-seed N`, `--world-time S` | Seed and clock of the weather schedule |
| `--moon-day D` | Day of the lunar cycle |
| `--sky-quality low\|medium\|high` | Cloud and atmosphere quality |
| `--cloud-depth-haze` | A low-deck cloud seen over the ground (below the horizon from inside or above the deck) takes the aerial perspective of its own depth and hides the haze behind it, instead of the haze of the whole path to the ground in front of it (a blue veil cut at the horizon). Not under a weather veil (fog, drizzle, rain, storm), where those frames stay as they were. Off by default until reviewed under the visual gate; `--no-cloud-depth-haze` restores the frame |
| `--no-volumetric-clouds` | Disable the default deep volumetric cloudscape (`eo-sky` `clouds::volumetric_decks`, `eo-render` `clouds.rs`, `sky_clouds.wgsl`, `sky_volume.wgsl`, `sky_recon.wgsl`, `sky_resolve.wgsl`, `water.wgsl`, `props_draw.wgsl`: 64^3 3D curl-advected detail volume, 512^2 meteorological weather and fibrous-cirrus map, 1.62–3.55 km fair-weather cumulus / up to 6.2 km storm volumetric raymarch out to 135 km over the curved Earth, multi-octave chromatic scattering, crepuscular sunbeams, volumetric cloud shadows, physical sun disc + corona, mid-level altocumulus, high-altitude jet-stream cirrus, and hemispherical water reflection dome) and restore the previous cloud deck (`--volumetric-clouds` re-enables) |
| `--no-vol-sky-state` | The volumetric cloudscape's cumulus cover takes the day's sky state as the low deck does (eo-sky `clouds::volumetric_decks`, `sky_state_kept`, `volumetric_cover`; eo-look `features::daylight().vol_sky_state`; on by default): `volumetric_decks` set the cumulus cover by the weather alone (clear 0.40, at 0.92 opacity, its ground shadow's optical depth x 1.95), over the day's drawn sky (`clouds::sky_state`: cloudless, cirrus or fair cumulus), so a clear cirrus noon (Mission and 3rd Streets, 1 August, play mode) drew cumulus over most of downtown: the sunlit street took about a tenth of the sun and read 0.15 of the blue sky's luminance (the atmosphere model's own 1.6). Now the cover is the weather's x the share of the low deck the day's sky leaves (`decks` over `raw_decks`): none on a cloudless or cirrus day, the whole on a fair-cumulus day, as before under every other weather. `--no-vol-sky-state` restores the weather's cover |
| `--no-measured-aerosol` | The clear day's air holds San Francisco's measured aerosol (eo-sky `frame::daylight_density`, `measured_aerosol_scale`, `CLEAR_DAY_AOD_550`; eo-look `features::daylight().measured_aerosol`; on by default): the physical daylight and the atmosphere tables integrated the clean coastal medium (eo-look `atmosphere::COASTAL_AOD_550` 0.04 x the clear preset's 1 + 2 haze: an aerosol optical depth of 0.048 at 550 nm, a clean maritime day's), half the column AERONET's sun photometers measured on the day of the 2011 bluff footage (0.087, interpolated over UCSB 0.061, Fresno 0.083 and Trinidad Head 0.116; INFERRED as the clear day's: over the medium's 1 km scale height it puts 0.087 / km at the ground against the 0.068 / km eo-sky `photo` takes from IMPROVE's Point Reyes visual ranges, a visibility of 39 km). With it every weather's aerosol is x 1.81 (the clear day's 0.087, each weather's haze scaling it as before): under a noon sun 65 deg up the sky's light on a horizontal surface rises from 0.11 to 0.16 of the sun's and whitens (blue/red facing up 1.93 to 1.65), the beam loses 3 %, and the far city takes the haze it sees. CPU and the tables' inputs only: no shader change. `--no-measured-aerosol` restores the clean medium |
| `--no-weather-fx` | Disable the default physical rain and wind surface details plus weather physics (`eo-sky` `weather.rs`, `eo-player` `weather.rs`, `eo-render` `water.wgsl`, `sky_light.wgsl`, `look_light.wgsl`, `sky_fx.wgsl`, `sky_volume.wgsl`, `props.rs`, `props_types.wgsl`, `props_cull.wgsl`, `props_draw.wgsl`, `props_shadow.wgsl`, `light_shadow_props.wgsl`, `hlod_trees.wgsl`: intensity-varied and spatially varying three-layer raindrop impact capillary rings, view-parallaxed splash crowns and rebounding droplet sparkles on ocean/bay water and wet floors; accumulated standing-water puddles with dark soaked rims and wind ripples across gutters, carriageways, sidewalks and flat horizontal ground; gust-driven falling rain streaks with sunlit sunshower sparkle and a physical antisolar rainbow; weather-coupled wind sway, per-leaf wiggle, raindrop leaf twitch and glossy wet foliage on trees, palms, shadows and distant hill trees; and wet-pavement tire grip, standing-puddle hydroplaning, puddle splash drag and aerodynamic wind forces in `eo-player`) and restore the previous output (`--weather-fx` re-enables) |
| `--agent-density N` | Multiply the traffic and pedestrian population (1 to 64) |
| `--crowd` | The street's population by place and hour, and a camera cut that arrives at a street already lived in (eo-sim `CrowdDensity`, consts.rs's crowd density model, region.rs `density_bubble`; eo-app agents.rs; off by default, so the frame is as before; no tier sets it). The people: each footway holds people at density = flow / walking speed, the flow from its street class, the storeys of the buildings fronting it (x the class), its share of walk-in shops and of bars, restaurants, clubs and theatres (the frontage survey: every building edge of the city file with its storeys and its OSM use, measured along each footway's outer side, eo-sim `NativeSource::with_frontage`), each at the day's footfall or the evening economy's (to the 02:00 last call), plus the walks that are places of their own (the city file's pedestrian, footway and path ways that are not a street's sidewalk or crossing: plazas, promenades, park paths; `with_paths`), within 150 m of the focus, at most 400 bodies, refilled off shot beyond 18 m or in shot beyond 90 m; in place of the People setting's fixed 40 bodies in 55 m. At a nightlife front in its hours many walks end at the doors (eo-sim pedestrians.rs: a group stands talking, facing each other, one alone stands or takes a call with their back to the front, looking out at the street, 30-150 s). The simulation's hour is the drawn hour (without the switch it starts at noon), a capture card's too when `--car-traffic` restarts the simulation at each card (it restarted at the run's start hour). A cut (the focus jumping more than 150 m or the drawn hour more than 15 minutes between two frames: a teleport, a capture card, a time change) rebuilds the simulation round the new focus at the new hour from the network source already loaded, off the render thread, and runs 600 fixed steps (walkers mid-walk, cars in flow and queued at the lights) before it replaces the running one, so the street fills in one step, settled, instead of ramping in from the disc's edge; the captures wait for a pending cut (props.rs `sync_parked`; before it the cards drew the simulation of the bench's first path, kilometres away, and showed no one). Each cut logs the disc's footways, mean activity and expected people (`crowd:`), and the light cards' JSON carries a `crowd` census. Where the walks are: each street's footways stand on its drawn walks (eo-street's plan, eo-sim `NativeSource::with_sections`: half a throughway in from the walk's frontage zone, the walk's middle on a narrow one) and stop and cross at the drawn kerbs, so walkers walk where the walks are drawn rather than on the class model's line (which lies on the wider drawn carriageway); a walk of its own is cut where it runs over a drawn carriageway; an unsignalled street is crossed only where a crosswalk is mapped (the city file's `crossingWays`, within two crosswalk widths of the crossing band; `with_crossings`; a signal's crossings always); the paved plazas the landmarks lay (sf-lots-parks) are crossed by straight walks between points of their outline and walked round (eo-sim `plaza_walks`: about one walk to every 18 m of the plaza's mean width). The bodies (eo-agents feed, `Feeder::separate`): each a 0.27 m disc (Fruin's body ellipse), overlapping pairs pushed apart (position-based, four passes a frame) and anyone inside a vehicle's footprint put beside it, so no two people stand in each other or in a bus; a walker first drawn takes the first of its keyed bodies no one within 30 m wears (no twins in sight). A walker over the landmarks' walkable paving (every `Structure::footway` the landmark set lays: the quay's apron, the pier decks, the plazas' paving; their triangles facing up, eo-agents `feed::Decks`) stands on its top rather than on the pressed ground under it (the Embarcadero's apron stands up to a metre over it: walkers were drawn sunk to the knees). The lanes, the stop bars and the cars are as before; `--no-crowd` |
| `--people-bounce` | The ground's light on the people (eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `pb_person`, compiled in by `SF_PEOPLE_BOUNCE`; off by default and compiled out, so the frame is as before; no tier sets it). The rig lights every surface's lower half with one ground colour (its ground lobe, and with `--ground-bounce` the sunlit open ground's exitance), nothing from the street lamps; a person stands over the very ground the camera sees round their feet, so its radiance L is read there instead: the previous frame's scene radiance (the frame SSR reads) at six points on a 0.9 m ring round the feet, each kept only where the previous frame's depth shows that ground (not a body or car in front of it), and the rig's ground term is replaced by a lower half-space of that radiance (irradiance pi L (1 - n.y) / 2, the view factor of a plane below), weighted by the share of the ring found, under the surface's occlusion and Fresnel. A lamp's pool lights a person from below at night (no more black cut-outs on a lit walk), a sunlit street warms an underside by day, a shaded alley's paving gives little; `--no-people-bounce` |
| `--people-capsules` | The ground round each person darkened by their whole body, for the sky and for each lamp (eo-render people_capsules.wgsl, in people_contact.wgsl's place and pipeline, drawn after the agents' opaque list; eo-agents `people::add_capsule_anchors` and `fit_capsule` at load, never cached; off by default: nothing compiled, made or drawn, and `--people-contact` draws its spheres as before; no tier sets it). Each person is thirteen capsules riding their skeleton (the shoes heel to toe, the shins, the thighs, the hips, the chest, the head, the upper arms, the forearms with the hands), each fitted to the near tier's vertices its joints carry in the bind pose (axis: their principal axis; radius: their RMS distance from it; ends: their extent along it, drawn in by the radius), posed by the joint matrices the body is skinned with and packed to the quad's flat outputs (1-2 mm). On a quad round the feet within 90 m (1.3 m half side by day; 3.2 m with the lamps lit, faded over its last 0.8 m) each ground point loses (1) the share of the cosine-weighted sky each capsule covers, in closed form (a cylinder of radius r seen as a strip 2 r wide, integrated exactly along its axis: (r / pi) [h (tau / (c (tau^2 + c^2)) + atan(tau / c) / c^2) - u_y c / (tau^2 + c^2)] between its ends lengthened by pi r / 4 for the caps; within 10 % of a brute-force hemisphere integral, eo-render test `the_capsules_sky_share_is_the_hemisphere_integral`): a standing body hides about 15 % of the sky 0.3 m from its axis, 9 % at 0.5 m and nearly all of it under a shoe, a tight dark line at the soles and a soft halo half a metre round, in shade and at night as in the sun; and (2) of each street lamp and headlamp of its light cluster (shaped as sky_light.wgsl lights the ground, at its own map's visibility), the share of the lamp's emitting disc (the cinematic look's source sizes: 0.3 m for a street lamp, 0.09 m for a headlamp, half its glazed side for a shop window) the capsules cover, two discs overlapping in the plane across the ray where each capsule passes nearest it: a person's own shadow from the lamps at night, sharp at the shoes, softening toward the head's (a headlamp whose map already holds the people, `--headlight-shadows`, is left to it). The frame there is multiplied by 1 - (light lost) / (light that reached it), as the ground's diffuse irradiance (the ambient, the key at its shadow and clouds, each lamp), under the fog. Each fragment of the quad takes the ground point it shows, where the view ray through it meets the ground's plane under the person, not the quad's own point 3 cm over it (which lay nearer the eye than the ground seen through it by 3 cm x the distance over the eye's height, 18 cm at 10 m: the line at the soles was drawn behind them); `--no-people-capsules` |
| `--people-contact` | The ground round each person occluded by their body (eo-render people_contact.wgsl, its own pipeline drawn after the agents' opaque list; eo-agents `people::add_contact_anchors` at load, never cached; off by default: nothing compiled, made or drawn, the library's bone table and model pads as before; no tier sets it). Each person is nine spheres riding their skeleton: each shoe's heel and toe halves, the shins, the thighs and the pelvis, each the centroid and RMS radius of the near tier's vertices those joints carry in the bind pose, posed by the joint matrices the body is skinned with (the walk and standing frames as blended there). A ground point's sky visibility is the product over them of 1 - the sphere's cosine-weighted solid angle ((d.n / d) (r / d)^2; 1 inside it), and that occlusion takes away the ambient share of the ground's light only: the sky, the hemisphere and the environment over them plus the shadowed key and the street lights at that point (which carry their own shadows). A quad 2.4 m across on the ground under each person within 90 m multiplies the lit frame by 1 - (1 - visibility) x that share x (1 - fog), depth-tested, not written: a tight dark line where the shoes meet the paving and a faint pool under the legs, in shade and at night as in the sun; `--no-people-contact` |
| `--people-crosswalks` | With `--crowd`, the walkers cross on the zebras the street draws (eo-sim `NativeSource::with_zebras`, network.rs `FootEnd::zebra_only`, pedestrians.rs `consider_crossing`; eo-app agents.rs `drawn_zebras`; off by default: the crossings as before; no tier sets it). A footway's crossing band at a street end was the class model's (its kerb trim plus half a crosswalk), which can lie metres inside the drawn junction from the painted crossing (Bush at Montgomery: a walker crossing Montgomery 4 m into the junction, in front of the stop line, read as walking down the middle of the road). Now each band moves to the drawn zebra across that street at that end (eo-street's plan: at a junction that earns paint, each approach whose crossing is painted and fits its run, the middle of its band, the approach's trim plus the crosswalk clearance plus half the band's depth from the node; taken within two crosswalk widths of the class band, ahead of the node and within half a crosswalk of the centreline), and a street end with no zebra drawn is not crossed, at a signal too. And a corner leg keeps to its corner (pedestrians.rs `same_corner`): the JS joins every footway end at a junction's node, so a walker reaching a corner could step off it across the road to another corner, with no crosswalk or signal (round SoMa 28 of 43 corner legs walked in four minutes crossed a street with walks; with the switch none); now it takes only a footway whose kerb point no street leg of the node separates from its own, and crosses streets only on the zebras; `--no-people-crosswalks` |
| `--people-evening` | With `--crowd`, a street of shops keeps its walkers into the evening (eo-sim consts.rs `shop_footfall`, `footway_density_with`, `CrowdDensity::evening`; eo-app agents.rs `region_config`; off by default: the density as before; no tier sets it). The crowd's walk-in shops' share of a footway's flow followed the offices' day (`hour_footfall`: 0.46 of noon at 21:00, 0.32 at 22:00), so a commercial street at night held a third of its noon walkers. San Francisco Planning's public life study of 9th Avenue and Irving and of Upper Market and Castro (2008: ten-minute counts in each hour, times six, weekdays 8:00-23:00) found a neighbourhood commercial street's evening at about its day: at 9th and Irving 200-850 people an hour a block, the three blocks' 20:00-23:00 counts 0.8-1.0 of their 10:00-17:00 mean; Market Street's weekday peak at 20:00-21:00 and Castro Street's counts rising again between 21:00 and 23:00. With the switch the shops' share follows that day (1 from 9:00 to 17:00, 0.85 at 18:00, 0.8 from 19:00 to 22:00, the counts smoothed at their low side; past 23:00, where the counts end, falling to the small hours, INFERRED); the homes' and jobs' share, the evening economy's and the places' are as before. A walk fronted by shops along 0.8 of it holds 0.72 of its noon walkers at 21:00 (0.46 before) and 0.69 at 22:00 (0.32); `--no-people-evening` |
| `--people-groups` | With `--crowd`, social groups keep together (eo-sim `RegionConfig::groups`, pedestrians.rs `Party` and `Crowd::keep_parties`, region.rs `spawn_group`; eo-agents feed `keep_personal_space_with`; off by default: the walkers as before; no tier sets it). Without it a group's two or three members set out together (0.38 m apart across the walk) and part at the first pause, each on its own schedule, and from the front or behind a diagonal file of three reads as one body. With it the group's first member keeps the schedule and the others walk, wait, cross and stop with it, each at its own place about the leader's point on the walk (Moussaid, Perozo, Garnier, Helbing and Theraulaz 2010: a pair walks side by side, three in a V with the middle one 0.35 m behind): walking abreast 0.75 m between neighbours' centres, waiting at a kerb side by side along it behind the leader (none past it into the junction) or at a window side by side along it, stopped to talk on a circle facing its middle 1.0 m between neighbours (Kendon's F-formation, at Hall's personal distance), each place reached at a side step's 0.6 m/s and each heading at a walker's turning rate; each keeps its own gait phase and stride. The feed keeps strangers to their personal room but a group's members to their formation (the output's `FLAG_PARTY` bits). A window-shopping stop's weight is also taken by the share of the walk's frontage that is walk-in shops, so nobody stops to look into a blank wall; `--no-people-groups` |
| `--people-idles` | The people's standing repertoire (eo-agents motion.rs `MORE_IDLES`, `roles`; library.rs `CookOptions::more_idles`; off by default: the bake, the agent library's cache key and the feed as before; no tier sets it). `ROLES`' three neutral idles and two waits all stand with the arms at the sides (elbows bent about 20 degrees, the hands at the hip), which read as a bind pose at a few metres. Ten more windows of the Rocketbox takes, each measured across its take (elbow bend, hand height, the pelvis's sway) and baked after `ROLES`' loops in slot 1: hands on the hips, hands resting at the front or the pockets, the forearms raised in front, looking about, a long stand with its weight shifts (the pelvis swaying 8-18 cm), a hand to the face, a hand to the head; waiting with the hands in front, looking about or fidgeting. A standing walker draws its loop from its family's (ten idles, five waits). Nothing is held in a hand: the takes with a bag, a drink, a paper, an umbrella, headphones or a camera are left out. The first run with the switch cooks the people again (its own cache entry, about 5 MB more joint tables); `--no-people-idles` |
| `--people-lamps` | The lamps' skin, cloth and hair on the people (eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `pl_person`, compiled in by `SF_PEOPLE_LAMPS`; off by default and compiled out, so the frame is as before; no tier sets it). The rig lights a person from each street lamp, headlamp and lit shop window with the city's two lobes only (Lambert and the GGX at the texel's roughness); what `--people-shading` adds for the key (the skin's terminator blurred over its mean free paths and its oily second lobe, the cloth's Charlie sheen, the hair's two Kajiya-Kay strand highlights; the class read back from the atlas's roughness) is added here for every light of the fragment's light cluster, at its irradiance there (each luminaire's shape and reach as sky_light.wgsl lights the city: the cobra head's batwing, the low beam's cut-off, a shop window's lobe; `pl_shape`, which `--people-capsules` shares) and, for a lamp that keeps a shadow map (`--light-shadows`), at its visibility. At night it is what outlines a person against the dark: the sheen along a jacket's shoulders and sleeves and the glint in the hair from a lamp behind or beside them, the warm band across a cheek turned from a shop window; nothing by day (the lamps are off); `--no-people-lamps` |
| `--people-motion` | The people's bearing (eo-agents rocketbox.rs `steady_head`, library.rs `CookOptions::steady_heads`, motion.rs `MotionMeta::steady`; off by default: the bake, the agent library's cache key and the feed as before; no tier sets it). Steady heads: in walking the head is held steady in pitch, its swing within about 5 degrees of the walk's mean, the gaze a little below the horizon (Pozzo, Berthoz and Lefort 1990), but several walk styles and clips carry the head bowed far past that (100STYLE's Depressed about 50 degrees past the Neutral take, Followed's look back about 55, Rushed and CrowdAvoidance about 23, the texting clip about 38), which read as people staring at their feet. In the bake each loop's mean nod below the level line the bind pose looks along is held softly within 10 degrees down and 6 up (identity to 4 and 3, compressed past them), each frame keeps its own swing about that mean and is held softly within 18 down and 10 up, the neck taking 40 % of the correction and the head the rest about the head's lateral axis (the face joints follow; nothing below the neck moves; a call's hand stays at the ear). The texting loop's walkers take a call instead (no phone is drawn in a hand). The first run with the switch cooks the people again (its own cache entry); `--no-people-motion` |
| `--people-near` | (captures, with `--car-traffic`) A warmed card hides the simulated people only within 4 m of its eye (`PEOPLE_NEAR_CLEAR_M`, plus the person's bounding radius), not the card's 8 m (`WARM_CLEAR_M`, which still holds for the vehicles): a crew holds traffic out of a shot, not the people on the walk round the camera, and a person nearer than 4 m would fill a street lens's frame (eo-app agents.rs `pump`; off by default: the cards as before; no tier sets it); `--no-people-near` |
| `--people-space` | With `--crowd`, strangers keep their personal room (eo-agents feed `keep_personal_space`, before `separate`; off by default: the bodies kept apart as before; no tier sets it). The bodies alone (0.27 m discs) let two strangers walk or wait shoulder against shoulder, 0.54 m apart, and a kerb's waiting crowd overlap at the arms; each walker now first keeps Fruin's no-touch zone (Pedestrian Planning and Design, 1971: a waiting pedestrian keeps a circle of 0.3 to 0.45 m radius free of others where the space allows), its outer 0.45 m radius, every overlapping pair pushed apart by half the overlap each in eight Gauss-Seidel passes a frame: strangers stand and pass 0.9 m apart centre to centre, about half a metre between shoulders, and a dense spot settles between that and the bodies' own breadth; `--no-people-space` |
| `--people-shading` | The people's skin, cloth and hair (eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `pp_person`, compiled in by `SF_PEOPLE_SHADING`; off by default and compiled out, so the frame is as before; no tier sets it). The Rocketbox atlases' class roughness (eo-agents rocketbox.rs `ROUGH_*`) is read back per texel. Skin: the key light's terminator blurred per channel over the angle skin's diffuse mean free paths (Jensen et al. 2001: 3.67 / 1.37 / 0.68 mm) span on a 15 mm feature (the clamped cosine's ramp convolved with that Gaussian: a soft band, red widest, either side of the terminator, its visibility looked up past it by look_light's one shadow lookup, `lk_vis_wrap`), and a second, sharper specular lobe (roughness 0.3, F0 0.028) for the oily film. Cloth: a Charlie sheen (roughness 0.7, half the fabric's own sqrt(albedo)) from the key and rim. Hair: Kajiya-Kay strand highlights on two shifted lobes along the head's fall line. Added after `look_light` at the key's visibility and under the fog it found (look_light.wgsl `lk_key_vis`, `lk_fog_out`); `--no-people-shading` |
| `--people-stagger` | With `--crowd` and `--people-groups`, a walking group's line is turned from square across the walk by its own angle (eo-sim consts.rs `GROUP_STAGGER_RAD`, pedestrians.rs `keep_parties`; off by default: the groups as before; no tier sets it). Walking abreast, a pair or the front of a V stood exactly level (0.75 m apart square across the walk), so two companions seen from the side were one silhouette with one shoulder line. Companions walk side by side but rarely level (Moussaid et al. 2010 give the abreast line as the low-density mean, the members' places spread about it): each group's line turns by an angle within 25 degrees either way, keyed by its leader and held while it walks, the V's middle still 0.35 m back; a pair stays 0.75 m apart and walks up to 0.32 m out of level, so from the side each keeps its own shoulder line. A model choice within those observations, not a measured constant (eo-sim tests/crowd.rs `walking_pairs_walk_a_shoulder_apart`); `--no-people-stagger` |
| `--people-windows` | With `--crowd`, a window-shopping stop only in front of a shop's window (eo-sim `FootActivity::shop_fronts`, `NativeSource::with_frontage`'s survey, pedestrians.rs `advance_activity`; eo-app agents.rs; off by default: the stops as before; no tier sets it). The pause table's browsing stop (stand facing the front, 4-13 s) was drawn anywhere on a walk (with `--people-groups` weighted by the share of the walk fronted by shops), so on a walk half shops and half houses people stood with their faces to a house's front or a blank wall. The frontage survey now also keeps which stretches of each walk a walk-in shop fronts (64 equal stretches, each the use of the front found at its middle) and a walker stops to look in only where its own stretch is a shop's; elsewhere the stop is one of the others (waiting at a corner, talking, a call, standing, carrying). Round SoMa with fronts alternating shop and house every 12 m, 948 of 1,933 browsing samples stood at a house before, none with the switch (eo-sim tests/crowd.rs `window_shoppers_stand_at_shop_windows`); `--no-people-windows` |
| `--width W --height H` | Frame size (default 1920 x 1080) |
| `--msaa 1\|4` | Multisampling |
| `--no-vsync` | Present without vertical sync |
| `--far M` | Camera far plane in metres (default 4200) |
| `--shadow-map N` | Sun shadow map size (default 3072) |
| `--quality low\|medium\|high\|hyper` | Quality tier (default `medium`, the street look: `--grime 1`, `--stand-ins`, `--surveyed-furniture`, `--tree-basins`); see [Quality tiers](#quality-tiers). A switch the tier sets, given anywhere on the line, wins over the tier |
| `--light-shadows [N]`, `--light-shadow-moving`, `--headlight-shadows [N]` | Shadows of the street lamps and headlights (eo-render `light_shadow.rs`, `light_shadow*.wgsl`; all off by default; a level left out is the `high` tier's). `--light-shadows N` (lamps, rounded up to a multiple of 16, at most 48): the N street lamps nearest the eye each keep a 512 px depth map, straight down from the luminaire over 72 deg from the nadir, drawn once (at most 2 new maps a frame, nearest first) from the city's objects at the level drawn, the props and the parked cars, and kept while the lamp stays selected; `--light-shadow-moving` draws the 4 nearest again every frame with every car and person; `--headlight-shadows N` (at most 8) gives the N nearest vehicles with their low beams lit a map each every frame, over both beams, from everything but the vehicle itself. One depth atlas (16 + 2: 2560 x 2048, 12.1 MB; 48 + 4 + 8: 4096 x 4096, 35.1 MB) at the look's group 1 binding 17, its table at 18; the lights' order and every light cluster's list become stable (nearest first, the same frame after frame and run after run); each lit surface's lamp light is multiplied by its occlusion and its map (four bilinear compares); the lit fog (`--lit-fog`) reads the maps too; `--light-shadows` also draws the street lamps in SF's 3000 K LED colour as a night-adapted eye sees it (eo-sky `lights::led_street_lamp_color`; 0xffc46a otherwise). Selected, culled and drawn on the GPU (no read-back), timed in the lighting set's `light_shadows` and `headlight_shadows` slots; nothing runs by day. `--no-light-shadows`, `--no-light-shadow-moving`, `--no-headlight-shadows` restore the frame |
| `--lit-fog [1\|2]` | Fog lit by the street lamps and headlamps (eo-render `lit_fog.rs`, `lit_fog.wgsl`; eo-sky `lit_fog`): a froxel volume on the light clusters' frustum (1: 160 x 90 columns x 64 log slices over the clusters' 0.5-300 m, 22.1 MB; 2: 240 x 135 x 96, 74.6 MB), filled between the sky's volume and its resolve with the volume's own fog at each froxel (the marine layer, the weather's veil, a continuous low deck, the haze, rain) and the light each clustered lamp sends toward the eye through the droplets' forward lobe (Henyey-Greenstein g 0.9, widening with the optical depth of the light's path; street luminaires with their tail out to the horizontal, so a lamp seen down the street is ringed by its glow), temporally blended at a low history weight with the history held near the new value (a moving beam leaves no trail), integrated front to back and added by the resolve at each pixel's depth; a light with a shadow map (`--light-shadows`, `--headlight-shadows`) lights only the fog it reaches, and the luminaires' own air light is not drawn where the volume draws it. Nothing by day or with no lamp lit (the passes skip, the slot is an empty span). Timed in the lighting set's `lit_fog` slot; the `high` tier sets 1 and `hyper` 2; `--no-lit-fog` restores the frame |
| `--cascade-blend` | The sun cascades hand over to the next over the last fifth of a cascade's slice (a smoothstep to the split) instead of switching at the split. The city, agents, landmark wires, CC0 decals and props take it; the far layer (past the cascades) has nothing to blend; the water and the shafts keep the switch (no visible change there) |
| `--far-shadows [1\|2]` | Sun shadows past the cascades' 160 m from a cached depth map round the camera: 1 one ring (2 km across round a street-level eye, growing with the eye's height to about 6.7 times it, up to the far plane: the far plane's 4.2 km from 300 m up), 2 a second ring out to the far plane (4096 px, Depth16, two layers a ring: 64 MB a ring). A ring is redrawn a quarter (one scissored tile) a frame, only after the key light has turned 0.05 degrees or the camera has moved an eighth of the ring from its fit; casters are each building's footprint prism or, while the far layer holds it, its cell's mesh, the terrain as drawn, the structures' coarse concrete and the landmarks' far items. Sampled in the cascades' fade (120-160 m) and past it by the city, agents, landmark wires, CC0, the far cells, far trees and lights, and the sea; each ring fades out with the ground distance from the eye. Props keep the cascades alone (Apple's shader compiler); the shafts end at 160 m as before |
| `--sun-bounce` | The sun's first bounce off the sunlit walls round each point (eo-render `surfaces_ao_sun.wgsl`, eo-look `sun_bounce`; off by default and in no tier; needs `--far-shadows` and the occlusion pass): the occlusion pass gathers, at half resolution, 24 texels of the far sun map's ring 1 on a 32 m disc round each point in light space as a reflective shadow map (Dachsbacher and Stamminger, I3D 2005): every texel is the first surface the sun meets in its beam and takes the key's irradiance over the texel's cross-section. A sunlit wall there (normals more than 45 degrees from up; the ground and roofs stay the rig's ground lobe's and the ground bounce's) adds its radiance (albedo 0.28, warm: weathered concrete, stucco and painted brick) less the rig's stand-in for those directions (the ground lobe, which with the ambient volume also lights the buildings that hide the sky), with the two cosines and the inverse square to the point; nearer than the screen-space bounce's 4 m only the share that pass leaves. The sum lands in the bounce channel every lit surface reads the next frame (city, structures, agents, props, landmark wires, the far layer): a facade across from a sunlit one, a canyon's lower floors under sunlit upper ones, a column beside a sunlit wall take the sunlit walls' light in the key's colour. No visibility between the point and the wall (a wall behind a building faces the point across a block at least). Its shader code is compiled out without it (`SF_SUN_BOUNCE`); timed in the occlusion pass's slot; `--no-sun-bounce` restores the frame |
| `--sun-bounce-reach` | With `--sun-bounce` (on by default; play turns the sun bounce on): its disc out to 64 m in light space instead of 32 m, the same samples (eo-look `sun_bounce::REACH_M`; a run bit, look.ao_params.w bit 4). A wall across the street from a point lies D sin(alt) + h cos(alt) from it in light space (D the street's width between the fronts, h the height up the wall): under a 65 degree noon sun over a 25 m street (Mission, Market, Van Ness and the South of Market's arterials have 25 to 38 m between their fronts) the far front starts 23 m out, so the 32 m disc reached only its lowest 22 m, with few samples; by the CPU reference it took 0.28 of that front's light at a 40 m front, the 64 m disc 0.93. At Mission and 3rd at noon (play look, camera) the shaded street's light rose 23% and its blue/red fell from 1.78 to 1.44 (128 m: 4% more); the residential streets (low fronts, narrow streets) are unchanged. `--no-sun-bounce-reach` restores the 32 m disc |
| `--ao-albedo` | The occlusion's multi-bounce at each surface's own diffuse albedo, per channel, on the indirect diffuse (eo-render `surfaces_ao_use.wgsl` `ao_multi_bounce_albedo`; off by default and in no tier): Jimenez, Wu, Pesce and Jarabo's fit (2016, eq. 10) takes the light the occluded part of the hemisphere passes on at the surface's albedo, its surroundings assumed alike; without it every surface takes the JS's 0.7. The city's surfaces are 0.1-0.4, so a corner, a recess, a kerb's foot or the ground under a car keeps less of the sky's light (a visibility of 0.3 keeps 0.37 at an albedo of 0.25 instead of 0.57) and a coloured corner deepens in its own colour. The specular occlusion and the street lights keep the single value. Its shader code is compiled out without it (`SF_AO_ALBEDO`); `--no-ao-albedo` restores the frame |
| `--soft-shadows` | The sun's penumbra (eo-render `look_light.wgsl` `lk_cascade_soft`, eo-look `penumbra`; off by default and in no tier): the sun is a disc 0.5334 degrees across, so a shadow's edge is a band 0.0093 x its distance from the casting edge along the beam (sharp at a contact, 9 cm at 10 m, 0.8 m where a deck 10 m up throws its edge 87 m along a sun 6.6 degrees high). In each sun cascade the casters in a disc of 0.3 m round the point (12 taps) give that distance: each caster's height over the receiver's plane at its own texel, the nearest one past 0.2 m (the map holds a caster's sun-facing surface, so the mean over a thick deck or block overstated the distance, differently either side of the edge, and bent the band); a band is at most 0.6 m wide (an edge up to 64 m up the beam); the comparison is then filtered over a disc of the penumbra's half width (16 bilinear taps, never wider than the search, at most 48 texels; a uniform disc, so a straight edge's band is the sun's disc past a chord), and only there (both discs constant Vogel tables, eo-look `penumbra::vogel_disc`, turned by one rotation a pixel and frame; in the cascade blend's band one cascade is looked up a pixel, the next where the pixel's noise falls under the blend's weight, so the search and filter run at most once): no caster in the search is lit, casters at every tap the umbra, every tap against the receiver's own plane (its geometric normal solved in the cascade's clip space). Percentage-closer soft shadows (Fernando 2005) at the sun's physical size; under a texel of penumbra the cascade's 3 x 3 tent stands, and where the widest penumbra (0.3 m each way) is no wider than the cascade's texel or half a pixel at the point's distance the search is skipped and the tent drawn (eo-look `penumbra::resolves`: from the city_overview path's 18 km every pixel). The far map past 160 m is unchanged; the props' pipelines keep the tent (their cut-out shader does not compile on Apple's GPU with the search; `LK_SHADOW_SOFT`). The cars and people take the penumbra too, looked up once at the top of their fragment (`LK_SUN_PRE`, agents_draw.wgsl `ag_shade`): inside look_light's key branch, with `--car-materials`, Apple's compiler aborted their opaque pipeline (an LLVM fatal error in MTLCompilerService's instruction emission). Its shader code is compiled out without it (`SF_SOFT_SHADOWS`); `--no-soft-shadows` restores the frame |
| `--screen-gi` | The screen-space indirect light (eo-render `surfaces_ao_gi.wgsl`, `surfaces_ao_gi_filter.wgsl`, eo-look `screen_gi`; off by default and in no tier; needs the occlusion pass): the half-resolution occlusion pass's horizon search becomes a visibility-bitmask gather (Therrien, Levesque and Gilet 2023) out to 12 m (at most half the frame's height on screen): one slice of 12 samples a side per pixel and frame (turned per pixel and frame; the history's mean over about 19 frames takes as many slices, each frame's slice weighted against the mean of its own and the orthogonal slice's weight, so the frames' mean is the two-slice weighted mean), each slice's hemisphere 32 sectors of equal cosine-weighted share, every sample a surface 0.75 m + 4 % of its eye distance deep (at least 0.3 x its distance from the point), hiding the sectors between its front and back, the sectors it hides first taking its lit colour, at most a white (0.8) diffuse surface's radiance under the whole rig by day (a glint is not a bounce). A thin rail or pole hides its own sliver, not the wall and sky behind it. The result (visibility through the occlusion's 0.9, the bounce) is blended into a history reprojected with the camera it was made with (weight 0.1 a frame, refused where its depth is not the surface's) and filtered over 5 x 5 texels by distance from the point's plane into the texture the lit shader reads (unchanged: it trades the rig's light over the hidden share for the bounce). The sun bounce (`--sun-bounce`) leaves the gather's 12 m to it, and with the history takes 12 texels a frame instead of 24. Its shader code and targets (the scratch target and the history, 20 bytes a half-resolution texel) are compiled out and not made without it (`SF_SCREEN_GI`); `--no-screen-gi` restores the frame |
| `--occluder-light` | The light of what hides the sky (eo-render `ambient.wgsl` `amb_irradiance`, `amb_occluder_exitance`, `amb_sample_ground`, eo-look `occluder`; off by default and in no tier; needs `--ambient-volume`): the volume knows how much of the sky a point sees; without this switch the sky it blocks is lit by the rig's ground lobe (`AMB_OCCLUDER`: every wall and soffit as bright as a sunlit street), so a canyon's lower floors, which see more wall, came out brighter than its upper floors and the ground under a deck as bright as open ground. With it the blocked sky is lit by the shaded walls' own exitance: the walls round a point whose street sees v_g of the sky are a canyon's and see the sky such a canyon's walls see (the two-dimensional canyon's view factors: a street seeing sqrt(1 + (H/W)^2) - H/W lies between walls that see (1 + W/H - sqrt(1 + (W/H)^2)) / 2 of it; the whole wall's, since what a street point loses is mostly the upper floors), the street below them (the ground lobe with its sky part at v_g) and, over the rest of their upper half, walls like themselves (M = rho E / (1 - rho (1/2 - v_w)), rho the sun bounce's warm 0.31 / 0.28 / 0.24); the street's lobe below the point, the ambient term's and the environment's ground share keep their sky part only as far as the street under the point sees the sky (the volume facing up 1 m over the ground there: a narrow street's or a deck's floor sends up the sky it gets); the specular environment's occluders take the same exitance; the rig's rim light (an unshadowed directional stand-in for the sky opposite the sun) is hidden as the sky along it is (the volume's visible share along its direction over the open sky's), taken from the fill it stands for; and the sun bounce (`--sun-bounce`) adds a sunlit wall's sun part whole (its sky part is the walls' exitance already). What hides the sky is a mix of walls and decks' undersides by where the sky the point still sees lies (the visible sky on a vertical surface over that facing up, a0 / (a0 + ay): 1/2 under the open sky, lower between walls, higher under a lid; the lid's share eases in from 0.5 to 0.8, eo-look `occluder::lid_share`): an underside sees beyond its edges as much open ground as the street under it sees of the sky beyond them (the same edges from the other side), that ground lit by the sky and the sun (the ground bounce's sunlit exitance), and under itself covered ground lit by the street's own sky, at a weathered concrete's 0.30 (`soffit_exitance`), so the ground under a viaduct takes the warm light of the sunlit street beyond it, not the sky colour of a canyon's walls. Where the volume knows the ground, the rig's ground lobe's sun part leaves whole for the sunlit ground's exitance over its open share (the ground bounce alone takes out at most that exitance a channel, which at a low sun left the neutral lobe's green and blue: a navy fill on every underside; eo-look `bounce::added_irradiance_whole`). With the open sky and open ground the sum is the rig's as before (an open plaza does not change). The pipelines that read the volume's levels take it: the city, structures and probe faces read the street's own sky apart (1 m over the ground under the point); the cars and people (since r2i3; agents.rs `agents_city_source`) take the street's sky from the volume at the point itself facing up (`AMB_OL_STREET` off), the lobe's sky part at it, the ground's sun part whole and the rim hidden as the city does, but what hides the sky lit by a stand-in set first thing in the fragment (ambient.wgsl `amb_ol_pre`: the ground lobe x 0.3, eo-look `occluder::AGENTS_OCCLUDER`, the shaded walls' exitance over the lobe for streets seeing a quarter to two fifths of the sky): their opaque shader with the cars' materials aborts Apple's compiler with the separate reads or with the walls' or a deck's exitance worked out per pixel, so a car under a deck now darkens with the deck's shade (it kept the sunlit street's lobe below it). The plane-only pipelines (the far layer and the props: street trees, lamp posts, bins) keep the stand-in (look.rs `city_shader_source_variant_probes`: `hlod-draw` and `prop-cutout` fail to build on Apple's GPU with it in some feature sets). Its shader code is compiled out without it (`SF_OCCLUDER_LIGHT`); `--no-occluder-light` restores the frame |
