| `--dome-env` | The environment's colour from the sky the frame paints (eo-render look.rs `LookGpu::frame`, eo-look `env_sh_with_dome_colour`; off by default and in no tier): the environment SH every lit surface reads for its diffuse environment and its rough reflections (the shaders' `lk_sh`: the cosine-convolved SH9 of the environment) was integrated from the analytic dome, while the sky pass paints that dome graded (San Francisco's haze desaturating and lifting the low sky, the horizon's haze band): noon's horizon is painted at b/r 1.1 and was reflected at 2.5 (the analytic zenith's 9), so rough streets at a grazing view and the environment's fill reflected a deep blue sky the frame never shows. With it each direction of the upper hemisphere keeps the analytic dome's luminance (the energy the rig's yield is solved against: within 2 % every way, tested) and takes the painted dome's chromaticity (16 x 32 directions, once a frame built, cached by the frame's own SH); the street field below the horizon is as it was. CPU only: nothing in the shaders, no GPU cost. The sharp reflections (`lk_sky_sample`) and the probes are unchanged. `--no-dome-env` restores the frame |
| `--dome-yield` | With `--dome-env`, the painted dome's colour stands in for the analytic dome's only (eo-look `env_sh_with_dome_colour_yielding`, eo-render look.rs `LookGpu::with_dome_env`; on by default, so on wherever `--dome-env` is, play mode included): by day the physical daylight (`eo_look::phys::apply`, weight `SkyTerms::daylight_weight`) replaces the analytic dome's environment with its own sky, integrated through the physical atmosphere and graded by the weather's cirrus and haze, and `--dome-env` recoloured that too with the analytic dome's paint. At Mission and 3rd Streets at noon on 1 August (play mode) the daylight's environment reads blue/red 1.9 facing up and 1.1 on a wall, the recoloured one 3.5 and 2.3, and the shaded street came out at blue/red 4.0 in the frame (a matched CC0 photograph of the same corner: 1.06). Now the colour is mixed by the analytic dome's share, `1 - daylight_weight`: the physical daylight's environment is kept whole by a clear day, the analytic frame (night, deck weather) is recoloured as before. CPU only. `--no-dome-yield` restores the frame |
| `--shadow-slope-bias` | The sun cascades' casters drawn with a slope-scaled depth bias (1.5 depth slopes a texel, at most 0.002 of the light range): surfaces the sun grazes (a viaduct's fascia, a wall nearly edge-on) no longer shadow themselves in stripes. The `high` and `hyper` tiers turn it on; `--no-shadow-slope-bias` |
| `--grime [1\|2]` | Surface weathering that reads at normal viewing distance (eo-render `weathering.rs`, `weathering.wgsl`; off by default, so the frame is as before): walks at an aged, soiled mid grey (LBNL weathered concrete 0.20-0.30, drawn at 0.225) with each scored slab its own pour and a few lighter replacement slabs, darker joints, a dense trodden-gum speckle that keeps its mean at any distance, blotchy stains and dirt along the kerb and (decals) the building line; kerbs grimy up the face with crumbled arrises and, at each corner on the vehicle approach side, the daylighting's red paint (`no-parking-crosswalk-daylighting`, 20 ft) faded and chipped; carriageways with a quilt of trench patches and repaved pieces in different greys with sealed saw cuts, cracks where the old concrete base's slabs meet under the asphalt (transverse a slab apart and longitudinal along the lanes, on a cell net stretched along the road; some sealed with a 2-3 in bead of binder and the squeegee's film, black fresh and dusty grey aged, the rest open hairlines with ravelled edges), more oil down the lane centres, darker gutter pans and worn thermoplastic markings (soiled, blackened in long tyre streaks along the line, worn through to the aggregate in fine specks, a few small chips; the scanned road-line mask's holes and marks kept at a third, since the scan repeats along the line); facades washed down below every sill line (some rust-stained), sooted under the roof edge and on old buildings' upper storeys, splashed at the foot, sun-faded on the walls the day's sun reaches most, each building its own age; and patterns that still read from a block away (50-200 m, where the sill streaks average out): soot and rust plumes down from the roof edge, damp patches with a tide line at the foot, and on painted fronts paint blocks repainted in a paint that does not quite match (most on the ground floor) or sun-faded; Blender-built buildings the plumes and damp, far buildings (the far layer) each their own tone; cars dusty on the lower body and grimy round the wheel arches, older ones' paint dull, each car its own; the kit buildings' windows (stage W5): a dirt film on the glass (haze that blurs and dulls the reflection, grime against the frame and in the bottom rail's tide band, run-off from the head, dried rain spots, more on old buildings and on their unwashed upper storeys, shop glass washed often and curtain walls on a contract, hand grease on shop doors; the lit room behind held back by it), old painted sashes and frames chalked and peeling to grey wood or an older coat, dirt on the trim's ledges and a dark drip edge under them. Albedo and roughness only (and the glass's metalness, clearcoat and opacity). Level 2 adds a second gum scatter and deepens the plumes and repaints. With `--structures` the freeway concrete weathers by each structure's era (eo-structures `Era`: the 1955-1971 viaducts and ramps heavily, the rebuilt Central Viaduct and Bay Bridge West Approach less, the Presidio Parkway hardly): soiling in water-stained blotches, run-off streaks down the fascia from the barrier top and on down the girder, down cap faces, walls and columns (some rust-coloured or white with efflorescence under joints and bearings), salts leaching from the soffit's joints, splash-back at column and wall feet, grime along the barrier toe; with `--tunnels` the bores' tile is sooted over the vault and in the grout, road-sprayed over the bottom metre, unevenly glazed with a few stained and replaced tiles (eo-render `structures.wgsl` `sx_weather`, `sx_tile_weather`). The kerb paint and building-line dirt are street decals and need the CC0 sets (`EO_CC0_ROOT`); the rest is procedural and needs nothing. The `medium` (default, since the owner's approval of 2026-11-20) and `high` tiers set 1 and `hyper` 2; `--no-grime` |
| `--street-life [1\|2]` | The small things that say people use a street (eo-render `street_life.rs`, `street_life.wgsl`; off by default, so the frame is as before), as street decals placed per tile by deterministic rules and drawn in the material of the surface they lie on: paper scraps, receipts, cups, wrappers, caps, napkins, cigarette butts and torn bits on the walks and against the kerb in the gutters, dead leaves under the surveyed street trees (DataSF street tree list), grit and sand drifts in the kerb corner and along the building line, cardboard and bags on neglected stretches; on the walk the litter lies where SF litter collects, not evenly over it: on the kerb's lip and in the furnishing zone beside it, against the building line and in recessed doorways (the standards table's `sidewalk-furnishing-zone-sf` and `sidewalk-frontage-zone-sf` on the ground plan), in the street trees' basins (at the trunks' kerb offset), round the bins and where people wait at the transit stops, in small clumps with bare walk between, the through zone left with the odd butt or scrap (its gum is the scanned wear decals'), the wrappers' and cups' prints sun-faded and dusty; clusters metres long with a dark wet halo that still read at 30-80 m (drifts against the kerb and along the building line, a pile at each surveyed storm-drain inlet, a bin's spill at the stops and corners); heaps at the wall's foot (bags, a leaning sheet of cardboard, a stained mattress); on the ground floors at the building line (every kit material but glass) bold graffiti: fat-cap and marker tags (2.5-7 cm strokes, some two-tone) in a low and a high band, throw-ups 0.5-1.25 m tall with drop shadows, drips and overspray, on the hottest walls a field of old faded tags and multi-colour pieces with a 3D block, buffed-over patches and wheat-pasted posters; round the freeway columns' bases (`--structures`) the same, covered to 2-3 m. Densities by land use (the building-metadata packs' `use`, else the OSM tags: industrial and vacant fronts most, shops some, homes little, civic buildings none), foot traffic (the surveyed stops and shopfronts), corners, the street's class and the decks overhead; each block side its own care (some swept and buffed, some neglected). No letters, names or marks anywhere: tags are meandering strokes with curls and hooks on no alphabet, throw-ups and pieces merged blobs and random strokes, posters colour fields, bars and dots. On the kit buildings' glass (stage W5): stickers on shop doors and in a corner of other shop glass (opening-hours plates, card marks, an alarm firm's square: plain shapes and colour fields), tags scratched or acid-etched into ground-floor panes where the wall beside them carries tags (jagged tangles, no letters), paper, kraft paper, newspaper sheets, board or soaped-over glass inside vacant shops (dark at night), and potted plants on flats' sills seen in parallax behind the glass (more on older buildings; dark against the lit room at night). Level 2 is a step denser (more litter, tags, posters and heaps) and adds stickers, utility locate marks and pigeon droppings under the decks. Needs the CC0 decal grid (`EO_CC0_ROOT`). The `high` tier sets 1 and `hyper` 2; `--no-street-life` |
| `--stand-ins` | A street-prop kind whose GLB is absent from the checkout draws a procedural model of the San Francisco design at the GLB's size (eo-render `props_lib2.rs`, stage W2a) instead of a grey box; with every GLB present it changes nothing. On in the `medium` (default, since 2026-11-20), `high` and `hyper` tiers; `--no-stand-ins` |
| `--surveyed-furniture` | The street-furniture walk fills the gaps between surveyed items with what a San Francisco footway has (eo-props `furniture::surveyed_first`): a slot its hashed walk gives to a kind rare on SF footways (bollards, planters, barricades, scaffolding, newsracks) stays empty, and one it gives to a street-commerce kind (benches, bins, post boxes, bike racks, café sets, sandwich boards) stays empty unless the frontage there trades; the walk steps on as if the item stood there, with the same draw. Surveyed, observed and forced items are as before. On a citywide sample of 265 tiles: bollards 4,486 to 0, planters 1,588 to 0, newsracks 726 to 0, bins 1,717 to 179, benches 1,305 to 127, post boxes 1,045 to 131; trees, meters and signs unchanged; hydrants 1,508 to 1,250 (an emptied slot takes a placed item's step); parked cars 3,784 to 3,981 (fewer kerbside items in the way). Render-only (the props are not in the collision world or the server). On in every tier; `--no-surveyed-furniture` gives the JS walk as ported |
| `--tree-basins` | A street tree's basin under each tree and palm the furniture walk places on a footway (eo-props `basins.rs`, eo-render `props_lib.rs` `Model::TreeBasin`): a panel flush with the walk whose bed is drawn in the props shader (props_draw.wgsl `pr_basin`): the scanned CC0 `park_dirt` soil where the scanned sets are on (`EO_CC0_ROOT`), else drawn grain, clods and grit, under damp patches and the watered ground round the trunk, the root flare (five tapering roots, read from the trunk's place in the instance's `variant`), leaf litter piled against the cut edge, a cigarette end now and then, moss by the edge and the cut face's shade line; a fringe past the edge keeps only the crumbs and leaves spilled onto the walk (cutout pipeline); centred on the trunk and squared to the kerb, moved out to clear the curb top, laid at its highest corner's ground plus the street's marking lift and tilted with the street's grade along the kerb, so it is never buried on SF's slopes. Its size is the minimum basin of SF Public Works Director's Order 187,246 for the footway's surveyed width (`street-tree-basin-sf`: 3 x 4 ft from 7 ft 6 in, 4 x 4 ft from 12 ft, 5 x 5 ft from 13 ft; across the walk x along the kerb; a narrower footway's older planting the smallest). Trees off a footway (medians, parks) get none. On a citywide sample of 177 tiles: 1,644 basins for 1,643 trees and palms (3 x 4 ft 380, 4 x 4 ft 165, 5 x 5 ft 1,099). Three levels, drawn to 110 m, no shadow. Render-only. On in every tier; `--no-tree-basins` gives the trees standing on bare paving as before |
| `--yard-lidar-trees` | Residential trees measured from the USGS 3DEP lidar point cloud (eo-props `lidar_trees.rs`; off by default until reviewed under the visual gate; distinct from the hill parks' default-on `--no-lidar-trees`): every crown the lidar survey (`eo-kit` `examples/lidar_survey.rs`) wrote to `$EO_LIDAR_DIR/<area>/trees.json`, drawn where nothing else stands (off the footprints, the drawn street and hill-park lidar areas, 2.5 m clear of a tree already planted, outside the parks' tree classes) with the street species whose crown-to-height ratio is nearest the measured one, scaled to the measured height and, within a third either way, the measured crown; in the near props and the distant trees alike. Only the areas surveyed have them (the pilot: Liberty Hill, 300 m square). `EO_LIDAR_TREES_TRACE=1` prints, per street tile, why measured trees were left out (a diagnostic; the survey's `EO_LIDAR_DUMP=1` writes every roof's cells). `--no-yard-lidar-trees` restores the frame |
| `--tree-crowns` | Trees past the near ring read as full, leafy crowns instead of a few sprig cards (off by default until reviewed under the visual gate; `--no-tree-crowns` restores the frame). Three parts. (1) The street species' window and far forms (70 m to 300 m) take a crown core (eo-props `eztree/crown.rs`, eo-render `props_lib2.rs` `CrownCores`): a closed hull fitted to each form's own leaf-card cloud (in each direction the 85th percentile of the cards' reach; 320 / 80 triangles, lumpy), coloured with its cards' tint at the outer leaves' tone times the mean of the species' leaf texels, on which props_draw.wgsl draws leaf clumps (`pr_crown_leaves`: clumps about 0.6 m and leaves about 0.2 m across in the tree's own metres, each clump lit by its facing, dark gaps between, each scale faded to its mean once a pixel spans it) and frays the outline (`pr_crown_edge`, in the cutout part); its sun shadow is dappled (props_shadow.wgsl) and a near tree's core casts none. The near form (to 70 m) and leafless species are untouched. (2) The props' trees end at 300 m (3D, to the bounding sphere) and the distant tree cards (eo-render `hlod_trees.rs`) are whole from 264 m measured the same way, instead of from 270 m along the ground, so from the air (straight down from 400 m nothing was drawn) the crowns no longer vanish between the two; each card faces the eye, a disc of the crown from straight above, its trunk hidden once the eye is more than 37 degrees up. (3) With `--yard-lidar-trees`, a measured crown is matched to the species' visible crown (its cores' footprint, 0.5-0.75 of the reach cap the cards touch) rather than the cap, the crown scale within 60 % of the height's. The pilot square straight down from 400 m: tree canopy 0.0 % to 5.9 % of the frame (the lidar's 10.6 % of the 1:1 ground is 6.8 % at the ground plan's x1.25 with real-size crowns); from 170 m 1.8 % to 3.9 %. Cost (interleaved runs at 1920 x 1080, the pilot poses' bench and the standard paths): +0.03 to +0.13 ms a frame (pilot poses +0.03 / +0.06 / +0.03 ms, straight down 0.00 / +0.13 ms; standard paths +0.00 to +0.19 ms). The crown cores are cooked into the props library's cache. Under the switch the cards are whole from where they start (no screen-door fade-in): each stands inside its fading prop |
| `--yard-detail` | Rear yards measured from the parcel map, the lidar and the aerial (eo-props `yards.rs`; off by default until reviewed under the visual gate): the yard survey (`eo-kit` `examples/yard_survey.rs`) writes `$EO_PARCELS_DIR/<area>/yards.json` and `yard-ground.bin.gz`. Fences: on the lot lines two parcels share (the side and rear lines; not a street frontage), where the USGS 3DEP lidar shows a thin raised line 0.9-3 m over the ground on a confirmed share of the yard stretch, at the measured height (median of the line's stations), the kit's unpainted cedar with vertical boards (a `Planks010` scan where the CC0 sets are on), cut where the drawn street or a drawn building stands, following the drawn ground; one block of the props arena, drawn to 1.5 km and casting into every sun cascade (eo-app `yards.rs`, eo-render `props_yards.rs`). Ground: each yard cell's class (lawn, planting, paving, deck: NAIP colour and texture with the lidar's vegetation and raised flat surfaces) drawn by the terrain's shader in the city's own ground tones with CC0 scan detail (eo-render `yards.rs`, `yards.wgsl`, group 0 binding 30; needs the CC0 sets). Plants: with the survey's `yard-canopy.bin.gz` (each yard cell's lidar vegetation height and aerial colour) the measured vegetation stands as plants instead of a ground tone (eo-props `yards::plants`): trees on the canopy's tops that no `--yard-lidar-trees` crown covers and the measured trees standing in the yards, as ez-tree garden trees (broadleaf, dense evergreen, conifer; drawn and casting as the coastal trees, to 360 m), shrubs at the measured height under them (ez-tree garden species, eo-props `eztree/garden.rs`: pittosporum, boxwood, rosemary, with the coastal coyote brush and sage; a third of the tall ones a big shrub pruned up into a small tree, the dense evergreen's shape; each its own value and cast, a fifth yellow-green, a tenth blue-grey, one in twenty bronze; leaf tints at a measured leaf's reflectance), small plants in the beds, and mown turf on the lawns (vegetation_models `V_LAWN`: blades 5-12 cm in clumps with a thinning rim, dead and yellowed blades, clover and a broadleaf weed now and then; a texture past 28 m), and rough grass in drifts through the beds' litter and over most of the open beds (a quarter turf tufts, the rest the headlands' short tufts and wild grass bunches, three to eight a 0.9 m square); one props block with the fences, the shrubs seen to 1/0.6 of their own reach (`YARD_REACH`) and casting into the near shadow cascade only. The fences gain their framing (4x4 posts, 2x4 rails on one side, a 2x6 cap; NIST PS 20 dressed sizes) and weather board by board to silver grey; paving regions (one parcel's each) that fill most of their least-area rectangle are laid as it, an irregular one cut into up to four such rectangles, never over a lawn (the survey's canopy file carries the squared classes), and paved yards carry edge grime, stains and 1.5 m control joints. The ground's class tones follow the aerial's brightness (and a third of its hue) cell by cell, two classes blend along their edge, paving takes worn or stained concrete, pavers or brick by yard, and the planting under the plants is a yard's own bed ground to 35-80 m (leaf litter, dark bark mulch, dead grass through the litter or gravel, by 10 m cells meeting along wandering lines; the litter's leaves at half their scan's chroma), the canopy's shade past it; paving and decks keep straight edges. Objects: with the plants, the rear yards hold what a city backyard does (eo-props `yards::items`, eo-render `props_lib2/yard_items.rs`, eo-app yards.rs `item_instances`): by 10 m squares, rows of the black, blue and green collection carts against the house, patio sets (weathered teak with a furled umbrella, or a black cafe set) and kettle grills on the paving, terracotta and glazed pots and half barrels planted with a shrub along a patio's edge, raised vegetable beds with their crop on open ground, small sheds backed onto a fence; each clear of the vegetation, the other objects and the fences, only in a rear yard (a house within 12 m, no drawn street within 6 m), weathered at its foot and in its colour. Park-size lots (over 4,000 m^2: Mission Dolores Park's) are left out of the canopy file's classes, so nothing is planted or placed there. Backs: with the plants, the backs of the kit's houses round the yards carry what a yard looks up at (eo-app `yards/backs.rs`, eo-render `props_lib2/yard_backs.rs`): each building beside a surveyed yard is planned again from the shared kit city (eo-render `kit::shared_kit_city`, at its tiles' floor), and on a back wall that opens onto a yard a wooden back stair stands where its columns fit: a landing and a back door (nine lites over two panels) at each flat's floor (a house's lowest), in a column of the wall no window of those floors crosses; switchback flights two a floor, side by side in two lanes, then on down to the yard (a shorter last flight where little is left, a few steps straight out from a house's floor near the ground); risers within the residential code's 7-3/4 in, 10 in treads, 36 in guards, 4x4 posts from the ground, nothing of it in a building, a street or across a fence, and the yards' shrubs, trees and objects kept off it; a downspout from a conductor head under the roof's edge to a shoe and splash block at a back corner. Each building's stair takes one paint (bare weathered wood, grey, white, a brown stain, sage), each door its own. Wood: the stairs, the patio sets, the raised beds, the barrels and the sheds take a grain along each piece (the `Planks010` scan, each piece its own patch) and painted wood wears through to grey bare wood where it is walked and rained on most (props_yards.wgsl `pr_yard_item`). Only surveyed areas have them (the pilot: Liberty Hill, 300 m square). `--no-yard-objects` leaves the objects out (the library as before); `--no-yard-stairs` the backs' stairs, doors and downspouts; `--no-yard-wood` draws the wood in its flat colours; `--no-yard-plants` restores the yards without the plants, objects and aerial tones; `--no-yard-detail` restores the frame |
| `--probe-grid [1\|2]` | Reflection probes on the streets round the camera in place of the single cube at the viewer: 1 four probes of 128 px, 2 nine of 256 px (in an array with two spare slots: 7.1 / 49.5 MB with the face target and table). Anchors at every junction's centre and along every drawn street's centreline at most 32 m apart, 1.7 m over the street datum, none inside a building; the set is the probes nearest a point 24 m ahead of the eye, each captured once when it enters (one face a frame in all, the single probe's deferral rules) and again only when the clock's quarter-hour or the sky moves. A new probe fades in, a dropped one out, a recapture cross-fades from its old cube (0.6 s): no reflection changes in one frame. Every lit surface (city, glass with the canyon lift, agents, props, landmark wires, CC0, the sea) reads the two probes nearest it by distance (and the analytic sky past 250-600 m from any); the far layer reads the analytic sky. With it or `--far-shadows`, a camera jump starts a capture under way again (a cube's six faces under one camera's shadow fit). `--no-probe-grid` restores the frame |
| `--wet-surfaces` | Every exposed surface takes the rain (the reflect track; eo-render sky_light.wgsl `sky_w2_*`; off by default, its code compiled out with `SF_WET_SURFACES`; `--no-wet-surfaces` restores the frame). A surface darkens by its porosity, read from its dry roughness (Lagarde 2012: open asphalt, concrete, render and soil about halve, painted metal and glazed faces keep their colour), and the film fills its relief; walks, plazas, open ground and roofs wet like the street (with the damp preset's drying patches); walls take the rain's runs, soffits stay dry. Puddles stand where the ground's hollow field (domain-warped value-noise settlement hollows of about 4, 1.6 and 0.55 m, and the gutter's fall) passes the weather's water level (its puddle fill: about 3 % of an open carriageway at fill 0.1, 11 % at 0.45, 16 % at 0.6, 30 % at 1, most of the gutter pan; walks and open ground half that), with a sharp shore widened to the pixel's footprint and broken into lobes and fingers by the aggregate's 12 and 5 cm relief where the pixel holds it, a damp halo of saturated ground round it (darker than the film: soaked, no film), and still water inside, never a mirror: its alpha widened by the wind's capillary wavelets finer than the drawn ones (their mean square slope a fortieth of the open sea's Cox & Munk 1954 a m/s, for a puddle's metre or two of fetch, at a fifth of the weather's 10 m wind: the street canyon's lowest centimetres), and in rain a second lobe (alpha 0.04, the drawn rings' own root mean square slope) over the share of the water under drop rings (1 - exp(-0.15 rate), from Marshall-Palmer drop counts and a ring's life and area) where the rings are too fine to draw (where they are drawn their slopes are the normal's), so a lamp's image in a puddle is a short bright streak, not a dark hole in the film's streak; rings drawn as capillary packets (2.2 cm wavelength, 0.25 m/s, 0.42 s) only where a wavelength spans three pixels or more, the rest of their slope in that lobe. The film and the water reflect their environment whole (the rig's environment class for a dry walk or road no longer dims it). Draws only in wet weather (`--weather rain`, `storm`, `drizzle`, `damp`, or the schedule's) |
| `--wet-relief` | The water where the street's own relief holds it, the film by the surface under it (the reflect track; eo-render sky_light.wgsl `sky_wr_*`; over `--wet-surfaces`; off by default, its code compiled out with `SF_WET_RELIEF`; `--no-wet-relief` restores the frame). On the carriageway and the gutter pan (with `--street-pbr`) the ponds are the street pass's: the wheel paths' ruts (laid out from the standards' lane, parking lane and design vehicle), the settled birdbaths and the pan's flow line hold water deeper than what has drained since the rain (street_pbr.wgsl `sp_wet_relief`, its depth `sp_pond_water`), over the pavement's profile inside those basins (two domain-warped octaves, 3 mm over 1.7 m and 1.2 mm over 0.45 m: the screed's undulation and the mix's segregation, an urban street's roughness), so a rut holds a chain of pools with lobed shores, not a strip with a straight edge, not `--wet-surfaces`' value-noise hollows (blobs either side of a crowned road's centre line, a canal from kerb to kerb in a narrow street); the shore anti-aliased by the pixel's footprint over the relief's slope, a ring of saturated ground half a millimetre of depth wide round each pond. There (not on walks and plazas, which keep `--wet-surfaces`' film and puddles: their noise hollows are their only ponds, and a rougher film seen low made them dark holes in a bright sheet; there the trace filters the film as an isotropic surface's, sideways by alpha n.v as it stretches toward the eye by alpha, where the look's anisotropic streak lobe, a seventh as wide across, drew a pillar, a post or a person on a wet walk as a hard-edged slab) the film's roughness is the look's calibrated film times the surface's own roughness over the street's mean (held within 0.7-1, never rougher than the look's film: its scan, the polished wheel paths, the oil band, the sealant, the patches; the film of a few tenths of a millimetre is roughened as much by its own drops and drying edges, and a floor of 0.45 made a smooth plate a hard-edged mirror and sealant lines laser-thin streaks), at every angle of view (no drop toward 0.06 seen low: GGX and Fresnel make the sheet, and the drop made the film as sharp a mirror as the ponds, which then could not be told from it), and the streak lobes' alphas times that squared, so a lamp's or a headlamp's streak breaks into bands along the wheel paths and glints on the sealant instead of one uniform bar, and a pond is a sharp mirror in a rougher film. There the film and the ground under a pond darken by Lekner & Dorf (1988), (1 - r_e)(1 - r_i) / (1 - r_i A) of the albedo A (r_e 0.0204, r_i 0.474 for water): 0.53-0.55 for asphalt after the street pass's own, 0.6 for the pan's concrete (asphalt kept a third of its albedo before, over the street pass's 0.7). Draws only in wet weather |
| `--lamp-streaks` | The cars' rear lamps streak on the wet street (the reflect track; eo-render sky_lamp_streaks.wgsl, sky_light.wgsl `sky_ls_*`, sky_reflect.rs; under `--look cinematic`; off by default, its code, its pass and its words after the streak list made only with `SF_LAMP_STREAKS`; `--no-lamp-streaks` restores the frame). A tail lamp on a wet road at night throws a long red streak toward the eye as a street lamp does, but its lens is a few hundred square centimetres: read from the frame through the film's wide lobe its few pixels averaged away with the dark body round them. So, like the street lamps and headlamps, the rear lamps are reflected analytically: one workgroup after the streak pick takes, of the cars whose rear faces the eye and stands in view, the six brightest at the eye (intensity over the square of the distance, quarter-EV bins, the bin that fills the list and the cars over it) and writes both rear lamps as light records past the frame list's cap, appending their indices to the streak list (inboard of the rear wheels' pivots, 0.45 m over them, 0.85 m behind the axle; ECE R7's tail lamp 8.2 cd or, while the car brakes, its stop lamp 105 cd, the geometric means of the standard's ranges; a lens of 0.03 m2; a cone of 20-75 degrees round the rear axis; the red primary at unit luminance, ECE red lying outside sRGB), at the frame's lamp units with the vehicle lamps' and the street lamps' clock (by day a rear lamp is lost in the daylit film). The streak loop (`sky_cine_streaks`) reflects them with the list's lights through the same film lobes, unchanged but for its count's cap (a loop that read them from a second place cost the city's opaque pass about a millisecond on the overview, compiled in and never run); the frame list's count leaves them out, so no cluster, sprite, fog or shadow sees them (the cars' paint, which reads the list's first 32, `--car-reflections`, sees those of them that fall there). While it runs, the frame list's own rear lamps (`--car-lamps`, `--rear-lamps`) take no place in the streak pick (sky_streaks.wgsl `lt_streak_q`), so no rear lamp streaks twice. Draws only on the wet street, at dusk and night |
| `--probe-sky` | The probe grid's sky and levels (the reflect track; eo-render probe_sky.wgsl, probe_grid.rs `SkyFill`; off by default, its pipelines made only with `SF_PROBE_SKY` compiled in; `--no-probe-sky` restores the probes): where a capture's face drew nothing, the sky the frame draws behind everything (the volume pass's own sky function, sky_volume.wgsl `vol_atmo_sky`, cut out of that file so the two never part: the atmosphere tables by their share, whole by day and twilight, none at deep night, over the drawn dome with its night blue, plus the city's glow), greyed toward its luminance by the cloud cover (the deck is not marched into a probe; clouds scatter every colour alike), instead of the analytic dome alone (golden and blue-hour glass reflected the dome's saturated cyan beside a gold or violet sky); and levels 1.. GGX-prefiltered at the roughness the lookups read each at (`pg_lod`), 32 samples a texel by filtered importance sampling (Krivanek & Colbert 2008) from the levels already made, n = v = r, weighted by n.l, instead of 2 x 2 box means (a hard-edged blocky blur with no GGX tail). Costs only at a capture (six faces a probe on the 0.25 h bucket) |
| `--screen-reflect` | The screen-space reflection's third generation (the reflect track; eo-render sky_light.wgsl `sky_sr_*`, sky_reflect.rs; off by default, its code compiled out with `SF_SCREEN_REFLECT`; `--no-screen-reflect` restores the frame): a trace along the ray's own path across the previous frame's half-resolution depth, stepped on the screen (McGuire & Mara 2014: 1/w and depth/w are linear there, so the ray's depth at each texel is exact) through the depth's hierarchy (Hi-Z: the pyramid's alpha holds the nearest view depth of each cell of 2 to 64 half-resolution texels, rounded toward the eye; a cell the ray crosses wholly in front of its nearest depth is passed in one step and the walk climbs a level, one it may pass behind is looked into a level finer), so every texel of the path is looked at in at most 48 steps (the linear march's 40 steps, packed toward the start, skipped up to 25 texels at a time at its far end and stepped over thin poles and kerbs), the first a texel and a half out, jittered per pixel and frame, a texel hitting where its depth lies inside the ray's depth across it and within the sample's assumed thickness (0.4 m + 4 % of its depth), but a ray that comes in behind a face (at its silhouette, deeper than its own depth across the texel and the bias) only as deep behind it as the thing is wide (measured as below, again only for another thing), so a person or a pole is not drawn out into a slab on a wet walk (the fixed thickness held a ray a metre behind a person), 5 bisections and a secant against the depth read bilinearly where its four texels are one surface (a reflected facade moves smoothly with the pixel, not in half-resolution blocks); the first thing a ray passes behind holds it where the ray is no deeper behind its face than its width (a canopy, a facade, a car body: the depth within 35 % of its own at taps 4, 12 and 32 texels ahead along the ray's path and either side of it, the smaller of the two), the hit its face there (a ray from a puddle into a street tree's crown read the sky past the leaves: a hole the sky's colour in the tree's reflection), while a pole, a sign or a person, narrow across the path, is passed; a ray that stays on the frame to its full reach and never meets or passes behind anything has escaped and sees the sky. The hit is filtered by the surface's lobe seen through the mirror, read from the previous frame's own mip chain (the two resolved histories carry eight levels of 13-tap means while the switch is compiled in, built after each resolve, the first level's taps held to the bloom's source ceiling, the alpha the Hi-Z from the same frame's occlusion depth; the cone reads levels to 1/32; every other reader keeps level 0): a GGX lobe of alpha a turns the ray by about 1.1 a in the plane of incidence and 1.1 a n.v across it (why lights streak toward the eye on a wet road, longer the lower they are seen), an angle sigma L / (D + L) at the hit (sharp where the reflected thing stands on the surface, long for far lamps, windows and signs), read at the across width with up to 12 taps along the streak, none jittered across a lobe (the speckle in tail-lamp and window reflections was jittered taps the history's clamp kept), capped at 24 x the exposure; the lobes are the wet film's anisotropic one, a puddle's own and, in rain, its rings' second lobe where the rings are too fine to draw (`--wet-surfaces`). A ray the trace does not stop reads the previous frame along its own direction through the same lobe where that is open sky or scenery past 1.5-3 km (tested on five depth texels round it, so a skyline fades in over a few pixels) or scenery far past the surface (parallax, range over depth, under 0.05-0.25 rad: the far towers in a puddle), the sky just above the skyline for an escaped ray whose direction lands on a nearer building; on ground facing up and for escaped rays a direction off the frame reads the frame's edge toward it where that is sky, scaled by the weather dome's gradient between the two by daylight (the CIE overcast's by its overcast weight, held within 0.5-2; flat at night) and trusted less with the angle between them (fully within 0.3 rad, not past 0.9); a pane's or a car flank's ray that neither hits nor escapes reads 60 % of what the camera sees along its direction (the facades across, the sky over them) over the probe grid. Glass traces in any orientation, its clearcoat reading the trace over the probes. The specular occlusion (the occlusion pass's visibility as a cone) darkens only the share of the reflection the trace did not find (the environment and the frame's sky along the ray): a hit's radiance is what the occluder sends, and occluding it again took most of a car's, a kerb's or a wall's reflection at its foot. Rides on the reflection and the occlusion pass (`--no-ssr` or `--no-ao` turn it off with them) |
| `--ambient-volume [1\|2]` | Large-scale sky visibility: narrow streets, courtyards and the ground under a deck get less of the sky's light than an open square, and what blocks the sky lights them instead (the rig's ground bounce), so nothing goes black. Baked on worker threads from the building prisms, the terrain and, with `--structures`, the decks, per 128 m tile round the camera (1: 4 m cells at least 430 m out; 2: also 2 m cells at least 170 m out; 24 layers to 80 m above the ground) and once over the whole city at 8 m, cached under `<cache>/ambient/`. Props and the far layer read the city-wide 8 m plane only (Apple's shader compiler does not take more in their shaders). Off by default; `--no-ambient-volume` restores the frame |
| `--ground-bounce` | With `--ambient-volume` (on with it unless `--no-ground-bounce`; nothing without it): light bounced up from the sunlit ground onto deck soffits, cap beam undersides, girder webs and walls facing a sunlit street. The volume's levels also bake the open ground's share of each cell's lower hemisphere (ground, roofs and deck tops with nothing over them; not the ground under a deck), weighted by how likely the ground round the cell is to be in the sun (its horizon of buildings and terrain: a narrow alley's floor bounces little); undersides read it shifted toward the sun by their deck's shadow. The shader takes the rig's ground lobe's sun part (it stands for a lower hemisphere of sunlit street) out of the lower hemisphere and puts the sunlit ground's exitance from the sun (albedo 0.20) x the cloud transmittance back over that share, so undersides read brighter towards an open edge and darker deep under (a lid over a closed yard keeps only the lobe's sky part); nothing changes facing up, with the sun down or in a cloud's shadow. Twice the volume's GPU memory. The city, the structures, agents and landmark wires take it; props and the far layer do not (Apple's shader compiler); past the volume's levels there is none |
| `--smooth-time` | The drawn hour follows the simulation's clock (eo-sim's fixed-step time, the server's with `--connect`; it starts at `--time-of-day` and stays there without a simulation, `--no-agents`), through keyframes of the look a clock minute apart blended each frame; the rig's quarter-hour rows through a monotone cubic; the moon takes the key over from the sun in a blend; vehicle lamps and the offices' morning share ramp instead of stepping; the exposure converges instead of snapping on a turn or a clock jump (a teleport still snaps it); the histories survive small weather steps. Off by default |
| `--rt-gi` | Ray-traced one-bounce diffuse light (a prototype; no tier turns it on). Inline ray queries (wgpu's experimental `EXPERIMENTAL_RAY_QUERY`) against the traced scene (eo-render `rt_scene`): acceleration structures built from the renderer's own vertex and index arena: the terrain, the street's flat far strips and plazas, the grade-separated roads' static level and the landmarks in 256 m cells, each resident kit tile's rung (the near rung where it is resident, else the mid one), and the hand-built models as they arrive, within 420 m of the camera (no agents, props, trees, water or the street's near packs: kerbs and paint). The city's opaque pass writes a thin G-buffer beside its colour (`rt_composite`: the indirect diffuse it added and what multiplied it, the vertex normal; the colour itself is unchanged); then, at half resolution, one cosine-distributed ray a texel from that surface: a ray that leaves the scene takes the rig's sky in its direction, a hit its Lambert radiance (the material's albedo, the kit paint and layer mean, the street class; lit by the sun through a shadow ray, the rim, and the rig's sky x 0.6 for the bounce beyond). Accumulated over up to 24 frames with reprojection and filtered by an edge-aware a-trous filter stopped by the surface's plane and normal and by the history's variance; a full-screen composite in the same frame puts it in place of the hemisphere light, the environment's diffuse (by the traced open share), the ambient volume and its ground bounce, and the screen-space bounce, with half the screen-space occlusion kept; it fades back to the rig's light from 260 to 340 m. The rest of the city pass (far layer, trees, props, agents, far shore, sky, glass) draws after the composite in a pass of its own and keeps the rig's light. Metal only (the one backend tested): on any other device it is off and the log says so in one line. `--no-rt-gi` (the default) leaves the shaders, the layout, the device and the frame as before. Tunables for experiments: `EO_RT_GI_*` (eo-render `rt_gi::RtGiConfig::from_env`; `EO_RT_GI_DEBUG=1` the traced light alone, `2` the open share, `3` the bounce) and the traced scene's `EO_RT_RADIUS`, `EO_RT_DROP`, `EO_RT_BUILD`, `EO_RT_KIT_NEAR`. The bench JSON's `lighting_passes_ms` times `rt_gi` (trace, temporal, filter; the acceleration-structure builds are outside it), `rt_composite` and `city_rest`, and `rt` reports what was traced. Cost (M5 Ultra, high tier, 1920 x 1080, a busy machine: load 15-30 with other sessions' GPU work; the lower of two interleaved runs' GPU frame medians against the frame without it): street_walk +0.7 ms, kit_street +0.0, drive +0.8, city_overview +0.4 (nothing is traced past 340 m there). Of it the trace and filter take 0.5-0.65 ms, the composite 0.6-0.8, and splitting the city pass so the rest draws after the composite 0.3-0.6; the city's early opaque draw is unchanged within the noise (stage R1 read the result in every lit pipeline, which cost that draw 1.0-2.5 ms). The bench's settle builds the traced scene round the camera before a capture is drawn |
| `--rt-reflect` | Ray-traced reflections (a prototype; no tier turns it on; with or without `--rt-gi`, one G-buffer for both). From the same G-buffer (the reflection the rig added, its Fresnel and occlusion weight, the roughness, the vertex normal), at half resolution, one ray a texel drawn from the surface's GGX lobe (the mirror direction below roughness 0.06) through the same traced scene: a hit takes its Lambert light, a ray that leaves the scene the analytic sky the rig reflects, scaled by the surface's environment class. Accumulated over a number of frames that grows with the roughness (none on a mirror, up to 12) and filtered as far as the roughness reaches; the composite puts it in place of the sky, probe and screen-space reflection on the streets, wet or dry, the buildings and their glazing, terrain and structures up to roughness 0.6, fading back to the rig's from 260 to 340 m. Not yet on car paint, props, the bay or the glazing's clearcoat (drawn after the composite: the rig's reflection), and what is reflected is lit diffusely, without props, agents, trees, water or emissive light. Metal only; `--no-rt-reflect` (the default) leaves the frame as before. Tunables: `EO_RT_REFLECT_*` (eo-render `rt_reflect::RtReflectConfig::from_env`; `EO_RT_REFLECT_DEBUG=1` the traced reflection alone, `2` its radiance, `3` the rig's reflection it replaces); the bench JSON's `lighting_passes_ms.rt_reflect`. Cost (as `--rt-gi`'s): alone street_walk +0.6 ms, kit_street -0.1, drive +0.7, city_overview +0.5 (the trace and filter about 0.2 ms, its composite 0.25-0.3, the split city pass as above); with `--rt-gi` street_walk +1.75, kit_street +0.6, drive +1.4, city_overview +1.1. |
| `--photo-finish [1\|2]` | A photographic finish (eo-render `photo.rs`, `look_photo.wgsl`; eo-look `tone`, eo-sky `photo`; off by default, so the post pass and the sky are as before): the post pass becomes a camera on the resolved frame. Its response keeps the old chain's neutral curve (middle grey lands where it did, so the exposure means the same) on each colour's brightest channel, keeps the colour's hue, pales bright colours toward white by as much as a per-channel curve would (a lamp's or a sunlit wall's core goes to white instead of clipping to a pure hue) and turns their hue a third of a per-channel curve's way in the shoulder; the grade adds no saturation or vibrance and rolls its brightness off to white instead of clipping. The sensor adds noise in the scene's units (shot noise and a read floor, by the ISO a phone camera at f/1.8 and 1/30 s picks for the scene's EV: base ISO by day, invisible; a few hundred on a lit street at night, a fine grain in the shadows), a new pattern each frame, after the TAA. The lens adds a residual fall-off (0.85 at the corners), lateral colour growing to about a pixel at the corners, and a bloom with a lower, softer threshold, more of a small source's light and less in the wide levels (a glow round lamps and glints, no veil). The sky's tables and the aerial perspective take San Francisco's measured air (IMPROVE's 50 km visual range row, an aerosol of albedo 0.9 under the 400 m marine inversion): a paler horizon and a warm-grey haze over the far city. Level 2 adds chroma noise and a smoother lateral colour. The `high` tier sets 1 and `hyper` 2; `--no-photo-finish` restores the frame |
| `--facade-clutter [1\|2]` | The things San Francisco fronts carry (eo-kit `clutter.rs`, stage W3; off by default, so the kit meshes and their cache key are as before): fire escapes on older multi-unit fronts (Tenderloin, Chinatown, Nob Hill, SoMa, the Mission most; one per stack of windows: slatted landings at each floor above the ground floor with picket railings, 60-degree stairs between them through a hatch, a raised drop ladder, knee brackets, a gooseneck over the parapet where it reaches; painted steel, mostly black, rusted by the building's wear; the port's own fire escapes are drawn this way in their place), awnings on shop and food tenancies without a shade (shed, dome, box and open retractable forms in the common fabric colours and stripes, sun-faded, a few torn), sign faces on the fascia boards and projecting blade signs (lit boxes or neon tubes on a dark board) carrying a generic word of the shop's category (stage S1: `SIGN_PHRASES`, CAFE, LIQUOR, DELI, LAUNDRY ...; never a name, a brand or a mark) in a bold, condensed, serif or script face found among the system's fonts at start (drawn in memory, never written to disk; with none installed, W3's colour fields and shapes), beside an emblem or in a border, painted, back-lit (letters over a dim face, or the face lit through under the tone curve's clip) or in neon tubes with their glow, faded by the building's wear; stacked letters on tall blades; lit by night where the shop is lit (`draw.wgsl` `fc_lit`: with `--shop-light` where the shop's own light is on, dimmed to its security level, else by the shops' lit share), roll-down security shutters (some down), bars over older ground-floor windows, downspouts at the party walls with their conductor heads and straps, meters, gas meter sets, conduit and the standpipe connection at the base, and on flat roofs chimneys with clay pots, vent stacks and flue caps, satellite dishes aimed at the south-south-east sky and TV aerials. Nothing passes through another piece or the plan's own parts, and nothing stands out over the walk past `sidewalk-width-sf-neighborhood-residential` less `sidewalk-edge-zone-sf` (the alley widths on a service way; awnings and signs also under the code caps and the 8 ft clearance). Near level all of it, mid level a simplified version, far level none. Level 2 adds window air conditioners (rare), dishes on fronts, pipe scaffolding and boarded shops. The kit tiles cook into their own cache folder with it. The `high` tier sets 1 and `hyper` 2; `--no-facade-clutter` restores the meshes |
| `--facade-glass` | The kit glazing drawn as glass is (eo-render draw.wgsl, rooms.wgsl behind `SF_FACADE_GLASS` and the frame switch `facade::RUN_FACADE_GLASS`; off by default, so the frame is as before): each pane a dielectric with two reflecting faces (F0 0.04 each, Schlick: the light's base layer and its clearcoat layer), opaque over its room, which shows through what the faces do not reflect, tinted by the glass, in place of a 28 % blend whose reflection took the dark room's colour as a metal's tint (the black glass); each pane on its own normal (rooms.wgsl `rm_pane_normal`: set up to ~0.46 degrees out of the wall's plane about each axis, a sealed unit bowed ~0.4 degrees at its edges; a window cut into panes of about 1.25 x 1.7 m), so the sky, the street and the sun's glint break pane by pane; behind it an office floor runs back 6.5-11 m to its core under a white acoustic ceiling lit by the daylight off the floor, each office its own troffer and grid layout, 85 % with them on and read by day as lit diffusers against the daylight (not the white shards of the default's gain); 62 % of office windows with a blind or (35 % of those) a dark solar screen of 14 % open weave, each at its own height, a fifth all the way down; homes also with roller shades, lit from behind by night (brightest across from the room's lamp, its light leaking past the fabric's edges); by night each lit room its own lamp's colour temperature (blackbody, seen against a 4300 K white: homes mostly 2300-3400 K, 12 % cool LED at 3800-4500 K and 3 % 5000-6200 K tubes; each office floor's tenant its own, 2900-4800 K) and offices as varied in brightness as homes; glass at the packing's limit (ribbons and curtain walls over 15.9 m) takes rooms of two or three 1.5 m mullion modules with their mullions and, on a curtain wall, the building's own storeys (its sill rhythm, `fa_drivers`): vision glass from floor to ceiling and an opaque spandrel to the next floor, not 3.6 m cells of the world (the checkerboard). INFERRED shares and sizes (rooms.wgsl `FG_*`). `--no-facade-glass` |
| `--far-facades` | The far layer's roofs and walls (eo-render hlod_draw.wgsl behind `SF_FAR_FACADES` and the frame switch `facade::RUN_FAR_FACADES`, hlod.rs `draw_wgsl`; off by default): a district's flat roof carries its plant (at most one unit of 1-3.6 x 1-3.2 m and 0.8-2.4 m tall in each 7 m square, 14-62 % of the squares by building), a stair or lift penthouse (3-5.5 x 3.5-7 m, 2.4-3.6 m tall) in 12-55 % of its 22 m squares, hatches and skylights, and the shade units and penthouses cast down the roof, as long as the sun's height throws it (a ten-step march toward the sun over their tops, so a golden-hour unit shades many metres), on a membrane of re-roofed patches and ponding stains (a cell's roof the membrane only: its bake carries the kit's own plant); each far window its own room (a blind or a lit ceiling in 36 %); a wall the far layer draws as its plain prism (a building with no kit plan and no model here, as the Ferry Building) a dressed-stone front: a ground-floor arcade of round-headed openings, a belt course, windows in stone surrounds with sills in storeys and bays, a cornice with dentils under its coping, each opening a recess its reveal shades and the low sun reaches only where the ray clears it, soiled at the foot and sooted under the cornice (the wall's top and first corner from its quad's first vertex, its foot from the cell's lowest point). Everything filtered over the pixel's footprint and given way to its mean below a pixel. INFERRED proportions (hlod_draw.wgsl `FF_*`, `FB_*`). `--no-far-facades` |
| `--facade-weather` | The kit walls' weathering where it reads (eo-render kit.rs `weather_marks`, draw.wgsl, facade_age.wgsl `fw_apply` behind `SF_FACADE_WEATHER` and the frame switch `facade::RUN_FACADE_WEATHER`; off by default, so the kit packs, their cache key and the frame are as before): every wall piece in a window's column (the band under a window and the band over the window below, one piece on the mid rung) carries its place across the window and its depth under the sill above in its normal's spare bytes (the kit tiles cook into their own cache folder with it), so the rain's run-off hangs from each sill: dark streaks from most sill ends (45-95 % by the building's wear), widening as they fall and fading over 0.5-3 m, uneven down their length, drips off the sill's front edge, and the dirt settled in the sheltered strip under the sill; at every wall's foot the splash-back (a ragged band of mud specks), on render and masonry rising damp with a pale salt tide line, and the street's grime fading up the ground floor. In the rain the walls are wet where the driven rain reaches them (more up the wall and under the roof's edge, less on the sheltered ground floor) and in the run-off's paths under each sill: render and masonry darker and deeper in colour, still matte; paint less dark and glossy; a sheet of water down the streaks. A pale finish ages: a white paint film chalks and takes the street's soot, a pale render dulls, so a white trim or wall reads about 0.6-0.7 (0.72 on a new building to 0.60 on a worn one, each wall a little its own, eased under that ceiling and greyed a little by chalking) where the kit's fresh white is 0.79. Every detail gives way to its mean as it shrinks under a pixel. INFERRED sizes and strengths (facade_age.wgsl `FW_*`). `--no-facade-weather` |
| `--canyon-glass` | The street canyon in the kit glass (eo-render rooms.wgsl `cg_canyon`, draw.wgsl, look_light.wgsl behind `SF_CANYON_GLASS` and the frame switch `facade::RUN_CANYON_GLASS`; off by default, so the frame is as before): a pane reflects what stands across its street, not the street-level probe's lower street. The canyon comes from the ambient volume (the city's own prisms): its height the plane's reach, its width from the sky its floor sees (a long canyon of aspect A shows its floor 1 / sqrt(1 + 4 A^2) of the sky), the opposite roofline's elevation from the sky the pane sees straight out ((1 - sin e) / 2); the reflected ray is cast against it and what it meets is shaded as the frame shades it: the sky over the roofline (the dome, as bright as the probe grid sees the sky straight up and greyed by the cloud deck's cover, or the probe itself where it sees open sky along the ray), the opposite facade below it (the sun by the shadow maps a step in front of it and the clouds, the sky and street light by the ambient volume there; an albedo by building from weathered concrete, limestone, render, brick and dark granite or curtain glass, its storeys of windows reflecting their own sky, lit rooms by night by the rooms' occupancy, a cornice shadow under each roofline and grime toward the street), roofs past a lower building, or the street (its sun and sky the same way). The reflected detail is filtered by the pixel's cone through the mirror, the panes' waviness and the glass's blur; a pane at the street keeps half the probe (its cars and shop fronts). With `--facade-glass` the pane's two faces (Schlick) carry it, so a facade seen near grazing turns to the canyon's mirror and its rooms show only near the normal. INFERRED albedos, window share, storey and bay (rooms.wgsl `CG_*`). `--no-canyon-glass` |
| `--far-relief` | The far layer's construction depth (eo-render hlod_draw.wgsl `fr_roof`, `fb_facade` behind `SF_FAR_RELIEF`, hlod.rs `draw_wgsl`, hlod_district.rs `roof_frame_words`; off by default and in no tier; compiled in only with the switch, no runtime gate). A district's flat roof sits under its parapet (the wall carried up past the roof; IBC 705.11.1 asks 30 in where one is required): the roof lies the parapet's height under the prism's top, so the eye's ray meets it that much further on, or first meets the inner face of the parapet across from the eye (lit as a wall, in the wall's colour), with the coping along the top; the parapet on the sun's side shades the roof as far as the sun's height throws it, over the key light only (look_light.wgsl `fr_sun_vis`), so a golden-hour roof is half in its parapet's shade and every flat roof reads as a box with its rim. Each roof-ring vertex of the district mesh carries its distances to the four sides of its footprint's minimum-area oriented box (0.25 m steps, coarser on long boxes) in the normal word the walls never read there (a wall triangle's flat words come from a base-ring vertex), the box's heading and the parapet's height in its uv word's upper half; the districts cook into their own files (`district_rf_*`), so a run without the switch reads and writes the districts as before. With `--far-facades`, the plain prism's stone front (the plaza's arcade front) casts the sun's shadow from its corona (0.85 m), belt courses (0.22 m), sills (0.12 m), pilasters (0.12 m) and pavilions (0.45 m), over the key light only, and the sky under each projection is cut as it hides the upper hemisphere (the ambient terms only); its ground storey is an arcade 2.6 m deep (shop fronts at its back, the vault and the paving in shade, the sun reaching in only where the ray clears the opening); a three-bay pavilion stands at each wall's first corner and every 13 bays, rusticated, its returns seen edge on; the other windows sit 0.15 m deeper; each block and each bay a little its own in tone, washed down from the cornice. The stone is a light grey-buff sandstone (linear 0.47/0.43/0.355, each building a little its own) rather than the prism's far tone (a plain prism's palette tone is its glass's pale blue); the soot crusts where the rain never washes it (under the cornice, belt courses and sills, in the reveals, the arcade's vault and back wall: by the sky each projection hides) and the upper faces of the sills and belt courses are washed lighter. Every band is a coverage over the pixel's footprint. INFERRED: the parapets' heights (low buildings 0.45-1.05 m, towers 1.1-1.5 m, by building; none under 24 m2) and the stone front's proportions (hlod_draw.wgsl `FR_*`, `FB_*`). `--no-far-relief` |
| `--facade-patina` | The kit walls' ageing laid on the building rather than on a lattice of blocks (eo-render facade_age.wgsl `fa_weather`, `fa_kit`, `fa_apply`, `fw_apply` behind `SF_FACADE_PATINA`; off by default and in no tier; compiled in only with the switch, no runtime gate). Without it a kit wall seen along a street is a patchwork of soft-edged rectangles a metre or a few across: with `--grime` (level 2 in the hyper tier) the weathering's paint blocks (weathering.wgsl `wx_mid`: a repainted or sun-faded rectangle a bay by a storey on a lattice of the wall) and its streak columns hung from every sill line of the building's rhythm, window or not; the ageing graph's own 0.35 m streak columns under the same sill lines; the scanned stain columns, 2.4 m wide, under them; and its grime, overpaint ghosts and mottle as value noise on one lattice square to the wall. With it and `--facade-weather`, none of those columns or blocks is drawn (weathering.wgsl is not changed: `fa_apply` leaves out its `wx_facade` call; the trim's chipping, `wx_window_trim`, stays), and what stains a wall follows the building: each window's run-off (`--facade-weather`) gains the sill's wash (the dirty water off its front edge soils the wall under the window across its width, to nothing at the jambs, fading over 0.7-2.6 m toward the head below, its foot ragged in 0.2 m fingers, each window its own: most a little, a few heavily); the parapet's run-off hangs from the roofline (streaks of their own lengths, 0.8-4.5 m, the soot crust right under the coping) and where the coping sheds its water a broad plume (0.5-1.5 m, one in 2.5-5.5 m at most, 30-70 % of them by wear) runs 20-65 % of the way down the wall, wandering and widening; the grime the rain carries is drawn long and upright (about 1.1 x 6 m and 2 x 8 m) in streaks with clean wall between; every remaining field rides a slow warp of the wall (2.2 m) on a lattice turned its own way, so its edges run in curves; the paint chalks by its exposure (most on a south or west wall, more up the wall than at its shaded foot). Metal (roller shutters, steel) rusts in its grooves (the finish's own occlusion), along its foot and bottom rail and in streaks run down from its fixings (up to four in five 0.7 m columns, by wear), in place of orange blots over the slats. On porous masonry (concrete, stone, brick) a window's run-off streaks spread 2.6 times as wide, run 1.35 times as long and read 1.3 times as dark as down a paint film (the face drinks the dirty water as it runs), and a face cast in units (concrete-precast, civic-concrete, cast-stone: facade.rs `PANEL_FINISHES`) gives each spandrel under a window (the pieces marked with it) its own tone, within 7 % lighter or darker and a little warmer or cooler, as precast panels from different pours are. Every detail gives way to its mean under a pixel, and no plume or streak is cut at its column's edge. INFERRED sizes and strengths (facade_age.wgsl `FP_*`). `--no-facade-patina` |
| `--window-depth` | The reveal round every kit window (eo-render kit.rs `split_ribbons`, `glass_depths`; rooms.wgsl `wd_pane`, draw.wgsl behind `SF_WINDOW_DEPTH`; off by default and in no tier; compiled in only with the switch, no runtime gate). Each upright glass face of a kit window carries how deep it stands in its reveal, across (behind its jambs' face) and up (behind its head's and sill's), in 0.03 m steps, 4 bits each, in the normal's y byte (an upright face's own y is 0; the shader clears it), cooked into its own kit cache folder (`|window-depth-v2`). On the near rung a ribbon of glass behind piers and floor bands (a downtown tower's punched grid, a curtain wall's bays) is cut into the openings they leave, each a window of its own (its own frame and room), the glass hidden behind a pier or band left out, the piers and bands carried back to the glass so no slot opens behind them, and each band cut at the piers into one piece an opening, which `--facade-weather` marks as the spandrel under that window (its run-off, and with `--facade-patina` its unit's tone); the mid rung keeps its ribbons. The pane is then shaded as glass in a hole in a wall: the sun reaches a point of it only if the ray to the sun clears the jambs and the head (their shadows across the glass and the blinds behind it, hard-edged, as long as the sun is low; over the key light only, look_light.wgsl `fr_sun_vis`); the room behind keeps the sky a slot's view factor lets through, one axis after the other (darker toward the reveal); and the glass reflects the reveal where its reflected ray meets a jamb, the head's soffit or the sill before it clears the opening, in the light that face gets there (the sun by the shadow maps, the sky and the street by the ambient volume), mixed over the canyon's reflection (`--canyon-glass`, rooms.wgsl `cg_apply`). A ribbon at the packing's limit leaves that axis open. INFERRED: the reveal's reflectance (0.3) and the opening's margin past the glass (0.03 m; rooms.wgsl `WD_*`). `--no-window-depth` |
| `--shop-light [1\|2]` | Lit shop windows that carry the night street (eo-render `shop_lights.rs`, sky_lights.wgsl `lights_shops`, rooms.wgsl `rm_shop_light`; eo-sky `shops`; off by default, so the frame is as before): behind every shop window the kit draws (the kit planner's shop bays, planned again on worker threads once the kit city is loaded, a few seconds) a light 0.3 m inside the glass at the window's middle height, aimed out and a little down with the window's lobe, in its trade's colour as a night-adapted eye sees it beside SF's 3000 K street lamps (bars ~2300 K, restaurants 2700 K, shops 3500 K, markets and laundromats 4300-5000 K), at its window's luminance (bars ~16, restaurants ~40, shops ~75, markets ~120 cd/m2) times its glazed area, lit by its trade's opening hours with a per-shop spread (most shops dark after 19:00-21:00, restaurants until 22:00-23:00, bars until 02:00, a few markets, pharmacies and laundromats round the clock, a share of fronts vacant, a share of closed shops keeping a dim display or security light), so a block is mixed. Through the frame's light list: clustered (after every street lamp and headlamp: a crowded cluster drops shops first, and the lamp shadows' stable order ranks them last), occluded with `--light-shadows` (no map of their own), lighting the fog with `--lit-fog`; the room behind a kit shop's glass reads its light (lit in its colour at its window's luminance, or dark when the shop is closed), giving way to the rooms' own rule at each room's own distance over the last fifth of the reach. Stage N2: that room is the shop's own, filled by its trade (eo-sky `shops::fit_of`; eo-render `shop_rooms.wgsl`, read through the fronts table at group 1 binding 19): gondola runs and wall shelving stacked with goods (markets, liquor, pharmacies, hardware, books, flowers), clothing racks and a display table, tables and chairs under pendants with a counter and a lit kitchen pass, a bar's counter, stools and back bar, salon chairs facing a mirror wall, washers and stacked dryers, a gallery's framed pieces, a clinic's reception, a cleaner's bagged rails, a gym's machines; a back wall in the trade's colour; ceiling strips, troffers, downlights or track spots, the light falling to the back and the corners; now and then a person; colour fields and plain shapes, nothing that reads. One room behind every pane of the shop (its door and windows); its lit surfaces where the night exposure shows them (about a tenth of the window's luminance), its fixtures blown out; by day the same room through the glass; a closed shop dim under its security light. One vacancy rule (eo-sky `shops::vacant`, per shop): a vacant shop's lights stay dark (a few keep a work light), its room is emptied and dark and `--street-life` papers its glass; a front `--facade-clutter 2` boards up is vacant and never lit. `EO_SHOP_INTERIORS=0` (a diagnostic) keeps N1's rooms and W5's own vacancy. 1: within 160 m of the eye, one light a window; 2: to the light clusters' 300 m, a light every 2 m of window. Nothing by day. The `high` tier sets 1 and `hyper` 2; `--no-shop-light` |
| `--car-detail [1\|2]` | The cars' detail (eo-agents `vehicles::far_body`, eo-render `agents::Tiers::with_car_detail`; off by default, so the frame is as before). Without it each traffic body has four levels from the browser build: the full body within 22 m for the 2 nearest cars, LOD 1 within 60 m for the next 10, LOD 2 to 160 m, then three stacked boxes (36 triangles) to 400 m. With it the far slot is the body's own LOD 2 simplified at load (each material and wheel part on its own, by meshopt's clustering, to 0.2 m: about a pixel where the slot starts; 1,080-1,800 of LOD 2's ~5,900 triangles over the shipped fleet), in the same mesh slots, so the people's mesh budget is unchanged; the library is cached under a key of its own. 1: the full body within 30 m for the 6 nearest cars, LOD 1 within 90 m for the next 24, LOD 2 to 220 m, the far body to 400 m; 2: 40 m (12), 120 m (40), 260 m, 400 m. Shadow casters follow the same distances (cascade 2 casts the far body). Levels still switch without a fade, and nothing is drawn past 400 m (the moving traffic lives within about 240 m of the camera). The bench JSON records the level and the tiers under `car_detail`. The `high` tier sets 1 and `hyper` 2; `--no-car-detail` |
| `--car-materials` | The cars' own materials and lamps (eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `ag_car_*`, eo-agents `vehicles::role_of` and `CookOptions::car_materials`; off by default, so the frame is as before): each fleet material's role (glass, privacy glass, tyre, plate, chrome, lamp cover, cabin, wheel, black trim, lamp reflector, light bar, brake, livery) is cooked into its flags, each licence plate's own coordinates into its vertices, and each vertex normal's residual into its spare byte (`vehicles::pack_normal_fine`: the two smaller components to a fourteenth of an 8-bit step, the largest from unit length; at worst 0.04 degrees where the 8-bit normal is off by 0.37, which a mirror-like lacquer doubled into ripples in its reflections); the agent library cooks under a cache key of its own. Paint is a base coat (the paint's colour x 0.8 under the lacquer, older paint chalked to a warm grey; metallic flake in a binder, metalness at most 0.55) under a clear lacquer (F0 0.04, GGX 0.04 new to 0.12 old, grime roughening it, smoother where a wet roof holds water); the base coat's diffuse takes the environment at the facades' class, as the street round it is lit, its reflections and the lacquer's at the painted-metal class (about 1 x the environment), the lacquer's environment occluded by the ambient volume as the base's is (a car under a deck reflects the deck, not the sky; a hook in look_light.wgsl, `sky_coat_env`). Glass is a thin dielectric: Fresnel reflection of the same environment and of the lamps' glints over the cabin seen through it (a clear screen passes about 75 %, privacy glass its material's share), in place of a diffusely lit panel; the cabin sits in its own shade (a third of the sky and ambient light). Lamps glow in saturated LED red and amber, the red and amber ones held under 8 x the exposure's white (past it the camera's crosstalk, eo-look `tone::SENSOR_CROSSTALK`, whitens the core, as a real sensor does), with the LEDs' 12 mm pitch where a pixel resolves it; a red or amber cover is a coloured translucent layer lit as a surface (by day the deep red or amber its bowl sends back through it; 40 % of what lies behind shows through), its lit lamp filling it at 8 % of the emitter's luminance round the brighter LEDs; a clear headlamp cover glows at 5 % of its lamp (never on a parked car); a red cover low in the bumper is a passive reflector (unlit, a deeper red); a lit lamp's reflector bowl glows with it; the rear plate is lit by its lamp with the tail lamps; a patrol car's light bar is lit only on a moving unit answering a call (a third), never on a parked one. Plates are California plates (white sheeting, the red script, seven embossed navy characters of no real registration in a 5 x 7 face read with rounded strokes, frame, bolts, the year sticker, road dirt), their characters drawn as their mean ink once a pixel spans half of one. Tyres are carbon-black rubber with road dust on the tread and the lower sidewall and raised lettering on the outer sidewall (shapes, no words), turning with the wheel; wheels carry brake dust, old or parked cars rusty discs, older cars sun-faded black trim. With `--grime` the road grime is the tyres' spray as a dark grey-brown road film (albedo ~0.12) densest at the sill and fading up the lower body to about the wheels' tops (highest behind each wheel, its top rising and falling slowly along the body and broken into run-off drips where a pixel resolves them), grime round the arches and a light dust film on the roof, bonnet and boot, at `--grime`'s per-car levels, in place of its blotches; no noise across a panel. The grime lies on the lacquer as matte cover (a dense film takes 85 % of the lacquer's reflection; between its particles the lacquer stays at GGX 0.16 at most) instead of turning the lacquer into a glossy 0.35 lobe. The wheel arches and the underside sit in the body's own shade. Every glossy part's roughness is widened by its normal's change across the pixel (specular anti-aliasing). Agents only: nothing else draws differently. `--no-car-materials` restores the frame |
| `--car-contact` | The cars' occlusion of the street round them (eo-render `agents_contact.rs`, agents_contact.wgsl; off by default: no pass is made or run and the agents' shader module is as before): a pass after the city pass (before the water and the occlusion pass) where each car within 120 m of the eye draws the back faces of a box round its lower body grown by 1.6 m, and each pixel under it facing up (the street, a kerb's top, a bonnet) gets, from the depth, the share of its cosine-weighted sky the car covers: the exact form factor of the body's occluder boxes (eo-agents `vehicles::contact_shape`, cooked with the cars' materials: the lower body from its underside to the beltline, the upper body to the roof, the four tyres) by Lambert's polygon formula over the faces the point sees, combined as independent occluders; nearly all of it under the chassis, most of it at a tyre's foot, about a fifth a metre beside the sill. Only the light the car takes is taken: the sky, ambient, rim and bounce light (where the frame's screen-space occlusion took less), never the key light where its shadow map lets it through. The street's lamps (each light of the pixel's cluster, shaped as the city's shading shapes it) are each taken where the car's bodies (the lower and upper body and the nearest tyre) stand between the point and the lamp: the ray to the lamp marched through the bodies' distance field, its least clearance over the lamp's penumbra there (the lamp a sphere of its luminaire's size), so the car's lamp shadows are hard at the tyres and the sill and soften away from them, fading out with the reach; where the lamp's own shadow map (`--light-shadows`) already darkens the point the lesser of the two is kept, and a light at or in the body (the car's own lamp) is never taken. The HDR colour is multiplied by what is left. The bodies' boxes come with the library cooked as `--car-materials` cooks it (which this switch also asks for; nothing of the materials draws without `--car-materials`). `--no-car-contact` restores the frame |
| `--car-reflections` | The street's brightest lights mirrored in the cars at any distance (eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `ag_car_far_lights`, `SF_CAR_REFLECTIONS`; with `--car-materials` and `--look cinematic` only; off by default, so the frame is as before): the lacquer, the glass and the lamp covers (and chrome, reflector bowls and wheels with their own F0 and roughness) reflect each of the frame's 32 brightest lights toward the eye (the wet street's streak list, sky_streaks.wgsl: street lamps, props lamps, headlamps, lit shop windows) as a sphere of its luminaire's size, without the light clusters' reach (a mirror keeps its source's luminance however far it is: the highlight shrinks, it does not dim): the GGX lobe widened by the source's angle (on paint also by the specular anti-aliasing at four times its variance, so a lamp's mirror image does not break into glitter where a panel's normals bend fast), the reflected radiance never over the lens's luminance, the lens's emission toward the car at least the share the eye sees of it; the share the clusters' loop already gave inside a light's reach is left out, and a lamp with a shadow map is hidden where its map (read 15 cm off the body, so the car's own depth in it cannot speckle the highlight) says the point does not see it. At night every lamp and lit window leaves its highlight along the bonnet, roof, shoulder line, glass and rims. A light whose half vector lies where the widened lobe is under 1e-5 of its peak is passed over before its shape, map and BRDF (most of the 32 on most of a car: the term's cost). Agents' fragments only. `--no-car-reflections` restores the frame |
| `--car-traffic` | (captures) The `--light-shots` cards' streets as a player arriving there finds them (eo-app agents.rs `warm_traffic`; off by default, so the cards are as before): before each card is drawn the simulation is built again round its eye (the game's own start: the network round it, its cars and walkers placed and run 600 fixed steps; deterministic), instead of the one built round the first view, which never steps while the cards are drawn and leaves their streets without traffic; simulated agents whose body comes within 8 m of the eye are not drawn while the card is (no bus or walker filling the foreground). Each card's manifest entry gets `traffic` (vehicles, people). `--no-car-traffic` restores the cards |
| `--car-lamps` | The cars' lamps as a driver works them, and their rear lamps as lights of the street (eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `ag_lamp`, `ag_lamp_night`; agents_lamps.wgsl in the light gather, `SF_CAR_LAMPS` and its `LT_CAR_LAMPS`; eo-agents `vehicles::rear_lamp`; with `--car-materials` only; off by default, so the frame is as before): a parked car's lamps are all dark (tail, stop, head, daytime running and indicator lamps, its plate lamp, its bowls); a moving or queued car's lamps are lit or not, at their night luminance whenever the vehicle lamps are on (eo-sky `night::vehicle_lamps`: from dusk, and in rain or fog) and at the day's otherwise, instead of fading with the sun's altitude (a lamp's luminance is the same at dusk as at midnight; the exposure makes the difference). By day a moving or queued car's daytime running lamps are lit at their own luminance (`agents::DRL_CD` 700 cd over `DRL_LENS_M2` 0.006 m^2, UN ECE R87's 400-1200 cd: about 1.2e5 cd/m^2, a bright strip in sunlight, in the rig's units through eo-sky `photometry::lux_per_unit`; `AG_CAR_DRL_DAY`, agents.rs `car_wgsl`), where the JS's day floor drew them a dim grey; a clear headlamp cover and the front bowls are filled by the headlamps alone (not by the running lamps' strip), so nothing else of the front glows by day. A red or amber lamp's emitter (its light guide or LED row) is held at `AG_CAR_LAMP_CORE_WHITE` (24 x the exposure's white) where its cover keeps `AG_CAR_LAMP_WHITE` (8 x): the emitter is far brighter than its cover, so its core runs past the camera's red and the crosstalk turns it orange-white while the cover round it stays deep red; the housing's pillow optics (`ag_car_facets`: 18 mm chrome facets, each a Gaussian spot of 0.15 of a facet, mean 1, up to five times it) break a red or amber cover's fill at night and its bowl's return by day into a grid of glints where a pixel spans a third of a facet or less, their mean where it spans a sixth or more. Each lit car's two rear lamps join the frame's lights beside its low beams (sky_lights.wgsl `lights_agents`): at the body's own tail lamps (the mean of its tail and stop lamp materials' vertices on its full level, found at load, `Library::models_with_rear_lamps`, in the model's spare words; the light leaves 5 cm behind the body's tail, so the car's own body is never in its cone), aimed back along the body, red LED light at unit luminance, 8 cd for a tail lamp and 100 cd while the car brakes (UN ECE R7: 4-17 and 60-185 cd; FMVSS 108: 2-18 and 80-300 cd), shaped as the regulations' test points shape a rear lamp (`agents::rear_lamp_shape_fn`: a lobe exp(-(tan h / tan 20 deg)^2 - (tan v / tan 10.5 deg)^2), two fifths of the axis value 10 degrees up or down (the regulations' minima there are a third to a half of the axis's: the lamp is made for the following driver's eye), an eighth at 15 degrees, and a third 20 degrees aside, so the road a few metres behind the car takes only the lobe's edge, over the lens's own diffuse floor, 6 % of the axis value times the cosine off it, into the whole rear half-space; no cone edge), a 0.0125 m^2 lens, reaching where it gives 0.25 lux (kind 8, `agents::REAR_LAMP_KIND`). Every reader of the light list shades kind 8 by that field (`sf_rear_lamp_shape`, which look.rs `ShaderFeatures::wgsl` declares in every set: the clusters and the wet street's streaks in sky_light.wgsl, the lit fog in lit_fog.wgsl, the cars' far lights and the contact pass), so a braking car's stop lamps put a faint red glow on the street from its bumper, gone within a metre or two, light the car behind on their axis, and light the fog behind the car only (not round it).
