The clusters take the rear lamps in their second pass with the shop windows' lights (sky_lights.wgsl `lights_cluster`), so a car's lamps never take a street lamp's or a low beam's place in a full cluster; with `--car-reflections` a rear lamp's mirror image in a car is held to the lamp's own drawn luminance (`AG_CAR_LAMP_WHITE` x the exposure's white). The clusters light the street and the car behind in red with them, and the wet street mirrors them in its streaks where they are among the frame's brightest toward the eye; that pick (sky_streaks.wgsl `lt_streak_q`) weighs a low beam and a rear lamp by what each sends toward the eye's side (a low beam aimed away keeps only its lens's floor), so traffic driving away gives its streak slots to its rear lamps. The rear lamps light no fog pool or sprite of their own. `--no-car-lamps` restores the frame |
| `--car-wet` | The cars in the wet (eo-render agents_contact.wgsl `ag_ct_wet`, `SF_CAR_WET`; with `--car-materials` and `--car-contact`, under `--look cinematic` on a wet street; off by default, so the frame is as before): the wet film mirrors the frame's brightest lights in long streaks down the road toward the eye (sky_light.wgsl `sky_cine_streaks`); where a car stands between a road point and a streak light, the point mirrors the car instead, so the streak is cut and the car's dark mirror image stands in the road. In the contact pass each car within 120 m reaches its box toward the eye along the road (up to 30 m, stopping 2.5 m short of the eye, its height grown by a 15 % grade), and each road point in it (within 0.35 m of the car's ground plane, growing with the grade away from the car) reckons each streak light's reflection as the street does (the film's base lobe, its puddles' sharper lobes left out) and the share of the light's sphere the car's bodies leave it (`ag_ct_lamp_vis`, the lamps' shadow march; the car's own lamps never), and the pixel is multiplied by what is left of its radiance when the hidden reflections are taken from it: its radiance before the cut is the previous frame's (the screen-space reflection's history, already without them) plus them, a fixed point at the radiance less the hidden streaks, so what else the pixel holds (the street's mirror of the sky and the facades, a car's lamps in the screen-space reflection) stays; the street's own diffuse (its wet albedo under the sky, the key and the clusters' lamps) is never taken. Nothing on a dry street. `--no-car-wet` restores the frame |
| `--car-finish` | The cars' finish as the street wears it (eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `ag_car_finish_on`, `ag_cf_*`, `SF_CAR_FINISH`; with `--car-materials`; off by default, so the frame is as before). Paint: the base coat's metallic flakes glint one by one in the sun where a pixel resolves them (`ag_cf_resolved`: a footprint under `AG_CF_GLINT_PX`, 2 mm, about 2.5 m from the 1080-line camera; in full at half of it): a pixel's flakes turned within a quarter of the sun's solid angle of the half vector are a count (`ag_cf_count`: one or none under a mean of one, the mean and its spread over it) of mean `AG_CF_FLAKE_RATE` x the pixel's footprint^2 x the base coat's GGX D, and the sun's share of the base coat's lobe is redrawn as that count over its mean (a field of sparks over the same mean, held to the body at an octave of the pixel's scale); further out the camera's reconstruction filter and its history average a pixel's few glints with its neighbours', so a frame shows their mean (the sparks crawled from frame to frame and read as glitter at the cards' distances); the lacquer's orange peel (a 4 mm ripple of half a degree, under a pixel at the cards' distances) as its lobe 0.015 wider; road grime where the road and the weather put it (`ag_cf_grime`, in `ag_car_grime`'s place): the tyres' spray as a dry grey-brown film (0.16, 0.145, 0.12) at up to 85 % cover reaching the lower third of the body (1.9 wheel radii), densest in each wheel's wake and uneven along the body (a 0.3 m deposit), its upper edge run down in drips; the tail's wake (the surfaces facing back below the rear glass) at 70 % of the film's cover, heavier toward the bumper; run-off streaks from the glass's foot down the doors and the tail (one may start every 7 cm along the beltline, 0.7-1.7 cm wide, reaching down up to 60 % of the beltline's height at 55 % of the film's cover; their mean where a pixel does not resolve them); settled dust over up to 45 % of the roof, bonnet and boot, uneven over half a metre; every pattern drawn only where it spans three pixels (`ag_cf_keep`), its mean where it spans under a pixel and a half, so no grain lies across a panel; on a third of the old cars the lacquer failed in patches on the surfaces facing the sun (chalked base, matte, its reflection gone). Glass: float glass's green-grey transmittance (0.70, 0.80, 0.74), so the cabin, lit through it and seen through it, is drawn at (0.633, 0.827, 0.707) of its light; its trim matte and dark (albedo at most 0.12, roughness at least 0.6; the screen keeps its gloss); a dust film on the outer face (heaviest on the rear glass, lightest on the windscreen, thicker toward each pane's foot, dried spots in it where they span three pixels) single-scatters the sky and the sun round it (Henyey-Greenstein g 0.5). Rain (`skyu.wet` raining): beads on the lacquer (7 mm cells, 65 % held) and drops on the glass (11 mm cells, half held, stretched into trails down the vertical panes) as spherical caps of their own normals where a pixel resolves them (three pixels a cell); where it does not, the lacquer's and the glass's lobes widen, and within a glint's resolve (`AG_CF_GLINT_PX`) the far lights they mirror (`--car-reflections`) break into a speckle of the same mean; the film on the glass washed thinner; wet tyres take a sheen (roughness 0.32). While it is on the cars and the people are drawn by colour pipelines of their own (eo-render agents.rs `person_mesh_split`, `AgentPipes::make_for`; agents_draw.wgsl's pipeline-overridable `AG_PIPE`, 1 the cars, 2 the people: the bodies' meshes come before the people's in the library, so the one command list is drawn as two ranges), each compiled without the other's code: the two in one pipeline with the finish were more than Apple's compiler takes (`agent-opaque`: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED). With `--tunnels` the agents' module keeps the tent and the occluder light's stand-in (agents.rs `agents_city_source`: with the light track's agents' code it aborted the same way); with `--car-lamps` or `--car-wet` as well, `agent-opaque` still aborts with `--tunnels` (as on the vehicles track's branch alone). `--no-car-finish` restores the frame (one pipeline, as before) |
| `--car-density` | The traffic by street class and hour (eo-sim `region::TrafficDensity`, `consts::lane_density_per_km`; eo-app agents.rs `set_car_density`; off by default, so the simulation is the JS pool's as before): the JS pool's 42 cars are put down first exactly as without it (the same lanes and draws: every card keeps the cars it had, its bus among them), then on each lane the cars its street class carries beyond the pool's cars on it (the fleet up to `DENSE_VEHICLE_POOL`, 128 cars), from a stream of draws of their own (`DENSE_EXTRA_SEED`), each lane drawn by its shortfall (a quiet hour still fills an arterial the pool's even spread left short), its street class's density (vehicles per lane-km: primary 39, secondary 29, tertiary 20, residential 7, service 3, motorway 21; the flow a lane carries in the busy hours over its speed with the signals' delay, INFERRED from the order of the Highway Capacity Manual's urban flows) times the hour's share of the busiest (`hour_footfall`) times a falloff (whole within 120 m, a quarter at 200 m), instead of evenly over every lane within 240 m; the local recycler keeps the near streets' share (their cars within 120 m, at most four fifths of the fleet) where it is over the JS's 32. With `--car-traffic` each card takes the density at its own hour (the crowd keeps its own clock). `--no-car-density` restores the simulation |
| `--car-panels` | The cars' panels cooked taut and their badges as pressed chrome emblems (eo-agents `vehicles::filter_panel_normals`, `cook_body_opts`, `ROLE_BADGE`, `CookOptions::car_panels`; eo-render agents.rs `set_car_panels`, `SF_CAR_PANELS` in `car_wgsl`, agents_draw.wgsl `ag_car_badge`; with `--car-materials`; off by default, so the library, its cache key and the frame are as before). The fleet's bodies are panel-cut meshes of a few centimetres' tessellation whose vertex normals wander from a smooth surface's by about a degree (vertex to vertex; up to 6 degrees on a boot lid), which a lacquer mirrors at twice the angle: the street's reflection broke into patches across a door and the panels read as crumpled under a low sun. Cooked with it, each paint and livery vertex's normal is the area-weighted Gaussian mean (sigma 5 cm, within 10 cm) of the normals round it on the same side of every crease (a neighbour turned by more than 20 degrees is left out, so a character line, a panel's edge or a tight fillet keeps its turn): the tessellation's wander drops to about a quarter, every curvature wider than the radius stays. Each badge's emblem (a `Badge_*` mesh in VehicleChrome, thin across its face: a lens of a few vertices whose interpolated normals swept across the face, so it shaded as a chrome ball mirroring the whole sky, a glowing white sphere in the low sun) takes a material of its own with the badge role and its disc's coordinates in its uv (as a plate's), and is drawn flat across its face with a bevelled rim turning 60 degrees to the side (from 86 % of the radius), a raised polished ring and a polished bar on a satin field (roughness 0.06 and 0.3; their mean under a pixel or two), its specular anti-aliasing from the bevel's turn alone. `--no-car-panels` restores the library and the frame |
| `--night-photometry` | The night on one photometric scale (eo-sky `night_photometry`; eo-render look.rs `night_photometry`, sky_lights.wgsl `lt_eye_share`, sky_fx.wgsl `fx_eye_share`, rooms.wgsl `rm_office_zone`; off by default, so the frame is as before): the rig's night fill stood for 1.6 lux on unlit ground (`NIGHT_UNLIT_GROUND_LUX` x `NIGHT_GROUND_FILL`), so a lamp pool's 30 lux peak was only ~19 x the unlit city and the night solve exposed that unlit ground at a fixed display level (a frame with every practical off was nearly as bright as one with them on). With it the unlit ground stands for its physical 0.8 lux (a Bortle 8-9 city sky's glow, the moon, the spill and the street canyon's inter-reflection nothing models; between RP-8's lit-road minima, 0.67 lux local and 1.5 lux collector), so every practical (street lamps, headlamps, shop windows, lit rooms, signs, vehicle lamps: the lamps' units per lux and, in the shaders, the emission carried in the rig's units, the look uniform's `fog.z`) is `practical_gain` = 2 x brighter against the natural light, reached geometrically from sun +2 to -6 deg (`night::nightness`; the twilight fill cap's lamp scale is not applied), and the exposure's night share (`night::exposure_nightness`) comes from an incident meter on the lit street instead of the unlit-ground solve: an 18 % card under RP-8's major-road average (9 lux, 0.52 cd/m^2) shown at the key an eye adapted to it sees (Krawczyk et al. 2005, a = 1.03 - 2 / (2 + log10(L + 1)) = 0.113: 0.67 EV under middle grey) through the delivered display. The lenses of signals and pedestrian heads are solved in display terms and take no gain. A full-cutoff street luminaire's sprite (its lens, air light and glare) takes its intensity toward the eye (the lit fog's luminaire shape: none at 90 deg from the nadir, at least 3 % for the optics' edge) instead of its nadir intensity, and a far lamp's air light keeps single scattering's 1 / distance once its halo is pixel-sized, so the lamps down a long street no longer sum to a wedge; globes, banner pendants, landmark and bore lamps keep their nadir. Office rooms are lit in zones along each storey (9-24 m, switching with the office share) at a tenant's lamp mix (4000 K fluorescent with its green bias, 5000 K panels, 3300 K, a few 5300 K and 2700 K) and dimmer, instead of a per-bay checkerboard of one neutral white. By day nothing changes. Shader code behind `SF_NIGHT_PHOTOMETRY` (and the gather's `LT_NIGHT_PHOTOMETRY`); no tier sets it; `--no-night-photometry` |
| `--lamp-bounce` | Each street lamp's pool lights what stands round it (eo-sky `lamp_bounce`; eo-render sky_lights.wgsl `lt_bounce`; off by default, so the frame is as before): one bounce of instant radiosity (Keller 1997). The lamp's flux onto the street (the surfaces' own batwing to the 75 deg cut-off: 6.8 lm per nadir cd) times the street's diffuse reflectance (0.15: CIE 144's R3 road, pi q0 = 0.22 new, worn asphalt and concrete walks round it) leaves the pool as an upward Lambertian light standing 1.2 mounting heights (the pool's half-flux radius) under its centre, so it is the disc's irradiance at the pool's level and a point far off: about 1-2 lux on a facade 6-10 m from an arterial pole, less higher up and further out, none on the ground. Unshadowed (the surfaces' occlusion multiplies it), its lobe widened to the pool's size (no glint), no sprite, no lit fog, no wet streak; ranked after the shop windows in each cluster; drawn for lamps within 40 m of the eye, faded out by 80 m (past it the unlit fill stands for the street's inter-reflection). Shader code behind `SF_LAMP_BOUNCE` (the gather's `LT_LAMP_BOUNCE`); no tier sets it; `--no-lamp-bounce` |
| `--urban-sky` | The sky over a lit city (eo-sky `urban_sky`; eo-render sky.rs, look_sky.wgsl `sky_stars_urban`; off by default, so the frame is as before): the city's skyglow (the uniform's `amb_top.w`: the dome over the city, the deck's lit base, the volume's veil) is the street lamps' light scattered, so with `--night-photometry` it takes the practicals' gain; and the star field shows only what a city sky leaves: each candidate's magnitude from its rank (counts grow as 10^(0.5 m) to m 6), the sky's surface brightness from the city's glow (a clear zenith of 18.0 mag/arcsec^2, Falchi et al. 2016's Bay Area range, brighter toward the horizon by the glow's shape and under a brighter weather's glow), the naked eye's limiting magnitude for it (Schaefer 1990: 4.0 at the zenith, 2.8 low over the city), and the stars over it drawn at the field's scale by their flux relative to a first-magnitude star's (faded in over half a magnitude above the limit). Shader code behind `SF_URBAN_SKY`; no tier sets it; `--no-urban-sky` |
| `--office-floors` | An office building's glass shows open-plan storeys (eo-render rooms.wgsl `rm_office_floor`; off by default, so the frame is as before): not a 3.6 m room box per pane (the street saw a checkerboard of lit and dark cells, a mosaic on a curtain-wall tower) but one floor behind the whole facade. A 3.6 m storey with a 2.75 m finished ceiling (9 ft), the slab, plenum and back-painted spandrel above it; a curtain wall's 1.5 m module with 65 mm anodised mullions (a wide punched window split into equal modules on its own frame); the core wall 9-14 m back (lease depths 30-45 ft) with its doors; workstation panels 1.05-1.2 m high in rows 3.2 m apart with a 1.5 m aisle every 7.2 m (benching floors with their screens); a 600 mm T-bar ceiling with each tenant's 2x4 troffers, continuous linear rows or 2x2 panels. Lit by tenants: a storey is lit by the office share (its phase), three quarters of its 7-22 m zones on; a dark storey keeps a few late zones; a lit zone's light reaches ~2.5 m into a dark neighbour, a dark one shows the egress lighting's ~3 % (IBC 1008's 1 fc against 300-500 lux on desks); each tenant's lamp (4000 K fluorescent with its green bias, 5000 K, 3300 K, a few 5300 K and 2700 K) and dimmer. Roller blinds on a third of the zones, each module at its own drop. By day the same floor at the day's depth fall-off, the mullions and spandrels in their own colour. Replaces `--night-photometry`'s office zones where both are on. Shader code behind `SF_OFFICE_FLOORS`; no tier sets it; `--no-office-floors` |
| `--room-depth` | Lit homes and shops seen from the night street (eo-render rooms.wgsl, shop_rooms.wgsl `sr_surface`; off by default, so the frame is as before): a home's lamp lights its room as a point source (inverse square and cosine over an eighth for the room's inter-reflection) from its ceiling fixture or, in two homes in five, a floor or table lamp low by a side wall with the ceiling light off and its shade seen lit, so a lit window shows a hot spot, a lit wall and dark corners instead of a flat card; curtains, blinds and sheers at the glass glow by the light reaching their backs (an unlined curtain's 0.3-0.6 transmittance with its colour), brightest over the lamp; the pane cap holds the room's mean (at half its 0.24), not each pixel. A lit shop's surfaces (walls, floor, goods; not its fixtures' glow) show at 0.4 of the window-adapted level (shop_lights.rs `ROOM_GAIN`), so seen at the street's exposure the room sits about 1.5 stops over a lamp's pool rather than 3-4 (a pale wall clipped to white), the fixtures still blown. By day nothing changes. Shader code behind `SF_ROOM_DEPTH`; no tier sets it; `--no-room-depth` |
| `--lit-rooms` | With `--room-depth`, a lit home's light on the street lamps' photometric scale (eo-render rooms.wgsl `RD_HOME_LUX`; off by default, so the frame is as before): 60 lux on a surface its lamp lights from 1.5 m at 45 deg (a 75-100 W-equivalent lamp's ~40-55 lux and the room's inter-reflection; IES residential living spaces 50-100 lux), in the lamps' units per lux, instead of a fixed level the pane cap held near the lit walk's own (lit windows barely beat the white trim beside them); against the night meter's 9-lux walk a lit room's walls stand ~2.5-3.5 EV over it, its lamp's hot spot clipping, its corners under, its coverings glowing by the same light, the cap a soft shoulder at 0.5; it comes in as the night does (`night::nightness`: the lamps' units per lux are the night rig's), the old level by day. Shader code behind `SF_LIT_ROOMS`; no tier sets it; `--no-lit-rooms` |
| `--wire-light` | The street overhead's wires lit as the scene's surfaces are (eo-render props_wires.wgsl `pr_wire_lit`; off by default, so the frame is as before): the JS's unlit wire colour, which the night's exposure lifted into strokes brighter than the sky, becomes a weathered cable's albedo (the same colour) on a matte Lambertian cylinder: the visible half of its round face lit by the rig's fill and the environment (hemisphere, ambient and SH irradiance at the half's middle normal) and by the key and the rim (the half's mean of max(n.l, 0) weighted by its width on screen); no shadow and no street lamp (full-cutoff heads send none above them). At night a wire is a dark line against the city's sky glow, by day a dark grey cable. Shader code behind `SF_WIRE_LIGHT`; no tier sets it; `--no-wire-light` |
| `--lamp-units` | The street lamps as the city's units, not one dimmer (eo-sky `lamp_units`; eo-render look.rs `lamp_units`, sky_lights.wgsl, hlod_lights.wgsl, props_draw.wgsl `lu_factor`; off by default, so the frame is as before): the JS rule turned every lamp up together (1 - smoothstep(-3, 8, sun altitude): 9 % at the golden hour's +6 deg, so every lens burned in full sun and the far lamps drew a perfectly regular dotted line from the air). Each unit switches on, at full output, as the sky's horizontal illuminance (the clear sky's, `photo::clear_global_lux`, times the weather's dome brightness: earlier under cloud) falls under its own level, the levels log-uniform between the sky with the sun 1 deg up (~1,090 lux) and 2 deg down (189 lux): none on in sun, all by the blue hour (INFERRED: the city's lamps come on round sunset, unit to unit, ANSI C136.10's 1-3 fc photocontrol tolerance); each unit at its own output (0.8-1.2 of the design, IES RP-8 light loss), colour bin (+-175 K, ANSI C78.377) and 2 % failed. The frame's `lamps_on` becomes the share of units on, and each unit (the near gather's, the far sprites', a district light's lit glass) is on or off by its hash (a district light's from its post's foot, so its light and its lens agree). Shader code behind `SF_LAMP_UNITS`; no tier sets it; `--no-lamp-units` |
| `--luminaires` | The luminaires' own optics (eo-render props_lib `glow_parts`, `luminaire_sockets`, props_lib2, props_draw.wgsl `pr_lamp_glass`, sky_lights.wgsl, sky_fx.wgsl; eo-app props.rs; off by default, so the frame is as before): a district light's lamp stands at its GLB's lit glass (the `lamp_glass` primitives' parts: a twin post's two lanterns, each with half the post's light, a cobra head's lens), not at the JS head (the historic post's light hung on its shaft 1.15 m under its lanterns); the glass is drawn lit by the same intensity as the light the gather shades with (in the lamps' units, which carry the practicals' gain once): a lantern's clear panes at a tenth of its intensity over their projected area (the light their seeds, dirt and inner faces scatter; INFERRED), brightest level with the lamp, the housing's frame dark between them, a cobra head's flat lens at I(theta) / (A cos theta) by the full-cutoff tail (bright from below, dark from above); and the fx pass keeps its core over the fixture: over a lantern the lamp seen through its panes (a 0.06 m core, `sky::LANTERN_LAMP_RADIUS_M`, INFERRED, its energy kept, so the frame round it is seen), over a cobra head its lens's glare (a lens far brighter than the camera's bloom carries on its own). The light shadows, the pool and the bounce follow the sockets. Shader code behind `SF_LUMINAIRES`; no tier sets it; `--no-luminaires` |
| `--rear-lamps` | A lit vehicle's rear lamps as lights of the frame (eo-sky `lights::REAR_*`, `REAR_LOBE`; eo-render sky_lights.wgsl `lights_agents`, sky_light.wgsl, lit_fog.wgsl `nt_lobe`; off by default, so the frame is as before): two red lights (SAE red) standing just behind the body's rear face (from its bounds, so its own body and the road under it take none), each of the rear lamps' photometric lobe (SAE J585/J586 test points as two Gaussians up/down and to the side: 0.33 at 10 deg up or down, 0.3 at 20 deg aside, a few per cent at 45), the tail lamps at 10 cd (SAE J585) and the stop lamps at 120 cd while it brakes (SAE J586; the braking flag its drawn lenses light by), out to where they give 0.1 lux: red on the road behind, the kerb and the car behind, in the wet street's streaks and the lit haze along its beam; ranked with the shop windows in the clusters; no map, no sprite. With `--car-lamps` compiled in, the cars' rear lamps are that switch's (each body's own lamps) and these stand down, so no rear lamp is lit twice. Shader code behind `SF_REAR_LAMPS`; no tier sets it; `--no-rear-lamps` |
| `--signal-spill` | Each traffic signal's lit lens near the eye as a light (eo-sky `lights::SIGNAL_*`; eo-render sky.rs `set_signal_lights`, sky_lights.wgsl; eo-app props.rs; off by default, so the frame is as before): the vehicle heads within 250 m (at most 256), each its lit aspect's lens (the signal head GLB's lens centres) as a light along the head's face from 0.3 m in front of it, past its visor, of the signal's photometric lobe (eo-sky `SIGNAL_LOBE`: the ITE VTCSH 300 mm points as two Gaussians below and to the side, the peak 2.5 deg down, 0.5 at 12.5 deg, 0.12 at 22.5; the visor cutting it above: under 5 % by 8 deg up) at ITE VTCSH's 12-inch LED minimum on its axis (red 365, yellow 910, green 475 cd) in the ITE colour (brought into the sRGB gamut along its line to the white point, which keeps its hue: the green is the LEDs' blue-green, not the display's yellow-green primary), out to where it gives 0.25 lux and nothing within 4.5-6 m of the lens (a working head's face is in clear view along its approach, CA MUTCD 4D.12, so near the head its beam is clear). Its beam has no shadow, so it stays out of the clusters: it lights no surface and no haze (in them, unshadowed, it lit trunks, poles, sign backs and the head beside it, and glowed in front of a trunk hiding the head); it shows as its lens's image, a lens-sized streak in the wet street (the streak list). At night the lenses are the light's own: the lit lens its intensity toward the eye by the same lobe over its 300 mm area (blooming when it faces the eye, dim from the side, dark from behind), the others dark glass (the display-referenced lens by day, blended by the night). No sprite. Shader code behind `SF_SIGNAL_SPILL`; no tier sets it; `--no-signal-spill` |
| `--look default\|cinematic` | The look profile (the cinematic look plan, stage L0; eo-look `cinematic`, `LookOptions::look`, `SkyOptions::cinematic`, the shader constant `SF_CINEMATIC`): `default` is today's frame on its physical references; `cinematic` is a graded open-world look (punchier contrast, warm lights against cool shadows, a richer golden hour, mixed night lamp colours, wet reflections), built by stages L1-L7. Every value it takes that departs from a physical reference the default keeps is a named constant listed in `CINEMATIC_DEVIATIONS` (eo-look `cinematic.rs`) and in the bench JSON's `look_profile`. `high` and `hyper` turn it on; `--no-look` (the same as `--look default`) turns it off wherever it stands. At stage L0 nothing draws differently yet: the switch, the compile-out constant and the deviation table only |
| `--foliage-light` | Leaves pass light (eo-render props_draw.wgsl `pr_foliage`, hlod_trees.wgsl `ft_foliage`; off by default; its code is compiled out while off: the `// sf:FOLIAGE` blocks, eo-render look.rs `sf_select`, so the shaders are then the text they were before it): a leaf is a thin two-sided scatterer, so besides what it reflects on the face the eye sees (look_light) it passes the light on its far face through to the eye, nearly diffusely, at a transmittance that is its reflectance's pigment taken deeper (the mesophytic leaf's transmittance-to-reflectance ratio, about 0.55 red, 0.85 green, 0.3 blue: the light through a leaf is a more saturated yellow-green). The far face takes the sun (shadowed like the key light, thinned by the clouds, 30 % of it in a forward Henyey-Greenstein lobe of g 0.5) and the sky and ground light on that side (the ambient volume's sky visibility with `--ambient-volume`, the screen-space occlusion). The street trees' leaf cards and the palms' green parts take it, and the street's lamps too: the same lights' loop on the leaf's far face, with its transmittance as the diffuse, so a crown over a lamp glows a warm green from below (it replaces the older sun-only back-light term; in the rain their cuticle beads water whichever way they face: roughness down to 0.5, albedo 15 % darker; a leaf's shading normal is kept facing the eye, n.v at least 0.1); the far trees' crowns take it times the crown's own transmittance along the view's chord (exp(-2 z), z the crown normal's share toward the eye: a backlit rim, little through the middle). `--no-foliage-light` restores the frame |
| `--temporal-lod-fade` | The street props' level cross-fade (views 4 and 5; the blend narrowed to the middle 40 % of the band, so an instance shows one level whole over most of it) and the far trees' fade-in move their screen-door dither every frame (interleaved gradient noise stepped 5.588238 px a frame over 64 frames; props_draw.wgsl `pr_fade_test`, hlod_trees.wgsl), so the temporal anti-aliasing resolves an instance in its fade band to a blend of the two levels instead of a fixed stipple. Off by default, its code compiled out while off (the `// sf:LOD_FADE` blocks, `sf_select`); without the TAA (`--no-taa`) the pattern shimmers instead. `--no-temporal-lod-fade` restores the frame |
| `--turf` | The terrain's grass shaded as a canopy (landmarks_surface.wgsl `nt_turf`, called after the ground scans in draw.wgsl; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:TURF` blocks, `sf_select`): the ground tones the scans take as turf, understorey or dry grass have their albedo scaled by the RPV reflectance model fitted to grass canopies (Rahman, Pinty and Verstraete 1993: k 0.75, a backward Henyey-Greenstein phase of -0.15 and the hotspot, as a ratio to a Lambertian plane at 45 degrees of sun and view, applied by the sun's share of the light where the sun reaches it, its shadow taken: in a building's shade the grass takes the sky's ratio alone): brighter toward grazing and with the sun at the eye's back, darker looking into the sun; over it irrigation patches (41 and 13 m), clumps and tufts (2.2 and 0.7 m, faded by the pixel's footprint), dry and worn patches on mown lawns (about 15 %) and the mower's stripes (1.6 m along the street grid's axis, light where the blades lean away from the eye). `--no-turf` restores the frame |
| `--palm-detail` | The palms' trunk and crown (props_draw.wgsl `pr_palm`; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:PALM` blocks, `sf_select`). The trunk is a Canary Island date palm's lattice of cut leaf bases (10 round the trunk, each about 26 cm wide and 17 cm tall and its own size, on crossing spirals that waver round the trunk) drawn as a height field on the trunk's own coordinates: each base a raised boss whose slope bends the normal (the surface gradient from the screen derivatives), dark fibre and the sky's occlusion in the grooves, each base its own tone, weathered grey-brown below and fresh orange-brown cuts under the crown, splash at the foot, matte (roughness 0.92), faded to the lattice's mean tone where a pixel spans a base; it replaces the atlas's flat cross-hatch. The crown's leaflets and rachises keep the atlas's age bands (dead, yellowing, mature, young) but take measured leaf reflectances (a mature leaflet about 0.06 / 0.10 / 0.05 linear, luminance ~0.09, where the atlas's young band was twice that and the rachis three times), each leaflet (its v), frond (its bearing) and palm its own tone, some palms yellowing; the sky light falls with the leaf area over each point (Beer-Lambert, k 0.5: the crown's heart and the fronds under the upper ones darker, the tips open) and the leaflets' shading normals lean 30 % to the crown's outward normal and turn each its own way (lit from above, the underside dark), kept in the eye's hemisphere (n.v at least 0.1: no grazing glints off the crown's far side), with their spread folded into the roughness where a pixel spans several leaflets (Toksvig: alpha^2 + 2 x 0.07). With `--foliage-light` the leaflets pass light by the atlas (dead ones a third, the rachis little). `--no-palm-detail` restores the frame |
| `--hill-cover` | The far hills' vegetation (farshore.wgsl `fs_cover`; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:HILLS` blocks, `sf_select`): the land's tones become a mosaic with edges (woods in the folds and on the north faces, broken by fractal noise from 300 m down to the pixel, each octave faded where a pixel spans it; coyote brush and chaparral on the steep faces, in clumps and up gullies that run down the fall line; rock on the ridges and the steepest faces; grass drier on the south faces and greener in the swales), and the woods and brush are lit as crowns over grass by Li and Strahler's geometric-optical model (1992): a Boolean scatter of spheroid crowns (oaks 4.5 m across the radius, centres 7 m up; brush 1.2 m, 1 m), the sunlit and shaded crown and grass seen in the shares the sun's and the eye's paths give, with their overlap at the hotspot; the grass among the crowns sees less sky. Woods and brushy faces go dark and textured under a low sun and away from the hotspot. `--no-hill-cover` restores the frame |
| `--ground-cover` | The city's unbuilt hills and wild parks (the hills' tones of eo-render `ground2.rs`: dry summer grass through coyote brush to groves by the measured vegetation share) as a mosaic with edges (landmarks_surface.wgsl `nt_cover`, called after the ground scans in draw.wgsl; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:COVER` blocks, `sf_select`): golden grass (drier on the south faces), coyote brush in clumps and up the steep faces, eucalyptus, cypress and oak groves (more on the north faces), and Franciscan chert breaking through on the steep faces in thin folded red-brown beds; the mosaic is fractal noise from 48 m to the pixel round the tone's level, cut with an edge a pixel wide. Brush and groves are crowns over the grass: where a pixel resolves them each crown is drawn (spheroids in jittered cells: groves 7 m cells, 3.2-4.6 m radius, 2.5 m up; brush 2.4 m cells, 0.8-1.4 m) with its dome's sunlit and shaded sides, leaf clumps and the shadows the crowns cast along the sun's path; where a pixel spans them, Li and Strahler's geometric-optical shares with the hotspot (as `--hill-cover`); direct and sky light weighed by the sun's share at the pixel (the building shadow taken). The terrain's hill tones are built from a smooth field (40 and 14 m) instead of a jitter per 8 m vertex, so its triangles no longer show. `--no-ground-cover` restores the frame |
| `--bare-ground` | The ground the land cover left in its flat placeholder tones (eo-render `ground2.rs` `bare_ground`, landmarks_surface.wgsl `nt_bare`, hooked after the ground scans in draw.wgsl; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:BARE` blocks, `sf_select`): WorldCover's bare or sparsely vegetated ground (its lots, fills, works yards and the strips the paving leaves), the shore band's water and wetland pixels standing on land, and the greens mapped as bare earth (bare pitches, construction sites) are marked at their terrain vertices (alpha 254) and drawn as bare ground: compacted fines (dry, about 0.17 in luminance) with gravel worked in in drifts (each stone its own: greywacke, sandstone, red chert, serpentine, a little quartz; two grids of 5.5 and 3.7 cm), damp humus in the low spots, and ruderal weeds in clumps (dry wild oats and foxtail, some green mallow and dock), weedier with the measured vegetation share and along the margins where the ground meets the paving (an edge broken by noise, the fines spilled a little beyond it); near the eye the clumps stand up (the eye's ray marched down through their 0.2-0.5 m height, six steps) and cast their shadows along the sun's path; every scale fades to its mean where a pixel spans it. Unmapped ground on a bank steeper than 0.35 within 8-9 m of the shoreline and under 5 m over the sea (or at the waterline) is a revetment's riprap: metre blocks with rounded shoulders and dark voids, in the bay's zones by height over the sea (wet with sea lettuce and rockweed to about high water, acorn barnacles above it, black tar lichen in the spray, then dry stone with orange lichen and the gulls' streaks on the crest). The shore band's built and vegetated ground (`SHORE_KEEP_M`, 40 m) takes the inland tones (`open_ground`) instead of the placeholder palette. `--no-bare-ground` restores the frame |
| `--palm-variety` | Each palm its own (props_draw.wgsl `pr_palm_variety`; with `--palm-detail` only, its code compiled out unless both are on: the `// sf:PALM&PALM_VARIETY` blocks, `sf_select`): from hashes of where it stands, the crown's green (yellower and paler to bluer and darker, about 15 % either way), a skirt of grey-brown dead fronds under the live crown on half of them (from 0.4-1.4 model m under the crown's heart, by frond), orange date clusters among the lower leaf bases on about a third (opaque: no light through them with `--foliage-light`), the bark's own tone, and on one in five the lower 0.7-1.6 model m of the trunk skinned smooth by pruning (pale fibre, the cut bases' shallow rings); and each trunk's own lattice (9 to 13 leaf bases round, their height 0.82-1.3 times the 17 cm) broken by weathering: patches on the lower and middle trunk where the bases are worn down to the fibre (relief down 80 %, a weathered grey), rain's dark streaks running down from under the crown, and grey-green crustose lichen on one side. `--no-palm-variety` restores the frame |
| `--crown-light` | The far trees' crowns lit as what they stand for, a volume of leaves (eo-render hlod_trees.wgsl `ft_crown`; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:CROWN` blocks, `sf_select`, and a one-line hook in look_light.wgsl that lays no key on the crown's own normal): the card's spheroid crown is a turbid medium of leaves facing every way (Ross 1981; extinction 0.3 per metre: an open-grown city tree's leaf area density of 1.0, G 0.5, the leaves clumped on their shoots, Nilson's index 0.6), and the light leaving it toward the eye is the sun scattered by the leaves along the eye's ray, each lit through the leaves between it and the sun (Beer-Lambert over its path to the crown's surface, shortened at the hotspot), weighed by Ross's area scattering phase function for bi-Lambertian leaves (Shultis and Myneni 1988, its transmission term tau / 3 cos beta: bright with the sun behind the eye, dark when side-lit, and with the sun behind the crown only what the leaves pass, a thin rim), integrated over three depths along the ray; the sky's light at the medium's albedo (half the leaf's reflectance and transmittance, with the same multiple scattering as the sun's) through the leaves above (the zenith and four directions 35 degrees up). The leaf reflectance is the tree's tint pulled to a measured mature leaf's chroma (about 0.055 / 0.10 / 0.043 linear) and held to a measured leaf's luminance range (0.07-0.11, LOPEX93), each tree a little its own, in place of the bright lime tints. A far crown in shade goes a dark olive lit only by the sky through its top, a sunlit one keeps a lit side and a self-shadowed side; the light through it with `--foliage-light` is the phase function's. The street trees' leaf cards (props_draw.wgsl) take the same measured chroma (half way, their luminance kept: the cooked tints ran to a saturation of 0.7 on screen), and the sky light on each falls with the leaf area above it (Monsi and Saeki's vertical Beer-Lambert profile, k 0.5 over a leaf area index of 2, the height in the crown from the wind weight), on its far face too with `--foliage-light`. `--no-crown-light` restores the frame |
| `--far-palms` | The street tree survey's palms drawn as palms in the far layer (eo-render hlod_trees.wgsl `ft_palm`, hlod_trees.rs `FAR_PALM`; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:FAR_PALMS` blocks, `sf_select`): a column to the crown's heart and 24 fronds (a Canary Island date palm carries 50-80) round a heart dense with their bases, their leaflets spread 0.17 of the reach, the card at least 0.44 of the palm's height across (the street props' palm's reach, where the survey gives no crown), that rise and bend under their weight (each its own bearing and length, the oldest hanging, some dying straw), at the palm leaflets' measured tones, every width at least half a pixel; and the palm's card takes over only where the street props' palm fades out (396-450 m: the palm model's 450 m less its fade band) instead of from 270 m, where it drew a round, trunkless crown round every near palm. `--no-far-palms` restores the frame |
| `--prop-far-shadow` | The street props past the sun cascades (160 m of view depth, faded from 120 m) take the far sun map (`--far-shadows`), where they stood in full sun: their fragment shaders compile the cascades alone (Apple's compiler), so the map is sampled once a vertex in the props' vertex shader (eo-render props_draw.wgsl `pr_far_sun`; the map and the comparison sampler are then visible to the vertex stage) and read by look_light.wgsl `lk_shadow` past the cascades (`lk_far_vertex`). A street tree, palm, pole or lamp in a building's shadow down a canyon is in shadow. Off by default; its code compiled out while off (the `// sf:PROP_FAR` blocks, `sf_select`). `--no-prop-far-shadow` restores the frame |
| `--hill-relief` | The far hills' gullies and spurs under the height field's 40 m lattice (eo-render farshore.wgsl `fs_relief`; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:HILL_RELIEF` blocks, `sf_select`): V-shaped channels down the fall line with rounded spurs between them (value noise folded about its median, stretched 5:1 down the slope), at 160, 56 and 20 m across and 10, 4 and 1.5 m deep (side slopes of about 0.25 to 0.3), deeper on the steeper faces and none on the flats, each faded out where a pixel spans it; their slope bends the lattice's normal, so a low sun and the haze pick out a hillside's drainage, and the mosaic's north faces (`--hill-cover`) follow the gullies' walls. `--no-hill-relief` restores the frame |
| `--leaf-shade` | The street's lights through a crown's leaves (eo-render sky_light.wgsl `sky_leaf_t`, set by props_draw.wgsl; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:LEAF_SHADE` blocks, `sf_select`): a light with no shadow map of its own (a shop window, a street lamp's ground bounce with `--lamp-bounce`, a headlamp; a lamp with `--light-shadows` is cut off by the props in its map already) reaches a palm's or a street tree's leaf through the crown's leaf area on its path (Beer-Lambert over the segment's optical depth inside the crown). A Canary Island date palm's crown: some 40 live fronds of about 3 m2 each, all leaving the heart, so their leaf area per volume falls as 1 / r^2 (sigma 1.43 / r^2 per metre in model metres, the integral along the path closed form), plus the rachises' little uniform part, out to 5.2 model m; a street tree's: an open broadleaf crown (sigma 0.4 per metre) round 0.64 of its height. A palm over a lit shop front no longer glows through to its top, and the fronds facing away from a light fall dark. The light through a leaf (`--foliage-light`) takes the transmittance over reflectance of each display primary's band (0.8, 0.95, 0.35: LOPEX93's fresh broadleaf leaves over 580-640, 510-580 and 440-490 nm) instead of one wavelength each (0.55 at 650 nm sits in chlorophyll's trough): a backlit leaf under a warm lamp goes yellow-olive, not lime. `--no-leaf-shade` restores the frame |
| `--wet-foliage` | Rain on the leaves and the bark (eo-render props_draw.wgsl `pr_wet_leaf`, `pr_wet_bark`; with `--foliage-light`; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:WET_FOLIAGE` blocks, `sf_select`): a leaf's waxy cuticle does not soak, the rain stands on it as drops and a film over about half to all of each leaf (each leaflet, or 25 cm of a leaf card, its own share), whichever way the leaf faces (the city's wetting took a leaf seen from below as a soffit the rain does not reach): there the specular is the water's (roughness 0.22, so the street lamps glint in it), the leaf some 15 % darker under it (Lekner and Dorf 1988, a non-porous surface), the spread of the leaves' normals widening the lobe where a pixel spans several; a dead frond's straw soaks like bark. A palm's trunk and a street tree's bark soak: half as bright (Lekner and Dorf for a porous surface), a film on their raised parts (roughness 0.45), darker in the runs down the trunk. `--no-wet-foliage` restores the frame |
| `--lawn-albedo` | A mown lawn's tone held to a measured turf's luminance (eo-render landmarks_surface.wgsl `NT_TURF_LUM`, with `--turf`; off by default; its code compiled out while off: the `// sf:TURF&LAWN_ALBEDO` blocks, `sf_select`): Kentucky bluegrass and tall fescue turf reflect 0.10-0.13 at 550 nm, a luminance of 0.06-0.10 (Trenholm, Carrow and Duncan 1999; Bell et al. 2002), where the mapped lawn tones ran to 0.15 (the imagery's haze over a lawn read as its own), twice a tree crown's: the parks from the air were lime sheets round dark crowns. The tone before the scans is scaled into that range, its hue and the scans' detail kept. `--no-lawn-albedo` restores the frame |
| `--structures`, `--tunnels` | Grade-separated roads (`eo-structures`: freeways, ramps, road bridges) and, with `--structures`, their tunnels; off by default, no tier sets them. A world switch: it changes collision and traffic, so `eo-server` takes it too and the handshake refuses a client whose setting differs from the server's. With it the street is built again with the structures' joins (`eo_structures::build_joined`; eo-app and `eo-server` alike, so they stand on the same street): a structure end where a street passes through or meets a junction is a leg of that junction (no kerb across its mouth), a stub ending at a structure is capped, a short bridge's stub climbs an approach embankment to the height its clearance needs, and the Central Freeway's columns stand in 13th Street's overlapping parking lanes; the structures stand retaining walls where that street meets them (eo-structures `walls.rs`): a wall with a coping over each face the street draws round a leg's junction, a walled fill with a barrier where a stub is raised past what its bank carries, a concrete edge beam under a kerbless stub's edge over the ground; the kit tiles of that street cook into their own cache folder. The props keep clear of the decks (trees become a smaller species or go, signal masts become pedestals or go, sign posts move along the kerb, lamps, furniture, overhead poles and wires under a deck and parked cars on a column go) and the built freeway decks get barrier-mounted luminaires every 50 m on both edges. Drawn by the city pipeline (`eo-render` `structures.rs`): every 128 m tile is one LOD group of the street's kind, its far and coarse levels static and its near level streamed by distance like the street's; the deck is the street's asphalt, the concrete a light warm grey with form panels, board lines and stain streaks (the CC0 board-formed scan when present), casting sun shadows at the level shown. `--tunnels`: tunnels draw and collide: bores (roadway, raised walkways behind a kerb, walls and a vault lined in glossy small cream glazed tile in grout, a glowing linear luminaire along the crown, a centre wall between twin bores), approach cuts with retaining walls (with a coping; the stubs lowered into them walled too), portal headwalls with a coping and an arch round each bore's mouth (twin bores each their own, the centre wall kept); a building over an approach cut or a portal's canopy is left out (as a building a landmark claims); the terrain (`scene::build_scene_with_cuts`) and the collision heightfield leave out the ground the cuts go through and the portals' canopies; inside a bore neither the sun nor the open sky light anything (only the daylight a portal lets in, falling off over its first 12 m), its lamps every 8 m under the crown are lit round the clock (100 lux on the roadway by day, the approach road's level by night, 4000 K) with an even interreflection off the tile, by day brighter over the threshold zone in from each portal (CIE 88: about 6 times the interior over the first 30 m, falling to the interior about 80 m in), and the exposure adapts to the bore over its first 20 m. A car (or the player) inside a bore is lit as the bore's own surfaces are (eo-agents `feed::in_bore` marks it with `AGENT_IN_BORE` and its depth in from the portal; eo-render agents_draw.wgsl `agent_bore_fs`, pipelines of their own made only when the world has bores: the bore's lamps and headlamps, its interreflection, the threshold zone and the portal's daylight; no sun, sky, rim or environment; `EO_AGENTS_NO_BORE_LIGHT=1` lights them as before, a diagnostic). Without the switch nothing is reserved, attached or drawn and the frame does not change |
| `--cache DIR`, `--no-cache` | Cache folder, or cook everything fresh |

### Bench

| Switch | What |
| --- | --- |
| `--frames N`, `--warmup N` | Measured and warm-up frames per path (720 and 60) |
| `--segments a,b,...` | Which camera paths to run, for example `street_walk`, `elevated_sweep`, `city_overview`, `authored_street`, `kit_street`, `drive`, `turn_around`, `waterfront`, `bay`, `bay_dive`; with `--structures` and built decks also `freeway_drive` (1.5 m over a built Route 80 deck near the I-80 approach, 150 m) and `under_viaduct` (1.7 m on 5th Street under the I-80 approach, sweeping across the deck) |
| `--capture` | Also write PNGs of each path to `bench-out/` |
| `--structures-shots` | Instead of the timing, the grade-separated roads' capture card (`capture-structures-<label>-<name>.png` and a JSON list): the I-80 approach, the Central Freeway, Selby Street under I-280 and the Presidio Parkway, each from the street and from above, at 15:00 under the clear preset (weather seed 7); the poses are placed on the structures (built for the poses alone without `--structures`), so runs with and without them match; `EO_STRUCTURES_SHOTS=a,b` keeps some, `EO_STRUCTURES_SHOTS_HOUR=H` takes them at another hour, `EO_STRUCTURES_SHOTS_HDR=1` also writes each card's scene luminance (`.lum`: `EOLM`, width, height, f32 a pixel); `EO_STRUCTURES_SHOTS` naming them adds stage A8's cards: `join-leg-214`, `join-leg-215`, `join-leg-service` (a junction where a structure end is a leg, from the street and from above), `join-raised-306`, `join-raised-132`, `join-raised-203` (a raised stub's approach), `thirteenth` (the Central Freeway over 13th Street), `tunnel-macarthur-south`, `tunnel-macarthur-north`, `tunnel-broadway-west`, `tunnel-broadway-east` (`-out` 45 m out on the approach looking at the portal, `-in` 40 % into the bore looking back at it), placed on the street datum and on a tunnel world built for the poses, so runs with and without `--structures` / `--tunnels` match |
| `--light-shots` | Instead of the timing, the lighting reference cards (`capture-light-<label>-<name>.png` and a JSON list): an alley and a plaza at noon and at golden hour, the skyline and a hill's shadow at low sun, a parked row and a junction at night, under the I-80 viaduct at noon and at golden hour, a dusk strip of the kit street, and (`far-*`) the `city_overview` path's first frame and the `elevated_sweep` path's first and quarter frames at golden hour; `EO_LIGHT_SHOTS=a,b` keeps some, `EO_LIGHT_SHOTS_HOUR=H` draws every card at hour H; `EO_PROBE_STRIP=path[,frames[,m a frame[,turn]]]` instead walks a street (default `kit_street`, 120 frames of 0.5 m, looking 45 degrees across) one frame a step, reads every frame back and writes a CSV of the probes' finished captures and the largest change of a screen cell a frame, with the frames round each capture. Every capture card (`--capture`, the cards here) first waits for `--probe-grid`'s set to be captured; `EO_CAPTURE_SETTLE_PROBE=1` waits for the single probe's capture too (matched pairs). `EO_LIGHT_SHOTS_WEATHER=fog` (any `--weather` preset) draws every card under that weather instead of clear; `EO_LIGHT_SHOTS` naming `night-kit` adds `night-kit-street-0`, `-mid` and `-260` (the night_kit_street path's frames at night); `EO_LIGHT_SHOTS` naming `shops` adds `shops-castro-18th` (Castro Street from the south-east corner of 18th, looking up the block), `shops-castro-walk` (12 m up it on the east walk) and `shops-mission-24th` (Mission Street 12 m north of 24th on the east walk), at night; with `--shop-light` each card's JSON carries the frame's lights by kind and the clusters' fill (`shop_lights`); `EO_HEADLIGHT_STRIP=frames` (default 60) instead waits at the props card `junction`'s street for a car passing at over 5 m/s, stands 11 m beside its path and writes every frame of it crossing the view (`capture-light-<label>-headlights-fNN.png`, at night, fog unless `EO_LIGHT_SHOTS_WEATHER` says otherwise) with a JSON (`--lit-fog`'s cards; `EO_HEADLIGHT_STRIP_VIEW=behind` stands 9 m behind the car looking along its heading instead). With `--light-shadows` or `--headlight-shadows` each card's JSON carries the selection (`light_shadows`); `EO_LIGHT_SHADOW_ATLAS=1` writes the atlas as a grey PNG beside each card and `EO_LIGHT_SHADOW_MAPS_OFF=1` draws the maps but lets no light read them (a pair that shows the shadows alone). `EO_LIGHT_SHOTS=hero` draws the six key views of the cinematic look plan (`hero-castro-night`, `hero-mission-wet`, `hero-downtown-golden`, `hero-viaduct-golden`, `hero-embarcadero-blue`, `hero-aerial-golden`; bench `hero_views.rs`), each at its own hour, weather and sky seed with the sky's clock held at `--world-time` and the water's fixed while it settles, so two runs give the same frame; the viaduct card needs `--structures`; `EO_HERO_TUNE` tries other cameras |
| `--cc0-shots` | Instead of the timing, the CC0 scanned sets' matched cards (`capture-cc0-<label>-<name>.png`): the kit street at noon, golden hour and night, a sidewalk walk, one close wall per finish class, the kerb, the walk and a wheel path up close, plazas, seawalls, a beach and the city from above; `EO_CC0_SHOTS=a,b` keeps some, `EO_CC0_SHOTS_HOUR=H` draws every card at hour H (the `--grime` evidence is taken at 15) |
| `--clutter-shots` | Instead of the timing, the facade clutter's cards (`capture-clutter-<label>-<name>.png`): the kit and authored street paths at two frames, fronts the planner dresses at level 1 whatever the run draws (fire escapes in the Tenderloin, Nob Hill and Chinatown, shop fronts in the Mission, the Castro and Chinatown, a blade sign, an awning and the services up close, an escape from 110 m) and a roof from above, and (stage S1) lettered signs close up in the Castro and Chinatown and neon signs in shop windows in the Mission and the Castro, so a run with `--facade-clutter` and one without stand at the same places; `EO_CLUTTER_SHOTS=a,b` keeps some, `EO_CLUTTER_SHOTS_HOUR=H` draws every card at hour H (default 15) |
| `--dusk-strip` | Instead of the timing, the drawn hour moving at one camera (the kit street, sky in view): 40 frames a clock minute apart across sunset and 40 across sunrise (`capture-light-<label>-dusk-<nn>.png`, `-dawn-<nn>.png`), a CSV each of the mean luminance and its step from the frame before, and a JSON summary. With `--smooth-time` each frame is reached through the soft path, without it through one `--time-of-day`-style set a frame; `EO_DUSK_STRIP=dusk` or `dawn` runs one |
| `--out DIR`, `--label NAME` | Output folder and run label |
| `--check-budget` | Exit 1 if a path's median frame reaches the budget (10 ms; the `--quality` tier's when one is given) |
| `--check-max` | Exit 1 if any path's median, p95 or worst frame reaches the budget |
| `--photo-bench` | Every path through photo mode at the depth of field's heaviest (f/1.4 focused at 0.5 m); first the photo passes' own GPU time at each path's start with and without the depth of field, into `photo-cost-<label>.json` (and, with `EO_PHOTOS_DIR` set, one 2x capture) |
| `--worst-frames N` | How many of the worst frames to report |
| `--pace realtime\|60hz\|all-60hz\|none` | How the bench paces its frames |
| `--shots FILE.json --out DIR` | Offscreen stills instead of the timing: one PNG per shot of the file, and `DIR/<name>.json` beside it (below) |
| `--scene-cards --out DIR` | The eight scene cards of [the visual gate](../Docs/VISUAL_QUALITY_GATE.md) at the Ferry Building and The Embarcadero, 2560 x 1440 unless `--width`/`--height` are given (below) |
| `--clip FILE.json --out DIR` | Moving clips along camera paths: every frame drawn at 60 Hz of wall time, every `60 / fps`-th written as `DIR/<name>/NNNN.png` with `DIR/<name>.json`; each clip is `{"name", "seconds", "fps", "time_of_day", "fov", "keys": [{"t", "eye", "look_at"}]}` in game metres (`bench/clip.rs`) |
| `--shots-only a,b` | Only the shots or cards whose name contains `a` or `b` |
| `--settle-timeout S` | The most a shot waits for streaming to settle (600 s) |
| `--card8-seconds S`, `--card8-fps N`, `--card8-speed M` | Card 8's length (30 s), frames written per second (10) and speed (the player's run, 5.6 m/s) |

Neither needs `--bench`. Every world switch and kill switch applies, and
`--time-of-day` and `--weather` set the hour and weather of a shot that
does not give its own.

**Shots.** The file is a JSON array; each shot has a `name` (letters,
digits, `-`, `_`, `.`; it names the files) and one pose:

```json
[
  { "name": "transamerica_oblique",
    "geo": { "lat": 37.7952, "lon": -122.4028, "alt": 110, "range": 800, "heading": 30, "tilt": 75, "fov": 35 },
    "time_of_day": 13, "weather": "clear" },
  { "name": "embarcadero", "eye": [2830.9, 4.5, -2366.9], "yaw": 314, "pitch": -2, "fov": 52 },
  { "name": "salesforce", "eye": [2830.9, 4.5, -2366.9], "look_at": [2475.1, 160.0, -1627.9] }
]
```

- `geo` is the look-at pose of the common web-globe URL convention:
  (`lat`, `lon`, `alt`) is the target, `alt` in metres above mean sea level;
  the camera stands `range` metres from it; `heading` is the compass bearing
  the camera faces (0 north, 90 east); `tilt` is the angle from straight down
  (0 looks straight down, 90 at the horizon); `fov` is the vertical field of
  view in degrees. The camera is `target - range * forward` on the WGS84
  ellipsoid, `forward = (sin tilt sin heading, sin tilt cos heading, -cos tilt)`
  (east, north, up). The same keys at the top level of a shot are read as a
  `geo` pose too.
- `eye` with `yaw` and `pitch` (degrees; yaw 0 looks north, -z, and 90 east,
  +x; pitch up positive), or `eye` with `look_at`, are game metres (x east,
  y up, z south).
- Optional: `fov` (degrees, default 52, the game camera's), `far` (metres;
  default `--far` if given, else 20000 m so a view across the city is not
  cut short), `time_of_day`, `weather` (`clear`, `overcast`, `fog`,
  `drizzle`, `rain`, `storm`), `what`, `photo`, `subject` (below), `person`
  (`{"feet": [x, y, z], "heading": degrees}`: the player's body standing
  there, the simulated people beside it hidden; `eo-landmarks`'
  `access_shots` example writes one such shot per landmark deck), and keys
  starting with `_` (comments).
- `photo` renders the shot through photo mode's pipeline: `focal_mm` (35 mm
  equivalent; it sets the field of view), `f_number` (none: everything
  sharp), `focus` (metres, `"look_at"` or `"subject"`), `grade` (`neutral`,
  `warm-film`, `cool`, `monochrome`), `strength` (0 to 2 of the grade),
  `filter` (monochrome's contrast filter: `none`, `yellow`, `deep-yellow`,
  the default, `orange`, `red`, `green`), `fade`, `ev`, `vignette` and
  `grain` (0 to 1), `letterbox` (an aspect), `frame_offset` (-1 to 1: the
  letterbox opening from the top to the bottom), `roll` (degrees), `blades`
  (0: a circle, else 3 to 16; default 9), `rounded` (default true), `cat_eye`
  (default true) and `peaking`. `subject` stands the player's
  body in the shot: `at` [x, z] game metres on the drawn street, `facing`
  (compass degrees; default toward the camera), `model` (an index into the
  people models), `walking`. With `--no-photo-mode` a shot keeps the lens's
  field of view and the subject and nothing else of photo mode.
  Any other key is an error.

Latitude, longitude and height go to game coordinates through
`eo_data::geo`: the city's projection (`sf-city.json` `meta`: centre and
metres per degree) times the ground-plan scale (below), and the height as
height above the surveyed ground kept over the drawn ground (the elevation
grid is NAVD88; mean sea level is game y -0.55; the drawn ground lifts
heights above mean sea level by the height scale, and buildings and
landmarks stand on it at their real size). Both the target and the camera
are converted and the game camera looks at the target, so the bearing is
kept and the tilt and distance change with the plan scale; the manifest
records both.

Before each PNG is written the hour and weather are set with the cloud and
water clocks held still, the traffic is rebuilt round the shot from the
simulation's seeded warm-up and stepped 2 s, streaming settles for that view
(the upload queue empty and every tile, model, far cell and prop the view
wants resident, up to `--settle-timeout`), and the temporal resolve is drawn
to convergence under the still camera (two frames one jitter cycle apart
under 0.5 RMS in 8-bit levels, or a difference that has stopped changing
over three comparisons: what is left changes every frame, such as the post
pass's display dither; 48 to 320 frames). `DIR/<name>.json` records
the input, the resolved eye and orientation in game coordinates (and the
eye's latitude, longitude and height), the commit the binary was built from
and the checkout's state, the full command line, the size, the hour, the
weather, every switch, the traffic, how the settle and the convergence went,
and notes (an eye inside a building or under the ground, a view against a
wall, a settle that timed out).

**Scene cards.** `--scene-cards` takes the gate's eight cards at the Ferry
Building / Embarcadero corridor, each placed from the data (the street
plan's runs and junctions by name, the shore field, the Ferry Building's
anchor): 1 Market Street's footway at noon; 2 the painted junction on The
Embarcadero where Market Street meets it, across the crosswalk; 3 California
Street among the towers at 18.5 h; 4 the Embarcadero promenade over the bay;
5 The Embarcadero by the Ferry Building in drizzle at 16 h; 6 card 1's
street at 21 h; 7 a third-person character standing, walking toward and
stopped at The Embarcadero's kerb (7a, 7b, 7c) with the on-foot camera rig;
8 the promenade south-east of the Ferry Building at the player's run,
rendered every frame at 60 Hz of wall time with streaming and traffic
running, written as `DIR/card8-traversal/0000.png`, ... and placed so a
128 m tile boundary is crossed halfway (the frames that cross it are in
`card8-traversal.json`). Each card's manifest is the shots manifest plus
the card's number, category and place, and `honoured: false` with the
reason when the run cannot give what the card needs (no people without the
optional people assets, so no pedestrians and no character; no traffic with
`--no-agents`).

```sh
EO_NATIVE_CACHE=... ./target/release/eo-app --shots views.json --out shots-out --width 1600 --height 900 --time-of-day 13 --weather clear
EO_NATIVE_CACHE=... ./target/release/eo-app --scene-cards --out cards-out
```

### Swim the Pacific and San Francisco Bay

The [SF coast and Bay water production plan](../Docs/SF-OCEAN-PRODUCTION-PLAN.md) sets the scope for the playable region, the three reference swims and the frame-time checks.

Start the single-player game at Aquatic Park:

```sh
./target/release/eo-app --play --ocean-life --dive-site bay
```

Other starts are `black-point`, `pacific` (Ocean Beach), `channel` (Golden Gate),
`east-bay`, `south-bay`, `shelf` and the Mile Rocks entries `reef` and `kelp`. With `--ocean-life` alone, walk into the
water from the usual city spawn. Swim with the movement keys and mouse, hold
Space to rise, C or Ctrl to descend, and Shift for a faster stroke. Shallow
water returns to walking. The dive uses the single-player fixed physics step.

The terrain uses the city's NOAA CUDEM soundings and NOAA ETOPO 2022 regional
relief. Small-scale ridges, rock shapes, patches of eelgrass, macroalgae, fish
schools and soft-bottom animals are procedural habitat suggestions. Outside
the city, the relief represents a grid roughly 400 m across. The habitat window
follows the diver. The start locations are bookmarks throughout the same sea.
The switch also enables water optics and dive exposure unless explicitly disabled.

Run `./target/release/eo-app --play --ocean-reef --dive-detail --dive-site kelp` to enter the
Pacific kelp habitat. The additional rock outcrops, colonies and schooling fish
use the licensed procedural mesh generators credited in the third-party notices.
The kelp grows on the modeled hard bottom within the lit coastal depth range.
These are habitat reconstructions. To rebuild the seven source forms and the
detailed coastal variants using the pinned sources and original plant models, run `node scripts/blender-ocean/cook-reef.mjs` at the repository root.

The marine library comes from the original Blender habitat study. Restore the
locked source with `npm run assets:pull -- ocean/sf-habitats` at the repository
root, then rebuild the game mesh and its digest with the following command:

```sh
blender -b artifacts/ocean/sf-habitats.blend --python-exit-code 1 --python scripts/blender-ocean/export_habitat.py
```

### Kill switches

Every visual feature can be turned off from the command line, so a change
can be compared with and without it (A/B) and a fault can be isolated. A
selection:

| Area | Switches |
| --- | --- |
| Content | `--no-models`, `--no-kit`, `--no-streets`, `--no-props`, `--no-agents`, `--no-landmarks`, `--no-water`, `--no-farshore`, `--no-sky` |
| Traffic lanes | `--traffic-street-plan` previews paved lane alignment in local play, captures, benchmarks, `--connect local`, and on a dedicated server. `--no-traffic-street-plan` restores the mapped road profile. Off by default. |
| Traffic bodies | `EO_TRAFFIC_MODEL_FOOTPRINTS` points to a Traffic Lab scale report for the game's current vehicles. Local play and the authoritative server then drive with the measured footprints. `--no-traffic-model-footprints` disables the preview. [Traffic Lab](../Docs/TRAFFIC-LAB.md) |
| Traffic passing | `--no-traffic-passing` turns off the paved-plan fleet's midblock passing. With the preview active, vehicles can signal and change into an adjacent clear same-direction lane across a broken white divider. |
| Street geometry | `--no-pavement-repairs` restores the earlier street surfaces, corner banks, distant junctions and service-drive entrances for offline comparisons. The repaired entrances fit the driveway to the receiving pavement and open the kerb and retaining wall across the mapped drive. |
| Surveyed junctions | `EO_SURVEYED_JUNCTION` loads a local surveyed pavement file for offline testing. Compare against the original street mesh with `--no-surveyed-junction`. [Traffic Lab](../Docs/TRAFFIC-LAB.md#preview-surveyed-junction-pavement) |
| Coastal plant communities | `--coastal-habitat` includes the reef and dive optics, with bull-kelp crowns at exposed rocks, eelgrass shoots on sheltered sediment, and broken whitecaps seen from below. Add `--no-coastal-habitat` to an `--ocean-reef --dive-detail` run for the former plants. |
| Rocky reef | `--rocky-reef` includes the coastal habitat with individual kelp forms, light filtered through the canopy, and partly buried ledges and boulders. Compare with `--coastal-habitat --no-rocky-reef`. With the reef enabled, `--no-reef-kelp`, `--no-reef-canopy-light` and `--no-reef-rocks` disable the three additions independently. |
| Reef surfaces | `--reef-materials` adds rock, damp sand and coarse beach sand scans to the rocky reef. With `--rocky-reef`, `--no-reef-materials` restores the previous rock and sediment shaders. |
| Submerged daylight | Add `--dive-daylight` to `--coastal-habitat` or `--rocky-reef` for wavelength-dependent scattered light, depth-filtered caustics and softer kelp shadows. `--no-dive-daylight` restores the previous underwater lighting on the same reef. |
| Underwater close detail | Add `--dive-detail` for camera-relative water rays, regional underwater light absorption, depth-filtered sunlight and moving reef blades. `--no-dive-detail` restores the previous dive. Use with `--ocean-reef` for the kelp and schooling fish. |
| Pacific kelp and reef forms | `--ocean-reef` includes the mapped marine habitats. Add `--no-ocean-reef` to an `--ocean-life` run for the original Blender habitats alone. |
| Mapped marine habitats and swimming | `--ocean-life` enables the surveyed seafloor, regional light transport, fish, plants and seabed animals. `--no-ocean-life` restores the previous water. |
| Water optics | `--water-optics` enables the preview, `--no-water-optics` restores the original water shading |
| Underwater habitats | `--underwater-habitats` adds the Bay and Pacific beds. `--no-underwater-habitats` removes them |
| Aquatic Park Cove | `--cove-detail` enables the beach profile, cobbles, pier parapet, distant wooded slope, fair-weather clouds and cove optics. `--no-cove-detail` restores the previous scene. The Bay dive-site starts enable the preview. |
| Black Point canopy | `--cove-canopy` enables the cove preview and resolves bark, branches and leaf cover from eight views of each park tree species. `--no-cove-canopy` restores the previous distant crowns. |
| Submerged cove sediment | `--cove-sediment` enables the cove preview with rough submerged sand and silt. `--no-cove-sediment` restores the previous seabed material. |
| Municipal Pier pile contact | The cove preview fits each support to the drawn seabed. `--no-cove-pile-contact` restores the previous fixed pile ends, and `--cove-pile-contact` enables the preview. The park describes the pier’s [piled construction and protection against scour](https://www.nps.gov/safr/learn/historyculture/aquatic-park-pier.htm). |
| Black Point intertidal | `--dive-site black-point` starts beside barnacle-covered rock and small algae at the west end of Aquatic Park beach. `--black-point-intertidal` adds this habitat to the map. `--no-black-point-intertidal` hides its geometry. The location follows the [National Park Service account](https://www.nps.gov/safr/blogs/a-hidden-community-in-aquatic-park-cove.htm). Individual rocks and organisms are modeled. |
| Black Point rockweed | `--black-point-rockweed` models forked blades with raised midribs and thickened tips on the rocky habitat. `--no-black-point-rockweed` uses the original ribbons. Blade margins transmit more backlight than the thicker rib. The geometry follows the form described by [Stanford’s Hopkins Marine Station](https://seanet.stanford.edu/brown) and the habitat reported by a [central Bay shoreline survey](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/8jt3v8mk). Individual plants are illustrative. |
| Cove daylight | `--cove-daylight` uses one depth-dependent bed-light model on both sides of the waterline. `--no-cove-daylight` restores the prior surface and underwater lighting. The regional reference is the median of 102 downwelling-light profiles at USGS station 20, about 2.9 km from Aquatic Park. The broadband coefficient is measured, while the RGB balance and caustics are modeled. [Measurements and attribution](../THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md#san-francisco-bay-underwater-daylight). |
| Dive exposure | `--dive-exposure` compensates for the loss of downwelling light at the eye. `--no-dive-exposure` restores the original exposure |
| Golden Gate Marin approach | `--golden-gate-finish` enables the Marin steel towers, landing survey, deck asphalt and median joints in play and capture modes. For the complete surveyed crossing, start `./target/release/eo-app --play --drive-golden-gate --structures --tunnels --quality high --street-pbr --street-wear --urban-sky --golden-gate-surfaces --golden-gate-net --golden-gate-floorbeams --golden-gate-pylons --golden-gate-pylon-silhouette --distant-bridge-decks --tunnel-rock-clearance --yerba-buena-section --golden-gate-pavement --golden-gate-wheel-wear --bridge-wire-joints --tunnel-portal-exposure --presidio-tunnel-finish` from `native/`. The car starts on the south approach facing Marin. `--no-golden-gate-finish` disables the candidate finish. |
| `--golden-gate-surfaces` / `--no-golden-gate-surfaces` | Use the surveyed Golden Gate lamp stations and amber optics, the curved approach alignment, the registered near and distant Marin aerial imagery, and the rain film on the scanned epoxy-asphalt course. The off switch restores the previous finish when used with `--golden-gate-finish`. The terrain uses the current map extent. |
| `--golden-gate-net` / `--no-golden-gate-net` | Show the installed safety mesh and its cantilevers below the Golden Gate sidewalks, with the vertical fence at the north anchorage. Enabled by the surveyed bridge finish. |
| `--golden-gate-floorbeams` / `--no-golden-gate-floorbeams` | Build the transverse plate girders, knee braces, deck bearings and longitudinal steel deck ribs beneath the three suspended Golden Gate spans. Off by default. |
| `--golden-gate-pylons` / `--no-golden-gate-pylons` | Fit the eight concrete approach shafts and their stepped crowns to the 2023 USGS laser scan. Off by default. |
| `--bay-bridge-pavement` / `--no-bay-bridge-pavement` | Use the existing scanned concrete aggregate on the [western Bay Bridge polyester-concrete road decks](https://dot.ca.gov/-/media/dot-media/district-4/documents/d4-transportation-planning-local-assistance/system-regional-planning/tcrs/f0020234_draft-final-i-80-sfobb-tcr-final-091917-a11y.pdf). Only the exposed upper roadway receives rain. Disabled by default. |
| `--bridge-deck-markings` / `--no-bridge-deck-markings` | Shade the Golden Gate and western Bay Bridge lane lines with the same scanned paint wear as the highway markings. Rain changes the paint on the exposed decks. The lower Bay Bridge roadway stays sheltered. Disabled by default. |
| `--distant-bridge-decks` / `--no-distant-bridge-decks` | Draw the real roadway panels, median, lane paint and open side trusses on the distant Golden Gate and Bay Bridge west spans. Both Bay Bridge road levels keep their own elevations. From `native/`, `cargo run --release -p eo-landmarks --example bridge_deck_continuity -- --distant-bridge-decks --golden-gate-surfaces` checks every contact face against both rendered models, including winding. Off by default. |
| `--golden-gate-pylon-silhouette` / `--no-golden-gate-pylon-silhouette` | Preserve the surveyed crowns and sidewalk openings when the Golden Gate changes to its distant model. Enables the surveyed pylons. Off by default. |
| `--golden-gate-pavement` / `--no-golden-gate-pavement` | Use the installed clean asphalt scan across the epoxy-asphalt deck and blend wear near the two Marin expansion joints. The shipped asphalt is the fallback when the optional scans are unavailable. Off by default. |
| `--bridge-wire-joints` / `--no-bridge-wire-joints` | Fit the screen coverage of suspension wires to their filtered diameter so short loops and clamps join the hanging ropes at driving distance. Off by default. |
| `--yerba-buena-lighting` / `--no-yerba-buena-lighting` | With the measured island tunnel section, draw paired lights along the westbound vault and eastbound sidewalls. Each pair shares the original light output. The layout follows the [upper-deck photograph](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Yerba_Buena_Island_tunnel.jpg) and [lower-deck photograph](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:Lower_deck_(5947642641).jpg). Off by default. |
| `--east-span-pavement` / `--no-east-span-pavement` | With structures enabled, use epoxy asphalt between the suspension deck hinges and polyester concrete on the two elevated Skyway roads. The mapped Oakland approaches keep their existing paving. [Skyway specification, p. 13](https://files.mtc.ca.gov/library/pub/30903.pdf#page=14) · [Suspension paving](https://mtc.ca.gov/news/bay-bridge-east-span-paving-self-anchored-suspension-span-under-way) · [Hinges, Appendix F](https://files.mtc.ca.gov/library/pub/28602.pdf) · [Skyway length, Fig. 1.1-1](https://files.mtc.ca.gov/library/pub/28860.pdf). Off by default. |
| `--east-span-lighting` / `--no-east-span-lighting` | With structures enabled, use the surveyed median standards on both Skyway carriageways east of pier E2. The roadway optics face in the direction of travel. [Lighting reference](https://files.mtc.ca.gov/library/pub/non/47654_12.pdf). Off by default. |
| `--yerba-buena-vault-finish` / `--no-yerba-buena-vault-finish` | With the measured island section and lighting enabled, shade the upper concrete lining along the circular section and wrap its material over the crown. Off by default. |
| `--tunnel-mouth-lighting` / `--no-tunnel-mouth-lighting` | Carry the shadowed exterior lighting across the tunnel portal onto the pavement, markings and walls. Its contribution falls with the visible opening. See the [FHWA Lighting Handbook, Tunnel Lighting](https://highways.fhwa.dot.gov/sites/fhwa.dot.gov/files/2023-05/FHWA-Lighting-Handbook_0.pdf). Disabled by default. |
| `--tunnel-portal-exposure` / `--no-tunnel-portal-exposure` | Meter the entrance and transition zones using the local tunnel lighting at the camera. Applies inside vehicle tunnels with `--tunnels`. Off by default. |
| `--no-tunnel-relief-cuts` | Restore the uncut high-resolution hillside over vehicle tunnel portals for comparison. With `--tunnels`, both terrain detail levels follow the excavated road by default. |
| `--no-tunnel-plant-clearance` | Restore plants rooted in excavated ground at vehicle tunnel approaches for comparison. Ground over enclosed tunnels can support plants. |
| `--tunnel-rock-clearance` / `--no-tunnel-rock-clearance` | Keep scanned rock faces that reach into a vehicle tunnel or its approach out of the traffic lanes. Uses the swept three-dimensional roadway clearance with `--structures --tunnels`. |
| `--yerba-buena-section` / `--no-yerba-buena-section` | Use the surveyed westbound vault, eastbound flat soffit, and intermediate deck at the island tunnel approaches. Heights follow [WSP figure 14 and table 3](https://catc.ca.gov/-/media/ctc-media/documents/programs/baseline-agreements/2025-10/04-2y780-baseline-agreement-new.pdf#page=54). |
| `--presidio-tunnel-finish` / `--no-presidio-tunnel-finish` | With `--tunnels`, use the box section, concrete lining and rows of individual ceiling fixtures on the Presidio Parkway. [SFCTA cross sections](https://www.sfcta.org/sites/default/files/2020-10/Presidio_Parkway_Final_Environmental_Impact_Statement_Report-Appendices.pdf) and [MTC interior photographs](https://mtc.ca.gov/news/presidio-parkway-construction-progress). Off by default. |
| `--golden-gate-wheel-wear` / `--no-golden-gate-wheel-wear` | Vary the exposed aggregate and roughness along the wheel paths of the six surveyed bridge lanes. Enables the deck wearing course. Off by default. |
| Bridge detail | `--bridge-detail` enables the Golden Gate detail preview (a darker paint tone, ribbed tower legs, framed portal struts, full arch pylons, lamp standards), each part drawn only where the bridges' own passes (Bridges, below) leave it as exported; left out, the preview is off. `--no-bridge-detail` is not its off form: it is the bridges' own detail switch (Bridges), so with both the preview's legs and portal struts stand in for that pass |
| Landmark decks | `--no-landmark-access` (the decks, their lifts and the tower bands and tower tops they take over; the base bridge sidewalk and harbour floors still support the player; the rooms' camera meter and the player's deck occlusion, whose shader terms compile out, `SF_LANDMARK_ACCESS`) |
| People | `--no-rocketbox`, `--no-rocketbox-motions` (either one: no people), `--no-crowd`, `--no-people-shading`, `--no-people-bounce`, `--no-people-contact`, `--no-people-motion`, `--no-people-evening`, `--no-people-windows`, `--no-people-stagger` |
| Demo player avatar | `--no-test-character` |
| Driver-door animation | `--no-car-door-animation` |
