| Props | `--no-props2`, `--no-furniture`, `--no-eztree`, `--no-wires`, `--no-overhead`, `--no-lamp-sprites`, `--no-stand-ins`, `--no-surveyed-furniture`, `--no-tree-basins`, `--no-tree-crowns` (with `--tree-crowns`: the trees past 70 m as before), `--no-yard-lidar-trees` (with `--yard-lidar-trees`), `--no-yard-detail`, `--no-yard-plants`, `--no-yard-objects`, `--no-yard-stairs`, `--no-yard-wood`, `--no-furniture-models` (a furniture kind whose GLB is not in the checkout draws as a 0.3 m box at its height, not its model drawn in code), `--no-street-props-v2` (the v1 street-prop files the JS lists, drawn as proxies or stand-ins where the checkout lacks them, as before the v2 models), `--no-shared-poles` (a street light beside a Muni trolley support pole stands its own post again, beside the pole, instead of standing on the pole's spot and holding its wires as one standard: eo-props `overhead::Overhead::lamp_moves`, after the SF Better Streets Plan's 'street lighting, traffic signal, and MUNI catenary poles should share poles wherever possible'), `--no-joint-dedupe` (where two pieces of one street meet end to end with no junction, both pieces stand their end trolley support station and their distribution poles again, side by side, instead of one station and the measured lower-quartile distribution spacing across the joint: eo-props `overhead::set_joint_dedupe`), `--poster-hoardings` (draw the furniture walk's poster hoardings again, as before they were taken out: about 970 plywood boards with flat-toned bills, stood off the building line in front of shopfronts and level against the slope; off by default, the walk still plans them so every other item stands where it did: eo-props `furniture::set_poster_hoardings`), `--no-sign-models` (the SFMTA sign plates draw the plates built in code again, instead of each inventory face's Blender model, `public/assets/street-signs/<face>-v1*.glb` built by `scripts/assets/build-street-signs-v1.py` from the official sign art: the face on a weathered aluminium blank with its bolts, saddle brackets and post bands, three levels to 25, 90 and 180 m; eo-props `sign_models`, eo-render `props_lib2::SIGN_MODEL_BASE`), `--no-sign-post-merge` (every SFMTA inventory post stands its own pole again: the inventory lists one physical pole's signs under several records, often at one point, and each stood a post a few centimetres from the others, one in front of another's plates; otherwise each plate draws one of three wear variants by a hash of where it stands, 60 / 25 / 15 in 100: aged, rusty (rust bled from the bolts, bands and saddles, run-off staining, corroded edges, rusted hardware), battered (a corner creased, knocked crooked on its pole, sheeting peeled to the aluminium, dents, marker scribbles); the plates of one pole wear alike; eo-props `sign_models::wear_of`), `--no-signal-models` (the vehicle signal heads draw the v2 head again and the pedestrian heads the box with its two lit halves, instead of the modelled McCain heads, `public/assets/street-signals/` built by `scripts/assets/build-street-signals-v1.py` on the geometry of two CC BY 4.0 models (credited in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md): the 12 in three-section head on a louvred backplate with its yellow border, its tunnel visors cut away underneath as SF's are (a slanted cut from the visor's bottom at the door to a chord at half its radius at the mouth, so a lens shows from below), the visors' and the lenses' rings rounded from the source's 16 segments to 32 (the new points on the circle, so a visor's mouth and cut edge read round, not faceted), the backplate closed behind the housing (the sky showed through slots down both sides of the head), its LED lenses domed (27 mm) with their faces turned out of the head, lit through a drawn LED module pattern and unlit as tinted glass over the module; the 16 in countdown head's face drawn as LED arrays under an egg-crate visor's diagonal lattice, its upraised hand and walking person lit through their masks; eo-props `signal_models`), `--no-signal-light` (each lit lens and pedestrian symbol is the display value it was solved to again, the same disc by day and night from any side, instead of a light: its luminance (eo-sky `SIGNAL_CD` over the 300 mm lens, ITE VTCSH 12-inch minima; `PED_SIGNAL_NIT`, ITE PTCSI Part 2 minima, hand 1400 and person 2200 cd/m2) toward the eye by the module's lobe about the head's facing (`SIGNAL_LOBE`, never under a modelled wide scatter) in the frame's units (daylight's by day, the lamps' at night), through the LED module's emitters (under the camera model's look its whole radiance: at night a white-hot core in a red halation; under the default display chain rolled off like a camera's highlights, a red at night a saturated pink-red disc, not white paint); the lens and face glass reflecting the sky where the ray leaves the visor through its mouth or its cut-away underside (strong at grazing angles; from elsewhere and from the sky a quarter of it, so in shade at street distance it stands near the black backplate); the inside of a modelled visor occluded by its own walls (its ambient light and its reflections through the mouth and the cut-away only: at grazing angles it reflected the sky); and the lenses' chromaticities as before, the green a yellow-green outside its ITE box; eo-render `props::set_signal_chroma`, `SL_VISOR_HOLD`, eo-sky `SIGNAL_XY`, standards `signal-lens-chromaticity`), `--no-signal-unlit` (the unlit LED module's albedo whole again, ~5 x the black housing at 30 m by day where photographs show it under it; `SL_VISOR_HOLD_TOP`), `--no-signal-bounce` (the screen-space bounce takes a lit lens and visor at a quarter of the white again, tinting its own backplate; `SL_AO_CAP_HELD`), `--no-signal-poles` (the signal poles and mast arms draw the tapered cylinders built in code again, with no mounts, instead of the Blender models in `public/assets/signal-poles/` built by `scripts/assets/build-signal-poles-v1.py` from Caltrans RSP ES-7C's base plate, anchor bolts and arm connection and the game's signal-hardware constants: the mast pole on its base plate, levelling nuts and anchor bolts over a round foundation, its handhole away from the arm and its cast cap; eo-props `signal_poles`, eo-render `props_lib2::SIGNAL_POLE_BASE`), `--no-signal-pole-variants` (every signal pole and its hardware painted; otherwise each takes the finish of the 48 m cell it stands in, painted or galvanised, half each: eo-props `signal_poles::finish_at`), `--no-arm-rise` (the mast arms stay level at the planned arm height, as before; | Bridges | `--no-bridges2` (all of these), `--no-bridge-scale`, `--no-bridge-paint`, `--no-bridge-detail`, `--no-bridge-wires`, `--no-bridge-night`; the second pass: `--no-bridges3` (all of its), `--no-bridge-air`, `--no-bridge-finish`, `--no-bridge-members`, `--no-bridge-floods`, `--no-bridge-curtain`; `--no-bridge-wire-sky` (the wire overlay reads its aerial perspective's sky colours per pixel, as before, instead of per vertex) |
| Harbours | `--no-floating-docks` (no floats along the harbours' pier lines, and so no berths or boats), `--no-boats` (the floats without the moored boats; `--no-props2` also leaves them out), `--no-boat-motion` (the boats at rest on the still water, as before they floated on the drawn sea) |
| Buildings | `--no-facade-detail`, `--no-facade-age`, `--no-rooms`, `--no-room-frames`, `--no-room-dim`, `--no-room-lit-runs`, `--no-tower-night` (a far tower's lit floors as offices: ceiling glow and fixtures over the desks, tenants' own lamps, cleaners' floors, blinds), `--no-tower-crowns` (a tall tower's crown at night: a faint glow over its top storey and LED lines at the parapet), `--no-roof-colours`, `--no-roof-plant`, `--no-roof-parapet`, `--no-roof-patches`, `--no-roof-neutral`, `--no-wall-paint`, `--no-period-paint`, `--no-wall-chroma`, `--no-facade-typology`, `--no-room-blind-runs`, `--no-pane-transmittance`, `--no-far-facade`, `--no-far-roofs`, `--no-facade-glass`, `--no-far-facades`, `--no-facade-weather`, `--no-canyon-glass`, `--no-far-relief`, `--no-facade-patina`, `--no-window-depth` (the last seven opt-in, off by default) |
| Landmarks | `--no-landmarks2`, `--no-towers2` (the five landmark buildings as the JS export's generators built them), `--no-skyline` (the named skyline towers back to the kit), `--no-towers3` (the landmark buildings' and skyline towers' third pass off: the lit band in place of the projector floodlights, the previous glazing, night windows and crown, base truss, Coit shaft, City Hall granite and Sutro lattice, and the groves), `--no-grove` (the tree canopy of Pioneer Park round Coit Tower and of Mount Sutro round Sutro Tower off), `--no-towers4` (the fourth pass off, the third's output: Salesforce's crown fins, sunshades and perforated panels with Day for Night's walkers, the facades' fin and reveal sun shadows, spandrel and pier finishes, 555 California's carnelian, the coated-aluminium spires and braces, City Hall's gold leaf and drum floods, and the groves' summit clearing, species forms, forest floor and Coit loop lamps) |
| Ground | `--no-ground-hills`, `--no-ground-green`, `--no-ground-contact`, `--no-ground-beach-fit` (the sand round a mapped beach spreads as the JS rule does: over lawns and paving mapped beside it and along a seawall's quay), `--no-terrain-lod` |
| Street ground | `--no-street-ground` (eo-street `ground`, `EO_STREET_GROUND=0`): the ground round the streets as before, the 8 m carpet held at the lowest street centreline within a cell of each vertex. With it (the default) each near street tile draws its ground on a 2 m lattice (`ground_overlay`, a terrain object of the street pack): the surveyed ground, held under a street's paved corridor (its carriageway and the walk on that side) and under a junction's pad (the hull of its approach mouths, 1 m out; a junction under its approaches' levels blended by distance, 0.25 m down), drawn where it stands over the 8 m carpet. The lattice is not drawn under the paving: a vertex next to it is moved onto the paving's edge, meeting a retaining wall's top, a bank's foot or the walk at grade there (a gap narrower than the lattice between two pavings is filled, lowered at most 0.15 m under them; a sliver between two pavings at two levels, every lattice vertex round it under one or the other, is filled from edge to edge, its vertices moved onto both pavings' edges, or lowered at most 0.6 m: the 8 m carpet showed through it, a pit up to a metre and a half deep). Open ground is shaded as the terrain tiles shade it (their normals over a survey cell, and their own vertex colour and normal w byte, the surface materials' codes and grass share, interpolated in their triangles); plants, park trees and the furniture stand on it; the street's banks and retaining faces meet it, the player collides with it. `--no-cut-slopes` (`EO_CUT_SLOPES=0`): the ground behind a walk rises straight to the survey, on a retaining wall the whole height; with it (the default) it rises from the walk's back edge at most 1V:1.5H over 9 m, a graded cut slope, the wall only the rest. `--no-street-islands` (`EO_STREET_ISLANDS=0`): a small piece of open ground the road's paving encloses (a traffic circle's island, a pork chop by a slip lane) is drawn flush with the ground round it, as before; with it (the default) it is raised one kerb face over a plane through the paving round it and closed by a kerb face, its outline traced from the paving's own edge (0.5 m marching squares, the crossings on the edge itself; an island that is not one simple outline stays on the 2 m lattice); a small piece of open ground with less road round it (a gore between two walks at an acute corner, a strip between a walk and a slip lane, up to 12 m across) is filled flush with the paving round it. It costs about a quarter more time building a street tile's ground (on the streaming threads). `--no-wall-coping` (eo-street `mesh::wall_coping`, `EO_WALL_COPING=0`): a retaining wall behind a walk is a bare face up to the ground, as before; with it (the default) a wall rising over 0.35 m stands 0.10 m proud of the ground it holds, capped by a 0.30 m coping and closed behind. Measured by `cargo run --release -p eo-street --example street_scan` |
| Junctions | `--no-junction-ground` (eo-street `mesh::junction_ground`, `EO_JUNCTION_GROUND=0`): a junction's pad is one flat fan from its approaches' mean datum and its corner returns blend their datums in a straight line from mouth to mouth, as before; on (the default) the pad's fan is subdivided every 4 m along its spokes and both stand on the ground (the survey filtered over 4 m: no crease of its cells, a valley's sag drawn round) plus the road lift, offset to meet each mouth exactly (an acute fork's pad, cut back tens of metres, was one flat slab across its slope); the fan stands on a point its whole ring can be seen from (its kernel's centroid), and a ring seen whole from no point (concave round its corners, one approach cut back far past the others) is ear-clipped and each ear subdivided instead: fanned from the node, 2,255 of the city's 10,521 pads folded over themselves, two pad surfaces one over the other (930 whose ring crosses itself keep the node's fan). `--no-walk-yield-strict` (eo-street `plan::walk_yield_strict`, `EO_WALK_YIELD_STRICT=0`): a run's walk and carriageway no longer give way on a junction's pad the run is not an approach of, nor a junction's corner return on a neighbouring junction's pad (or its walk 1.5 m inside its own: an approach trimmed short, the walk across the cross street's roadway), a walk keeps the junction guard over any lesser road's mouth, a run crossing an earlier one with no junction drawn there (more than 45 degrees off it) keeps its span over the other's carriageway, the two coplanar and fighting, and a walk whose outer edge alone lies on another run's carriageway (a divided street's median-side walk, wider than its median) is kept, a slab a kerb over those lanes, as before. Measured by `street_scan` (its junction and stacked paving rows) |
| Road seams | `--no-road-seams` (eo-street `seams`, `EO_ROAD_SEAMS=0`): every run is drawn whole at its own datum (the survey under its own centreline), as before: where two carriageways overlap (a slip road beside its freeway, a divided road's two carriageways mapped closer than their widths) both are drawn, one up to 0.9 m over the other, and two runs meeting end to end with no junction end square to their own headings (a wedge of ground on the outside of the bend, an overlap inside). On (the default), a run whose carriageway overlaps a busier run's alongside it (within 35 degrees, busiest first: class, width, seed) draws only its part outside the busier one, with no kerb, walk or bank on that side, and its datum is moved so its surface meets the busier one's along the seam (as is a run whose edge comes within 0.75 m of a busier one's); the move fades out along the run over 24 m and carries through its junction mouths and into the ground held under it. Two runs more than 1 m apart are at two levels (a split-level street, a slip lane under a retaining wall): neither is moved, the lesser is clipped and a face stands between the two along the seam; a stub raised or lowered to a structure (`joins`) is clipped but never moved, and the structures meet a moved street at its drawn datum (eo-structures `solve`, `StreetWorld::street_datum`). A slip road merging into or leaving a through road alongside it (within 25 degrees, the slip with no walk: a freeway's ramps, at a freeway or a boulevard, whose walk gives way along it) is drawn from the through leg's mouth, clipped where the through road covers it and flush with it, and the junction's pad is the through legs' alone (it had fanned over the through road's lanes to the slip's far mouth, cut back 100 m and more, up to 0.6 m off them). A walk lying on a busier run's walk alongside it (two parallel streets, a divided road's two carriageways, their walks over the strip between, one over the other) ends at that walk's edge, its outer edge at that walk's level where the two are within 0.5 m (further, its own level, its bank meeting the other walk). Runs meeting end to end end on their bend's bisector and meet at one datum; the ground carpet and the street ground are held under the moved datums and round the bends' mitres. About 150 ms more to build the street world (once, at load); 0.5 % fewer street triangles. Measured by `street_scan` (its stacked paving row) and `cargo run --release -p eo-structures --example street_scan_joined` (the street as `--play` builds it, with the structures' joins) |
| Street geometry gate | Not a switch: eo-street `tests/geometry_gate.rs` runs the street scorecard (`examples/street_scan.rs`, `measure`) on every 8th street tile of the city (about 1,300 tiles, 6.4 km2 of paving, 1,300 junctions) and fails when stacked paving, ground poking through, cliff edges, sunken gaps, junction holes, junction steps or junction stacks rise more than about a tenth over the rates measured on 2026-11-27 (any junction hole fails). With the street switches above off six of the seven fail (stacked paving at 3.5 times its limit); with `--no-road-seams` alone five. About two minutes in release. Lower a limit when the street gets better; never raise one to pass. The whole city's scorecard: `cargo run --release -p eo-street --example street_scan -- hits.csv` |
| Water | `--no-water` (the flat sea plane), `--no-water-instanced` (the pass needs multi-draw-indirect-count, as before; without it the flat sea plane), `--no-water-brdf` (the earlier Fresnel, roughness, sun lobe and reflection march), `--no-water-body` (the earlier body colour), `--no-water-foam-cover` (the earlier whitecap cover and foam sampling), `--no-water-glint` (the earlier hashed sparkle), `--no-water-turbid` (the earlier three-wavelength body), `--no-water-shore` (the earlier waterline, wall collar and the lift under piers), `--no-water-foam-lit` (the earlier whitecap shapes and foam light), `--no-water-ppr` (the reflection march alone), `--no-water-wind-texture` (no gust patches, tidal current or rotated chop; drawn from the `high` tier up, `--water-wind-texture` below it), `--no-water-grazing` (one mean Fresnel times the sky in one direction) |
| Waterfront corridor | `--no-ferry-building` (its record's plain box instead), `--no-tram-tracks` (no streetcar rails), `--no-quay-railing` (no guard on the Embarcadero quay wall), `--no-ferry-building-detail` (the Ferry Building's first pass: no ashlar course scale, rustication, deep reveals, modillions, granite plinth, weathering or tower balustrades), `--no-rail-finish` (one bare-steel rail head and a black groove), `--no-corridor-palms` (no Embarcadero palms), `--no-guard-detail` (the quay guard's first pass, on the JS crest: no kick plate, solid pickets), `--no-pier-sheds` (the Ferry Plaza's JS shed boxes), `--no-rail-polish` (the rails' worn band whole at roughness 0.4, no burnished strip or sealant joints), `--no-facade-relief` (the Ferry Building's second pass: one glazing pane an opening, the second pass's reveal tone and dirt under the cornices, no fanlight frames or voussoir tones), `--no-palm-detail` (the corridor palms' first pass: no tree well, root initials, scar lattice, senescent leaves or spear, and unsurveyed palms at the median size) |
| The hills ([Docs/HILLS-PLAN.md](../Docs/HILLS-PLAN.md)) | `--no-deck-ground` (the roof decks the bare-earth survey keeps as ground left as surveyed, Moscone Center South's roof an earth mound over 4th and Folsom Streets, not brought down to the streets' grade by `native/assets/sf-decks-v1.json`), `--no-aerial-ground` (the terrain's colour from its vertex tones, not the hill builder's aerial macro map), `--no-city-relief` (the 2 m relief patches round the Golden Gate photo eyes alone, from the survey, not over every hill from the hill builder's cooked relief), `--no-city-planting` (outside the survey's image, the round-2 planting rules and the parks' mown lawns, not the NAIP rules from the hill builder's planting map), `--no-city-canopy-trees` (the greens' park trees on their whole 14 m lattice, near and far, not only under the canopy the hill builder's planting map finds in the aerial image), `--no-lidar-trees` (in the hill parks the lidar covers, McLaren, Bernal Heights, Twin Peaks, Mount Davidson, Glen Canyon, Corona Heights, Bayview Hill, Lands End and Golden Gate Park's west end, the greens' park-tree lattice again, near and far, not the trees the USGS 3DEP lidar measured there, each at its measured position, height and crown as a blue gum, Monterey cypress, Monterey pine or small broadleaf by its height over its crown (over 30 m a blue gum), from the hill builder's `<cache>/hills/trees-v1.bin`; `EO_HILLS_TREES` names another file), `--no-tree-variety` (the lidar trees' species picked by height and crown alone, as blue gum, Monterey cypress, Monterey pine or one street broadleaf, upright, with the species' card colours; without it each park draws its own mix of eleven species from the city's natural areas plan, with second builds, a lean downwind on the coast, each tree's own hue, and far cards in each species' own shade; `eo-app props/lidar_trees.rs`), `--no-city-grass` (outside the survey's image box, the meadow's short sward, wild oats, thatch and fescue patches and the flora pass's seed stalks on the city's open grassland, not the annual grassland's standing canopy: wild oats and the other annual grasses 0.4-0.9 m over their own litter, darker down in the canopy, from heading in March until the autumn rains flatten it; `eo_landmarks::vegetation::MEADOW_ANNUAL`), `--no-hill-outcrops` (the hills' rock as the props' procedural blocks alone, with no scanned rock faces, clusters or stones from the survey set's CC0 rock scans, and the scans not looked for in `external/survey` without `EO_SURVEY_ROOT`; with it the scans' 2k maps take about 300 MB of GPU memory), `--no-city-lithology` (the hill rock's type by its boxes alone, chert north of the strait, serpentinite in the Presidio's bluff band and sandstone elsewhere, not by the city's bedrock map `native/assets/sf-lithology-v1.json.gz` from the USGS geologic maps, which makes Twin Peaks, Mount Davidson, Bernal Heights and Corona Heights chert, Potrero Hill serpentinite and the greenstone outcrops greenstone wherever it maps bedrock) |
| Scanned surfaces | `--no-cc0` (all), `--no-area-paint` (a lane-wide marking worn as coloured pavement, not as a line), `--no-cc0-street`, `--no-cc0-walls`, `--no-cc0-ground`, `--no-cc0-decals`, `--no-cc0-grime`, `--no-cc0-stains`, `--no-cc0-pom`, `--no-cc0-tiling` |
| Lighting and sky | `--no-headlamp-stray` (a low beam lights everything over its cut-off at the glare zone's 620 cd again, at any height; otherwise from 4 deg up its stray light falls to 62 cd by 10 deg: FMVSS 108 Table XIX holds every US lower beam to 125 cd from 10 deg up to the zenith, and compliant lamps measure 37 to 104 cd there; a car stopped under a signal head lit the head's visors and its pole white from below; eo-render sky_light.wgsl `sky_head_glare`, sky_reflect.rs `set_headlamp_stray`; the lit surfaces' lights loop only, the cars', the people's and the lit haze's own copies of the low beam unchanged), `--no-shadows`, `--no-ao`, `--no-lamp-spread`, `--no-far-street-light` (past ~300 m the streets lit from a map of every lamp's pool), `--no-sky-vis`, `--no-contact-shadows`, `--no-atmosphere`, `--no-phys-sky`, `--no-ap-lut`, `--no-night-shaping`, `--no-fog`, `--no-clouds`, `--no-cloud-shadows`, `--no-cloud-depth-haze`, `--no-volumetric-clouds`, `--no-shafts`, `--no-moon-stars`, `--no-lights` |
| Lighting additions (opt-in) | `--no-cascade-blend`, `--no-far-shadows`, `--no-ambient-volume`, `--no-probe-grid`, `--no-light-shadows`, `--no-light-shadow-moving`, `--no-headlight-shadows`, `--no-lit-fog`, `--no-smooth-time`, `--no-ground-bounce`, `--no-sun-bounce`, `--no-sun-bounce-reach`, `--no-ao-albedo`, `--no-soft-shadows`, `--no-screen-gi`, `--no-occluder-light`, `--no-dome-env`, `--no-dome-yield`, `--no-shadow-slope-bias`, `--no-grime`, `--no-street-life`, `--no-photo-finish`, `--no-facade-clutter`, `--no-shop-light`, `--no-car-detail`, `--no-look`, `--no-night-photometry`, `--no-lamp-bounce`, `--no-urban-sky`, `--no-office-floors`, `--no-room-depth`, `--no-lit-rooms`, `--no-lamp-units`, `--no-luminaires`, `--no-rear-lamps`, `--no-signal-spill`, `--no-wire-light` (each wins over `--quality`), `--no-rt-gi`, `--no-rt-reflect` (no tier turns `--rt-gi` or `--rt-reflect` on) |
| Natural detail (opt-in) | `--no-foliage-light`, `--no-temporal-lod-fade`, `--no-turf`, `--no-palm-detail`, `--no-hill-cover`, `--no-ground-cover`, `--no-bare-ground`, `--no-palm-variety` (each off by default; the last of a switch and its `--no-` form wins) |
| World additions (opt-in) | `--no-structures`, `--no-tunnels` (the last of a switch and its `--no-` form wins; `eo-server` takes them too) |
| Street surface (opt-in) | `--street-pbr` draws the street's physically based surface (eo-render `street_pbr.wgsl`, `SF_STREET_PBR`): the carriageway at a weathered asphalt's albedo with one world-anchored tone field across every lane, polished wheel paths and an oil band per lane (the lane, parking lane and design vehicle widths from the standards table), the patch quilt in the road's own frame with sealed, eased edges, the weathering's crack sealant and the patches' sealed saw cuts drawn as bitumen (black fresh, dusted grey aged, a glossy cast film that a low sun or a bright sky down the street draws as bright ribbons, the overband's crown a few millimetres proud) and open cracks occluding their own floor (they stay dark under the road's sheen), smooth carriageway normals (no camber or junction-fan facets), a concrete gutter pan with its cold joint and a silt band of wandering width, rounded kerb arrises chipped in pieces at no repeat, scored slabs each its own pour tone and settlement tilt (a few replaced or stained) with dark joints, cracks and broken corners, raised paint edges; markings worn through to the road in the asphalt scan's own texture (patches drawn out along the line, ragged edges, more on old lines) with the road's cracks and sealant running on through them; the open spaces' paving at its material's albedo (soiled granite setts 0.22, clay brick pavers 0.28, concrete pavers 0.30, in place of the styles' 0.45-0.7 display tones); the plazas' paving scans parallax-occlusion mapped near the eye, stochastically tiled (height-blended hex tiles) past 12 m and self-shadowed toward the sun (a height march near, Smith masking far; look_light.wgsl multiplies the key by `sp_sun_vis`); in the wet, asphalt soaked to the scans' aged tone, water standing in ruts, birdbaths and the gutter's flow line past what has drained (sky_light.wgsl `sky_wet_street` takes `sp_pond` as its puddle), the film filling the asphalt's fine texture (its detail normal eased toward the film's: no salt-and-pepper glints) and the markings wet and glossy. Off by default and in no tier; `--no-street-pbr` (the last of the two wins) compiles it out and gives the frame before it |
| Street wear and glare (opt-in) | `--street-wear` (eo-render `street_pbr.wgsl` `sw_*`, `SF_STREET_WEAR`): the carriageway's cracks as one web over the street (carriageway, junction pads, the markings on them) in place of the weathering's crack web: its 3.4 m cells' edges bent at every scale a crack follows (metres, decimetres, round the stones) and only some of them cracked, on a framed road most of those running across it (thermal cracks, the FHWA's LTPP distress manual) and fewer along it (block cracking where the road is older), at a junction pad either way and denser; each crack in runs that taper out (its ends and its branches), its width from a hairline to LTPP's moderate 6-19 mm, the wider ones spalled at the lips, a dirty margin of a centimetre or two along it; past where that margin is under a pixel, fewer cracks drawn each a pixel wide (the web thins, the lines stay read); under a third sealed under a ragged overband 5-8 cm across, glossy only fresh (the oxidised band holds grit, roughness 0.7), its tone soaking toward the road's under the wet film; a marking keeps only the cracks that run either way; fatigue cracking in small sharp-angled pieces only in the wheel paths' worst stretches; every line filtered over the pixel's footprint across it, and none past 110 m. Trodden gum on the walks where people stand and wait (the activity field's patches, the kerb's edge, half as many on a plaza), each disc flattened into its own ellipse, mostly specks and a few 3-5 cm, from two scatters at different pitches, and the scanned gum decals' discs thinned in irregular gaps, each stretch its own age (no grid of even dots). The decals' drip line down each lane is drips' pools 3-9 cm across, fresh ones black and old ones spread and paler, densest at the track's middle (a soft band from afar, never a streak), and a utility repair's pieces share one pour's tone keyed on the world (no panels). `--street-glare` (`sg_*`, `SF_STREET_GLARE`): the asphalt as two surfaces, the rough binder in the texture's valleys and the polished aggregate tops, the tops drawn as a smooth layer (look_light.wgsl's coat, F0 0.04 on the plane) whose share of the view grows toward grazing as Smith's masking hides the valleys (the low sun's glare down a road, brightest on the wheel paths, stone by stone near the eye; dry, by day and toward grazing only, where its Fresnel share counts: elsewhere the layer's lighting is not run at all), the markings' resin film with it (where worn, half of it; a wide coloured surfacing over 0.7-1.2 m, a bike or bus lane's anti-skid resin with its own aggregate, matte: 70 % less); the setts' crowns polished by feet the same way, the kerb arris worn smooth. In the wet the drips go under the film and the wheel paths no longer smooth it. Both need `--street-pbr` and the cinematic look for all of it; off by default and in no tier; the last of a switch and its `--no-` form wins, and the `--no-` form compiles it out |
| Street aggregate (opt-in) | `--street-aggregate` (eo-render `street_pbr.wgsl` `sa_*`, `SF_STREET_AGGREGATE`): the hot mix's coarse stones near the eye (Caltrans Type A 1/2 in mix: a Voronoi cell per 9.5 mm stone, four in five holding one), each its rock (greywacke, basalt, pale and red chert at their measured reflectances), its top worn out of the binder film from the middle outward (45 % of the tops between the wheel paths, 85 % in them) and polished (roughness 0.5, 0.36 in the tracks), its fractured face tilted its own way and its crown and arris falling into the mastic, so a low sun picks the road out stone by stone; the mastic of binder, fines and dust between them, its sky a little hidden. Every term is divided by the layout's mean (measured in street_pbr.rs `aggregate_means`), so the road's calibrated tone and finish hold: the tones are kept while a stone spans a few pixels across the view, the relief and finish while it does over the footprint's mean, then the layer is its mean. The asphalt scans' blotchy 5-10 cm relief ("crumpled foil" in a low sun) gives way to it on the carriageway and junction pads (65 % of it out); near the eye the glare layer (`--street-glare`) leaves most of the polish to the resolved stones. Needs `--street-pbr`; off by default and in no tier; `--no-street-aggregate` (the last wins) compiles it out |
| Plaza setts (opt-in) | `--plaza-setts` (eo-render `street_pbr.wgsl` `ss_*`, `SF_PLAZA_SETTS`): the grey-stone plazas' cobble scan (both plaza paths, through `sp_pave`: parallax-mapped and self-shadowed as `--street-pbr` lays it) with what its one patch cannot carry over a plaza: the joints' fill at a level of its own along each stretch (in the scan's height units, between its joint floor and the foot of the stones' rounded sides: swept in nearly flush in places, washed and swept out in others; the parallax ray stops on it), drawn as its own material where it lies over the scan's floor (jointing sand, packed grit and soil, moss where the joints stay damp and unswept; the scan's photographed joint under it carries the grain, the fill's material its tone; occluded by its depth under the crowns); each sett's own wear and soiling over the tops; the crowns (the scan's height over its local mean, a stone's blur) cleaned and polished by soles where the foot traffic runs, the glare layer (`--street-glare`) on them; the scanned gum decals on the setts thinned to a few trodden clusters (no disc on every stone). Needs `--street-pbr`; off by default and in no tier; `--no-plaza-setts` (the last wins) compiles it out |
| Walk relief (opt-in) | `--walk-relief` (eo-render `street_pbr.wgsl` `wr_*`, `SF_WALK_RELIEF`): the kerbs cast in segments, a contraction joint at every third line of the walk's scoring grid (the registry's `sidewalk-scoring-grid-sf` pitch), each segment its own pour tone, nose worn to a radius of its own and small yaw out of line; the arris spalled at random places along the kerb (a Poisson scatter of breaks 6-22 cm long, skewed, lumpy and ragged, the same break on the face and the top, most of them old and dirty, a few fresh and light) in place of the weathering's value-noise chips, which sat on their lattice's nodes (a sawtooth at fixed intervals); a silt and water line along the foot of the face at a height that wanders. The walk's tooled joints in relief: slabs settled each to a height of its own (a lip of a few millimetres, heaved slabs a centimetre, cold-patch repairs sunk), each joint seen through a one-dimensional height field across it (the near slab's top and rounded nose, the groove's dirty floor, the far slab's riser, its lip above the near slab open to the sky, its nose facing the eye), each part's share of the pixel its interval's overlap with the footprint across the joint (a box filter of the exact profile), the dirt soles grind into the noses round it; these joints replace the flat joint darkening of the street pass, the scoring and the weathering. The walk's along coordinate is anchored on the street tile's corner instead of the eye (its across direction comes from screen derivatives, and `wx_run` multiplied their noise by kilometres: the speckled seams and stippled joints at a slab's edge), its grid's phase stepping once at a tile's edge; the slabs' own terms fade over the last 3 cm to a joint; a slab replaced with asphalt is cold patch in the asphalt scan's texture, not a flat fill. Needs `--street-pbr`; off by default and in no tier; `--no-walk-relief` (the last wins) compiles it out |
| Matte wheel paths (opt-in; on in `--play`) | `--matte-wheel-paths` (eo-render `street_pbr.wgsl` `sp_wheel_polish`, `SF_MATTE_WHEEL_PATHS`, `street_pbr.rs` `ASPHALT_ROUGH_POLISHED_MATTE`): the wheel paths' polished roughness 0.68 instead of 0.52. At 0.52 the paths in a building's shade reflected the bright sky down the street as sky-blue bands from the eye's feet to the far end (Mission at 3rd, noon); the photograph of that street from the same corner shows the near carriageway matte and the polish only as a faint sheen far down the road toward grazing, which 0.68 keeps. Needs `--street-pbr`; off by default and in no tier, on in `--play`; `--no-matte-wheel-paths` (the last wins) compiles it out |
| Aged aggregate (opt-in; on in `--play`) | `--aged-aggregate` (eo-render `street_pbr.wgsl` `sa_aggregate`, `SF_AGED_AGGREGATE`, `street_pbr.rs` `AGG_AGED_TONE`, `AGG_AGED_NEAR`, `AGG_AGED_FAR`): with `--street-aggregate`, an old street's grime film over the stones (each stone's tone keeps 0.3 of its departure from the layout's mean) and their tones only where a stone spans a few pixels across the view (whole over ten pixels, gone at four), as a camera's lens and sensor take a three-pixel grain's contrast down. At Mission and 3rd at noon the shaded carriageway 2-4 m from the eye measured a fine contrast (linear luminance less a 6 px blur, its spread over its mean) of 0.76 with the stones' full tones, 0.29 with this and 0.13 in the photograph of that corner (0.20 without the aggregate): a near-even grey instead of a mosaic of pale stones. The tones' widened tops had also drawn the street 16% lighter than its calibrated tone at that distance; that is gone too. Needs `--street-pbr` and `--street-aggregate`; off by default and in no tier, on in `--play`; `--no-aged-aggregate` (the last wins) compiles it out |
| Waterfront fill (opt-in) | `--quay-fill` (eo-landmarks `LandmarkSet::add_quay_fill`): where the ground between the quay apron and the street's walk lies lower than both (the sunken strip of bare ground behind the seawall on the Embarcadero), the apron's paving is carried on to the walk's edge, station by station along each quay wall edge, at no more than the curb ramp's running slope and never past a building; elsewhere nothing changes. Off by default and in no tier; `--no-quay-fill` (the last wins) gives the ground before it |
| Vehicles (opt-in) | `--no-car-materials`, `--no-car-contact`, `--no-car-reflections`, `--no-car-traffic`, `--no-car-lamps`, `--no-car-wet`, `--no-car-finish`, `--no-car-density`, `--no-car-panels` |
| Weather | `--no-rain`, `--no-wet`, `--no-wind`, `--no-weather-fx`, `--no-wet-surfaces`, `--no-wet-relief`, `--no-lamp-streaks` |
| Camera finish | `--no-taa`, `--no-bloom`, `--no-adaptation`, `--no-ev100`, `--no-filmic`, `--no-ssr`, `--no-screen-reflect`, `--no-probe-sky` |
| Camera model | `--cine-camera` (with `--look cinematic`; off by default, no tier sets it) meters each frame from its own luminance histogram (lower-centre weighted, a percentile window, clamped against the hour's exposure: by day it lifts a shaded street by up to 1.3 EV and never darkens a sunlit frame); a bilateral-grid local exposure that compresses the frame's base only as far as its measured range overflows the display's room (the darkest 3 % of the metered weight under grey; the brightest 1 % of the whole frame over a knee 1.6 EV over grey by day, so a sky gap comes down while a sunlit wall keeps its level; a frame that fits keeps its contrast), raises the detail against the grid's edge-aware base (x1.22 by day, x1.12 at night), and at night pushes a pixel's own highlights (up to x1.5 in EV over grey) until the frame's brightest 0.1 % sit 4 EV over grey (the lamps burn); a camera response with a long toe (4, 5 and 6 EV under grey at sRGB 17, 10 and 6 instead of a crush at 4.5 EV under) into a hue-preserving AgX-style log sigmoid with a print's saturation, and a grade by the hour whose S-curve works over the mid-tones only and whose warm highlights stay off a blue sky; a threshold-free, energy-kept bloom over seven levels with a power-law tail and a 1.2 % veil; the visible sun's veiling glare (a core and a 1/r^2 skirt), a soft starburst only far over white and dispersed hexagonal ghosts, in place of the lamps' sprite starbursts; a depth of field from a full-frame lens (the field's focal length, f/2.8 at night, f/4 by day) focused on the frame's middle (its mean dioptre; the resolve leaves each pixel's distance in its alpha), gathered over 16 taps where the blur circle passes 0.6 px; a robust contrast-adaptive sharpen (after RCAS, off in flat gradients, halved on noise, off where the focus blurs); the lens's lateral colour (2 px red to blue at the corner), cos^4 fall-off (half of it: 0.7 EV at the corners), and a grain a little over a pixel across in the shadows and mid-tones (0.42 % by day, 0.65 % at night; none in a bright sky) (eo-render cine_post.rs, look_cine_camera.wgsl, cine_camera.wgsl, sky_resolve.wgsl). `EO_CAM_DEBUG=1` draws the meter's state as bars along the top; `EO_CAM_DEBUG=2` writes the frame's log luminance (the hour's exposure, -16 .. +10 in red and green) in place of the picture. `--no-cine-camera` restores the previous camera |
| Camera tone, balance, halation, sharpen, flare, resolve and print | Each needs `--cine-camera` (and `--look cinematic`); off by default, no tier sets them; the last of a switch and its `--no-` form wins. `--camera-tone` keeps the camera model's meter, local exposure, per-channel curve and the hour's keys, and makes the grade safe at the top and split by the light: the grade's table is baked open (its colour is not clamped at 1; the highlights' warm balance held until display luma 0.97 instead of fading from 0.92) and its result is brought into the display's gamut by a soft edge (a channel past display-linear 0.85 is held under a rational knee that reaches 1 at 1.6, the excess taken first by darkening the colour, 0.8 of it in log times the colour's saturation, so a grey is never darkened, then by desaturating it toward its own luminance), so a golden sky or a sunlit wall keeps its hue and a gradient where the display-space warm balance had pushed red to a flat 255 plateau; the shade's colour goes toward neutral in the scene before the curve (by day 0.7 of its chroma kept from 4 EV under grey down, all of it again from 1.2 EV under; none at night: the lamps keep their colour), so an open shade's sky blue and a deck's orange bounce read near grey against the warm light instead of one cast over the frame, and by day the shadows' cool balance is neutral (a shaded street is not navy); the lens's veiling glare as a floor (1.5 % of the metered mean at night, 0.4 % by day: a night's unlit facade keeps a trace of detail); by day the highlights' room 2.5 EV over grey (was 2.8); a grain of luminance only that follows the meter's lift (0.55 % at night, 0.35 % by day). `--white-balance` balances the camera on the scene's light: a grey-world estimate over the frame's surfaces (no sky, nothing far from middle grey, no strongly coloured surface) corrected along the colour temperature's axis by day 15 % of a warm light's cast and 50 % of a cool one's, at night 25 % and 20 % (by day under a high sun, `--wb-high-sun`, on by default with `--white-balance`: the warm light's 15 % rises to the whole cast as the sun climbs from 30 to 50 deg, eo-render cine_post.rs `wb_warm_degree`, `CAM_WB_HIGH_SUN_DEGREE`, so a midday street's sunlit grey reads near neutral as a camera's daylight balance renders it, where the 15 % left the 5200 K beam's asphalt on Folsom Street at noon at blue/red 0.64 (now 0.86-0.93) against the matched 2011 photograph's 0.93-1.00; a frame mostly in blue shade reads as a cool light and keeps the day's 50 % of a cool cast; a sun under 30 deg keeps the warmth as before; `--no-wb-high-sun` restores the day's 15 %), and 35 % along the green-magenta axis (within 0.45 and 0.1 EV a channel), converging as the meter does; the hour's colour moves out of the display-space grade into the camera as a white-balance preset and colour rendering in the scene before the curve, the display grade keeping the hour's curve with near-neutral balances. `--lens-halation` grows the bloom's veil, halation and its cap on a source's energy with the weather's water (the weather's lamp haze over clear air's and the rain's rate), energy kept. `--camera-sharpen` sharpens as a camera's processing does: the robust contrast-adaptive sharpen at 0.2 stops under its limit (the model's 0.5) and an unsharp mask of the 3 x 3 neighbourhood's luminance (0.45 of the pixel's difference from its binomial blur) held inside the neighbourhood's range widened by 8 % of it (no halo, no ringing), off in flat gradients and where the focus blurs. `--lens-flare` gives every bright point source the lens's diffraction: the meter lists the frame's point sources (in each 32-pixel cell its brightest 4 x 4 block over 3.3 exposed, about 4.2 EV over grey, refined to its brightest pixel, kept where no pixel within 5 px is brighter and it stands 2.5 times over a ring 18 px round it, or 5 times over a ring 36 px round it (a near lamp's globe inside its own glow): a lamp or its globe, a headlamp, a glint, not a lit window; not the sun's disc; at most 64), each with its flux over 1.0 exposed (logarithmic past 400) and its colour; the post pass draws six spikes per source (a hexagonal aperture's three edge pairs, turned 15 deg), a pixel wide at the core and fanning out (1 + 0.012 px a px), falling as a straight edge's diffraction, (3 / (3 + r))^2, from 0.004 x the flux (over 11 x 11 pixels) at the core, each colour at its own wavelength's length (red 1.12, blue 0.86 of green's: the tips warm), drawn until under 0.003 exposed, so a spike's length grows as the square root of its source's flux (a lamp at the flux cap about 75 px, the brightest 140 px at most; a source whose spikes would be under 12 px draws none and keeps its round bloom), fading over the last 60 % of it; the sun's ghost chain at 0.8 of the model's, its own soft starburst unchanged. `--camera-resolve` (eo-render sky_resolve.wgsl, `SF_CAM_RESOLVE`) accumulates the temporal resolve in the unjittered image: the history is read where the camera's motion alone moved the pixel's point (the model's resolve read it where the point lay without this frame's jitter but wrote the result back at the jittered pixel, so every frame resampled the history up to half a pixel off at random and the fine detail went soft), the first frames after a cut are averaged evenly (frame n weighs 1 / (n + 1) until the steady 0.1 takes over), and a still pixel's clipping box is 2 standard deviations wide (1.25 before; past half a pixel of motion the model's again), so an edge or a wire whose coverage the jitter moves accumulates instead of being clipped back to this frame's sample. `--camera-print` gives the camera's response a print's black by day: the toe's slope in the deep shadows runs from the cine camera's 0.45 at night to 0.85 by day (blended over the sun's altitude as the model's terms are), so 6 EV under grey goes a stop deeper toward black while 2 and 3 EV under keep their level; the lens's veiling floor (`--camera-tone`) fades out by day with it; and the detail against the local exposure's edge-aware base is raised to 1.35 by day (the model's 1.22; the night's 1.12 unchanged) (eo-render cine_post.rs, cine_camera.wgsl, look_cine_camera.wgsl, look_cine_flare.wgsl, sky_resolve.wgsl). `--no-camera-tone`, `--no-white-balance`, `--no-lens-halation`, `--no-camera-sharpen`, `--no-lens-flare`, `--no-camera-resolve`, `--no-camera-print` restore the camera model as before (`--lens-flare` changed in round 2 iteration 3: its spikes are shorter, energy-scaled and drawn on near lamps' globes, its ghosts fainter; no switch restores its earlier spikes) |
| Camera look and exposure | `--no-gg2-camera` (all three below), `--no-camera-look`, `--no-scene-meter`, `--no-lamp-photocell`, `--no-night-lift` (the darker night of 2026-11-17: unlit ground at 8/255, not 14) |
| Daylight and sky | `--no-gg2-daylight` (all of these), `--no-ground-albedo`, `--no-photo-date`, `--no-clear-sky-state`, `--no-cirrus`, `--no-vol-sky-state`, `--no-measured-aerosol`, `--no-wb-high-sun`; reserved, nothing reads them yet: `--no-terminator-bias`, `--no-spectral-sky` |
| Golden Gate, fourth pass | `--no-bridges4` (all of these), `--no-bridge-shade2`, `--no-bridge-dielectric`, `--no-bridge-haunch`, `--no-bridge-bracing`, `--no-bridge-pylons`, `--no-bridge-arch2`, `--no-bridge-fender2`, `--no-bridge-crest`, `--no-bridge-collar` |
| Strait water | `--no-gg2-water` (all of these), `--no-water-exposure`, `--no-water-current`, `--no-water-lean` (drawn from the `high` tier up, `--water-lean` below it), `--no-water-ppr-blur`, `--no-beach-profiles`, `--no-water-streak-caps`, `--no-water-far-calm`, `--no-baker-surf`; reserved: `--no-swash-line` |
| Shore ground | `--no-gg2-ground` (all of these), `--no-beach-sand-limit`, `--no-sand-albedo`, `--no-rocks`, `--no-riprap`, `--no-pier-lines`, `--no-season-seam`; reserved: `--no-ground-steps` |
| Far layer | `--no-gg2-far` (all of these), `--no-far-fallback`, `--no-landmark-far-models`, `--no-far-tree-density`, `--no-presidio-rule`; reserved: `--no-far-paint` |
| Corridor traffic | `--no-corridor-traffic` (the whole layer), `--no-deck-walkers` |
| Coastal vegetation | `--no-gg2-vegetation` (all of these), `--no-coastal-trees`, `--no-ground-cover`, `--no-hill-rock` |
| Round 3, all tracks | `--no-gg3` turns every round-3 track's roll-up on (restores round 2's output) |
| Frame budget | `--no-gg3-perf` (all of these), `--no-draw-ranges` |
| Photo dates and sky | `--no-gg3-sky` (all of these), `--no-photo-season`, `--no-cirrus2`, `--no-sky-day-fade`, `--no-live-calendar` |
| Colour and camera | `--no-gg3-colour` (all of these), `--no-sun-spectrum2`, `--no-meter-highlights`; reserved: `--no-sky-calibration`, `--no-camera-style`, `--no-haze-visibility` |
| Terrain surface | `--no-gg3-ground` (all of these), `--no-facet-normal-fix`, `--no-terrain-detail`, `--no-terrain-materials`, `--no-sand-detail2`; reserved: `--no-bluff-soil`, `--no-bank-grade` |
| Scanned rock | `--no-gg3-rock` (all of these), `--no-scan-rocks`, `--no-cliff-dressing`, `--no-surf-rocks`, `--no-scan-riprap`, `--no-rock-geology` |
| Coastal vegetation, round 3 | `--no-gg3-vegetation` (all of these), `--no-naip-planting`, `--no-cliff-rock-cover`, `--no-far-species`, `--no-far-tree-light`, `--no-bloom-date`, `--no-grass-color-variety`; reserved: `--no-veg-scans` |
| Grass, second pass | `--no-grass2` (all of these: run 1's meadow), `--no-meadow-cover`, `--no-meadow-types`, `--no-blade-shading` |
| Grass, third pass | `--no-grass3` (all of these: run 2's meadow), `--no-scrub-mounds`, `--no-scrub-bloom`, `--no-bare-ground`, `--no-meadow-breakup`, `--no-mat-free-meadow` |
| Flora (scrub species, grassland season, chert, serpentinite, flowers) | `--no-flora` (all of these: the third grass pass's output), `--no-scrub-species`, `--no-grass-ramp`, `--no-bluff-scrub`, `--no-chert-ground`, `--no-serpentinite`, `--no-flora-flowers` |
| Flora 2 (Battery Spencer knoll, Baker bluff rock and boulders) | `--no-flora2` (all of these: the flora pass's output), `--no-knoll-ground`, `--no-knoll-relief`, `--no-bluff-rock`, `--no-boulder-surf` |
| Surf and bathymetry | `--no-gg3-water` (all of these), `--no-bathymetry`, `--no-surf-break`, `--no-swash-foam`, `--no-obstacle-foam`, `--no-water-body2`; reserved: `--no-ppr-dash` |
| Golden Gate, fifth pass | `--no-gg3-bridge` or `--no-bridges5` (all of these), `--no-paint-brdf2`, `--no-paint-weathering`, `--no-concrete2`, `--no-leg-cells`, `--no-cable-detail` |
| Shader compilation | `--no-agents-relaxed-math` (fly-through and `--bench`): with the cinematic look, the agents' draw shader compiled with Metal's fast math, as before, instead of the relaxed math mode. With fast math, Apple's compiler drew cyan speckles on car paint once the cinematic look, `--grime`, `--ambient-volume`, lamp or headlight shadows, `--far-shadows` and `--cascade-blend` were all on (the `high` and `hyper` tiers); see `vendor/wgpu-hal/EARTH-ONLINE.md`. Without the cinematic look and on other backends it changes nothing |
| Visibility and streaming | `--no-occlusion`, `--no-view-detail`, `--no-upload-ring`, `--no-submit-pacing`, `--no-indirect-count`, `--no-count-emulation`, `--no-bounded-draws` (wins over the opt-in `--bounded-draws`; see [What is drawn](#what-is-drawn)), `--no-city-classes` (the city's opaque pass as one pipeline and one list pair, as before the split), `--no-motion-bounds` (the count emulation's late opaque lists always floored by their early lists' bounds and the early lists at their reserve, as before the motion-aware bounds), `--no-probe-face-counts` (the count emulation draws the scene probe's faces to the read-back history's bound alone, as before; by default a capture's faces wait for its count pass and each is drawn to at least its own count), `--no-cut-window` (the count emulation keeps the view before a camera cut in its read-back window until 64 newer read-backs push it out, and the cut's own frame in it, and the occlusion turning on or off is no cut, as before; by default a cut starts the window afresh from the frame after its own), `--no-vm-blocks` (macOS: every block from the system allocator, as before; by default blocks of 1 MiB and more come from the virtual memory manager through a cache of their own, `eo-render/src/alloc.rs`) |
| Interface and sound | `--no-hud`, `--no-audio`, `--audio-null` (mix to a null output), `--no-settings-apply` (`--ui`: the settings file does not reach the renderer or the world) |
| Optional assets | `--no-external` (read each set only from the folder its variable names, not from `external/`: the output before that folder was read) |
| Online (`--connect`) | `--no-remote-bodies` (other players drawn as before: first walk style and idle, linear path, no emotes or phone, street people may wear a player's body), `--no-name-tags` (no tags, no tag depth probe), `--no-player-contact` (no ground contact under their bodies, and its shading compiled out of the agents' shaders: `SF_PLAYER_CONTACT`), `--no-shared-sky` (the client's own hour and weather) |
| Photo mode | `--no-photo-mode` (none of it: P does nothing, shots draw no depth of field or finish), `--no-dof` (photo mode without its depth of field) |

A new visual feature should come with its own switch, defaulting to on only
once it has been reviewed.

The lighting switches: `--no-phys-sky` keeps the browser game's light rig,
aerosol and dome anchor in place of the physical daylight (the sun, the sky
and the ground's light from the clear coastal atmosphere in `eo-look`
`atmosphere` and `phys`, the sky drawn at the sun's own scale, the city's
reflections read from the sky tables); `--no-ap-lut` keeps the haze toward
the horizon's colour in place of the aerial-perspective volume; `--no-filmic`
keeps the ACES fit with the post chain's shoulder, grade and finish in place
of the filmic curve, the daylight white balance and the chroma gain;
`--no-ev100` keeps the JS exposure curve in place of the incident meter's
EV100; `--no-sky-vis` keeps the occlusion pass's 4 m contact term in place of
the canyon-scale sky visibility and wall bounce; `--no-night-shaping` keeps
the rig's rim light on at night (without it an unlit wall at night is lit by
the night sky's fill and the practicals alone). All six together give the
previous output.

The camera switches. `--no-camera-look` keeps the filmic curve, its chroma
gain and the incident meter's +0.85 EV offset in place of the camera look.
The camera look is the daylight white balance, the ISO 12232 SOS placement
(the meter's grey at scene 0.18, sRGB 118 at 0.2215) and the ACES 2.0 output
transform for a 100-nit sRGB display, baked into a 65^3 table (eo-look
`tone::camera`); the submerged camera (`--water-optics`) finishes through
the same table. `--no-filmic` still keeps the older ACES-fit chain and turns
the camera look off with it. `--no-scene-meter` keeps the dome-estimate meter
in place of the reflected-light meter on the drawn frame: a
quarter-resolution, centre-weighted luminance histogram, K = 12.5, within 2
stops of the incident reading, holding the top 2 % under the shoulder.
`--no-lamp-photocell` keeps the sun-altitude lamp ramp in place of
street-lamp photocells (ANSI C136.10 levels, 1.5 +- 0.5 fc on, at most 1.5x
off) on the twilight illuminance. `--no-gg2-camera` turns all three off and
gives the previous output.

The daylight switches. A clear day's sky is cloudless, cirrus-only or fair
cumulus, drawn per game day of the world clock from San Francisco airport's
2014-2023 METAR shares for the month of the sun's date (the table is
`eo-look/data/ksfo-clear-day-sky.csv`, generated by `ksfo-clear-day-sky.py`
from the query it records; input through `EO_KSFO_METAR_CSV`,
digest-checked). The state fades with the weather's clear weight
(1 - smoothstep(0.1, 0.3, overcast)), with no jump. `--no-clear-sky-state`
keeps the fair-weather sky on every clear day; `--no-cirrus` keeps the 5.2 km
sheet in place of fibrous ice cloud at 9.5 km; `--no-ground-albedo` keeps the
city's ground albedo under the sky light everywhere in place of the land
cover's round the eye; `--no-photo-date` makes a shot ignore its
`day_of_year`. Shots can hold the sky (`sky`), the date (`day_of_year`) and
the game day (`game_day`). `--no-gg2-daylight` turns them all off.

The Golden Gate's fourth pass (`--no-bridges4`, read by eo-landmarks
`suspension`): `--no-bridge-shade2` keeps the JS's dark bake on the struts,
fins, bracing and floor beams and the baked occlusion in place of one paint
and the sky the deck and the opposite leg hide; `--no-bridge-dielectric`
adds the sea's light on top of the environment's ground share instead of
replacing it; `--no-bridge-haunch`, `--no-bridge-bracing`,
`--no-bridge-pylons`, `--no-bridge-arch2`, `--no-bridge-fender2` and
`--no-bridge-collar` leave out the portal struts' casings and haunches, the
bracing under the roadway, the pylons S1, S2, N1 and N2 with the tie-downs
and anchorage housings, the Fort Point arch's laced ribs, the south fender
at its published size, and the truss closed at the tower legs;
`--no-bridge-crest` keeps the roadway's sag in place of its surveyed crest at
midspan (the corridor traffic follows the deck either way).

The water switches (`--no-gg2-water`, read by eo-water `switches`):
`--no-water-exposure` leaves out the exposure field and the strait's
measured wind floor; `--no-water-current` the tidal current on the game's
clock; `--no-water-lean` the anisotropic cascade fade and sub-pixel slope
variance (left out below the `high` tier unless `--water-lean`: Quality
tiers); `--no-water-ppr-blur` the slope-blurred projected reflection;
`--no-beach-profiles` each beach's own foreshore slope and turbid bottom;
`--no-water-streak-caps` the measured afternoon wind and streaked
whitecaps; `--no-water-far-calm` the far calm and current lines;
`--no-baker-surf` the open coast's surf.

The shore-ground switches (`--no-gg2-ground`, read by eo-landmarks
`features::shore_ground`): `--no-beach-sand-limit` lets the beach sand run
past the berm and the cliff toe; `--no-sand-albedo` keeps the earlier sand
tone in place of measured dry and wet sand, the tide-keyed damp band, wrack
and footprints; `--no-rocks` leaves out the boulders; `--no-riprap` the
quarried-block revetment and the Fort Point seawall's coursed granite;
`--no-pier-lines` the decks built from the citywide map's pier lines
(Torpedo Wharf, the Coast Guard walkway); `--no-season-seam` keeps the city
terrain and the far layer on their own dryness curves.

The far-layer switches (`--no-gg2-far`, eo-render `far_city`):
`--no-far-fallback` keeps the far layer off where the adapter lacks
multi-draw-indirect-count; `--no-landmark-far-models` lets Fort Point's
authored model leave residency at its old range; `--no-far-tree-density`
keeps the old far park tree sampling; `--no-presidio-rule` keeps the
Presidio's buildings in the generic palette instead of off-white walls and
red tile roofs.

The corridor traffic (eo-app `agents::corridor`): through traffic on US-101,
CA-1 and Marina Boulevard and walkers on the bridge's east walk, from a flow
layer on the world clock (eo-sim `corridor`) at the published counts, with
the driver handbook's three-second gap. `--no-corridor-traffic` leaves the
whole layer out; `--no-deck-walkers` the walkers alone.

The coastal vegetation (eo-landmarks `vegetation`): coastal tree stands,
ground cover and hill rock on the props tiles. `--no-coastal-trees` keeps the
park species in the mapped woods; `--no-ground-cover` leaves out the grass,
tufts, flowers and scrub; `--no-hill-rock` the rock outcrops.
`--no-gg2-vegetation` turns all three off.

The round-3 switches. `--no-gg3` turns every round-3 track off at once and
gives round 2's output; each track also has its own roll-up.

The frame budget (`--no-gg3-perf`, eo-render `pace`): `--no-draw-ranges`. On
an adapter without multi-draw-indirect-count, the camera's opaque and glass
lists and each refreshed shadow cascade keep one slot per object, and the
cull pass writes instance count 0 or 1 into each slot. By default only the
blocks of 64 slots that this frame's cull can fill are issued, in slot
order. The renderer keeps a CPU copy of the object table and the far layer's
residency to decide this; a block written in the last 8 frames is issued
whole. The frame is the same either way. With `--no-draw-ranges` every slot
is issued as before: about 640 k Metal draws a frame and 26 ms of
render-thread time on golden_gate, plus wgpu's indirect-validation pass
before the water pass. The bench also reports `fixed_slot_lists` (slots
issued per frame), `gpu_serial_ms` (each pass's end minus the previous
pass's end, which adds up to the frame; begin-to-end pass windows overlap
on Apple GPUs), the PPR and scene-meter passes, the bridges' wire overlay
(`wire_overlay`, drawn after the bloom; untimed, its time was counted in
the scene meter's share), and a budget verdict of "not run" when no
standard path ran.

The photo-date sky (`--no-gg3-sky`, eo-look `season`): `--no-photo-season`
keeps round 2's season curve, read once at load, instead of the cited
grassland phenology the far layer reads every frame; `--no-cirrus2` keeps
round 2's one high deck and its streaks instead of the second cirrus deck,
which takes its METAR amount (FEW/SCT/BKN from KSFO, or a shot's
`sky_oktas`) and covers that share of the plane at every mip;
`--no-sky-day-fade` lets a clear day's sky flip at the game day boundary
instead of giving way to the next day's over the world clock's 21-24 h;
`--no-live-calendar` keeps the date on 22 September instead of advancing one
day each game day (the renderer's sky tells the calendar the world clock).

The colour and camera (`--no-gg3-colour`, eo-look `features`):
`--no-sun-spectrum2` draws the direct sun's colour from the atmosphere's
three channels instead of its spectrum; `--no-meter-highlights` restores the
scene meter's 2 % highlight hold and its centre weighting (by default the
meter averages the whole frame, ISO 2720's K = 12.5). `--no-sky-calibration`,
`--no-camera-style` and `--no-haze-visibility` are reserved and change
nothing. `EO_LOOK_DEBUG=10..17` (also read as `EO_AO_DEBUG`) draws one term
of a surface's scene radiance, log2-encoded over 2^-14..2^4: 10 all, 11 sun
diffuse, 12 sun specular, 13 sky diffuse, 14 sky specular, 15 bounce, 16
none (aerial in-scatter only), 17 unit white; decode with eo-look
`probe::decode_level`. (The colour track's ACES 1.3 option is not in this
build until its licence text is approved.)

The terrain surface (`--no-gg3-ground`, eo-landmarks `features::gg3_ground`;
the survey readers need `EO_SURVEY_ROOT`, and without it the ground draws as
round 2): `--no-facet-normal-fix` keeps round 2's two-sided flip of the
interpolated normal instead of orienting it by the triangle and bending it
toward the eye's horizon; `--no-terrain-detail` leaves out the 1-2 m relief
from the 1 m DEM (published through eo-data `detail_ground`, which the
plants and rocks sit on); `--no-terrain-materials` the ground materials on
the NAIP land cover; `--no-sand-detail2` keys the damp band, wrack and
footprints to round 2's constant slope instead of each beach's measured
foreshore. `--no-bluff-soil` and `--no-bank-grade` are reserved.

The scanned rock (`--no-gg3-rock`, eo-landmarks `rocks::scan`; the Poly Haven
scans under `EO_SURVEY_ROOT`, cooked under `EO_NATIVE_CACHE`): `--no-scan-rocks`
leaves out every scan (round 2's procedural rock); `--no-cliff-dressing` the
cliff band's rock faces; `--no-surf-rocks` the intertidal rock field from the
imagery and the bed; `--no-scan-riprap` the
revetment's armour as scanned stone; `--no-rock-geology` keeps round 2's
colours instead of tints from the mapped geologic units.

The coastal vegetation, round 3 (`--no-gg3-vegetation`, eo-landmarks
`vegetation::Gg3`): `--no-naip-planting` keeps round 2's placement instead of
scrub, trees and grass placed from the 0.6 m NAIP image; `--no-cliff-rock-cover`
lets plants stand on a cliff over a beach, mapped bare rock or a sand cliff;
`--no-far-species` keeps round 2's far tree forms instead of the near tier's
coastal species; `--no-far-tree-light` keeps round 2's far card light instead
of a leaf volume with transmission; `--no-bloom-date` leaves out the cited
bloom window and the flower heads' own colour; `--no-grass-color-variety`
keeps every grass bunch in the ground's tone (no meadow mosaic of green and
cured patches by aspect, slope and hollow, no grey-green or brown patches, no
lighter seed heads or flower-tinted tips, no olive to grey-green scrub).
`--no-veg-scans` is reserved.

The second grass pass (`--no-grass2`, eo-landmarks `vegetation::Grass2`):
`--no-meadow-cover` keeps the scattered bunches and tufts instead of a meadow
patch in every 1 m cell of grass, scrub and (inside the NAIP image) the bare
class, each a matted thatch and short-sward mat with blades over it, and keeps
the soil the terrain materials draw on the bare class; `--no-meadow-types`
makes every patch short sward (no wild oats, fescue tussocks, clover and
forbs, ice plant or thatch patches by slope, aspect, bluff edge and scrub, and
no low scrub mounds in the scrub's gaps); `--no-blade-shading` leaves out the
blades' leaf optics (transmission, keel and rolled-margin normals, clump
occlusion, waxy sheen).

The third grass pass (`--no-grass3`, eo-landmarks `vegetation::Grass3`; it acts
inside the second pass's meadow cover): `--no-scrub-mounds` leaves out the
lumpy coastal scrub mounds (coyote brush, sagebrush, bush lupine; leaf clumps
0.5-1.5 m tall) planted where the land cover and NAIP show scrub (including
the summer image's bright green) and on the Marin headland's seaward slopes;
`--no-scrub-bloom` leaves the lupine stands out of flower (coyote brush
there); `--no-bare-ground` gives the eroded ground (steep faces the NAIP shows
bare, red-brown soil, warm bare knoll tops) back to the meadow and the
terrain's bare class back to sward, instead of a red-brown soil mat with
sparse tufts; `--no-meadow-breakup` keeps the meadow's round type and cure
patches, the patch levels' fixed distances (a band across the view) and the
mats' full discs; `--no-mat-free-meadow` puts back the ground disc ("mat",
about 1.9 m across, in its own colour and darkened near the eye) that every
meadow and bare-soil patch laid under its blades, which on a slope overlapped
as dark plates with hard rims (without it the terrain is the meadow's floor;
the bluff patches' mats go too; [the hills plan](../Docs/HILLS-PLAN.md), stage
H1).

The surf and bathymetry (`--no-gg3-water`, eo-water `switches`; the CUDEM
tiles under `EO_SURVEY_ROOT`): `--no-bathymetry` keeps the geometric exposure
instead of the measured sea bed and the swell refracted and shoaled over it;
`--no-surf-break` the surf breaking where the depth says; `--no-swash-foam`
the bay beaches' thin swash line; `--no-obstacle-foam` the white water at
rocks, the fender and walls; `--no-water-body2` keeps round 2's constant
grey in the reflected sky. `--no-ppr-dash` is reserved. `--no-gg2-water`
turns these off too.

The Golden Gate's fifth pass (`--no-gg3-bridge` or `--no-bridges5`, eo-landmarks
`suspension`): `--no-paint-brdf2` keeps the fourth pass's paint instead of
the District's named colour match as a dielectric film; `--no-paint-weathering`
the chalking, touch-up patches, rust bloom at rivet rows and dirt on ledges;
`--no-concrete2` the pylons', housings', piers' and fender's weathered
concrete with its pour lifts and zero-mean staining; `--no-leg-cells` the
tower legs' cellular section and the portal casings' own shadow;
`--no-cable-detail` the cable bands, four-rope suspender groups, spacing
clamps and the lamp standards at the published 150 ft pitch.

### Quality tiers

`--quality` expands, once every switch has been read, to the settings
below; a switch it sets that is also given on the line (`--shadow-map`,
`--no-ssr`, `--sky-quality`, a lighting switch or its `--no-` form) keeps
its own value. Each tier has its own frame budget for `--check-budget` and
`--check-max` at 1920 x 1080; `--budget-ms` overrides it. The bench JSON
records the tier and budget under `settings` and every lighting option under
`lighting`. Every lighting addition draws (none is plumbing only any more).
A feature that is off at start has its shader code compiled out of every
pipeline (eo-render `look::ShaderFeatures`, chosen from the switches before
the renderer is made): the lighting additions, `--grime`, `--street-life`,
`--structures`, `--tunnels`, `--shop-light`, `--facade-clutter`,
`--look cinematic` (`SF_CINEMATIC`), the playable house (`SF_HOUSE`: its
terms, its varyings and its finishes' runtime branches), the sea bed's
habitats (`SF_SEABED`, with a seabed object in the world),
`--cove-sediment` (`SF_COVE_SEDIMENT`), `--facade-glass`, `--far-facades`,
`--facade-weather`, `--canyon-glass`, `--far-relief` (`SF_FAR_RELIEF`),
`--facade-patina` (`SF_FACADE_PATINA`), `--window-depth` (`SF_WINDOW_DEPTH`),
`--street-pbr` (`SF_STREET_PBR`), `--street-wear` (`SF_STREET_WEAR`),
`--street-glare` (`SF_STREET_GLARE`), `--street-aggregate` (`SF_STREET_AGGREGATE`),
`--plaza-setts` (`SF_PLAZA_SETTS`), `--walk-relief` (`SF_WALK_RELIEF`), `--matte-wheel-paths` (`SF_MATTE_WHEEL_PATHS`), `--aged-aggregate` (`SF_AGED_AGGREGATE`),
`--wet-surfaces` (`SF_WET_SURFACES`), `--wet-relief` (`SF_WET_RELIEF`),
`--lamp-streaks` (`SF_LAMP_STREAKS`: its pass too), `--screen-reflect` (`SF_SCREEN_REFLECT`),
`--probe-sky` (`SF_PROBE_SKY`: its pipelines), `--car-materials` (`SF_CAR_MATERIALS`),
`--car-reflections` (`SF_CAR_REFLECTIONS`), `--car-lamps` (`SF_CAR_LAMPS`, and the light gather's `LT_CAR_LAMPS`),
`--car-wet` (`SF_CAR_WET`), `--car-finish` (`SF_CAR_FINISH`), `--car-panels` (`SF_CAR_PANELS`, in the agents' own constants),
`--night-photometry` (`SF_NIGHT_PHOTOMETRY`), `--lamp-bounce` (`SF_LAMP_BOUNCE`),
`--urban-sky` (`SF_URBAN_SKY`), `--office-floors` (`SF_OFFICE_FLOORS`),
`--room-depth` (`SF_ROOM_DEPTH`), `--lit-rooms` (`SF_LIT_ROOMS`),
`--lamp-units` (`SF_LAMP_UNITS`), `--luminaires` (`SF_LUMINAIRES`),
`--rear-lamps` (`SF_REAR_LAMPS`), `--signal-spill` (`SF_SIGNAL_SPILL`) and
`--wire-light` (`SF_WIRE_LIGHT`); `--car-contact` is a pass of its own whose
code joins the agents' module only with it on. With none of them the frame
costs what it cost before they existed; with one on, its code is the code it
ran before, so its output is the same.

| Tier | Sets | Budget (ms) |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| `low` | `--shadow-map 2048`, `--no-ssr`, `--no-contact-shadows`, `--sky-quality low`, `--no-ao`, `--no-shafts`, `--no-bloom`, `--surveyed-furniture`, `--tree-basins` | 8 |
| `medium` (default) | the street look: `--grime 1`, `--stand-ins`, `--surveyed-furniture`, `--tree-basins` (made the default with the owner's approval on 2026-11-20, after the citywide street audit; `--no-grime --no-stand-ins --no-surveyed-furniture --no-tree-basins` gives the frame before it) | 10 |
| `high` | `--cascade-blend`, `--far-shadows 1`, `--ambient-volume 1` (with `--ground-bounce`), `--light-shadows 16`, `--headlight-shadows 2`, `--lit-fog 1`, `--smooth-time`, `--shadow-slope-bias`, `--grime 1`, `--street-life 1`, `--stand-ins`, `--photo-finish 1`, `--facade-clutter 1`, `--shop-light 1`, `--car-detail 1`, `--surveyed-furniture`, `--tree-basins`, `--look cinematic`, `--water-wind-texture`, `--water-lean` | 16.6 |
| `hyper` | `high`, then `--shadow-map 4096`, `--sky-quality high`, `--far-shadows 2`, `--ambient-volume 2`, `--probe-grid 2`, `--light-shadows 48`, `--light-shadow-moving`, `--headlight-shadows 8`, `--lit-fog 2`, `--grime 2`, `--street-life 2`, `--photo-finish 2`, `--facade-clutter 2`, `--shop-light 2`, `--car-detail 2`, `--surveyed-furniture`, `--tree-basins`, `--look cinematic` | 33.3 |

The sea's wind texture (`--water-wind-texture`: cat's-paw gust patches,
the tidal jet's rough water through the Golden Gate, the rotated re-read of
the chop cascade) and its lean (`--water-lean`: the anisotropic cascade fade
and sub-pixel slope variance) are drawn from `high` up; `low` and `medium`
leave them out of the water's shaders (each `--no-` form turns it off at any
tier, the positive form on at any tier). With the water's features compiled
in as constants, the two together cost the `medium` water pass about 0.5 ms
on `bay` (2.6 -> 3.1 ms of `water_and_glass` GPU time), 0.25 ms on
`golden_gate` and nothing measurable on `waterfront`.

The tiers are tuned to their budgets on the bench paths (stage T2). With
every feature compiled in, what a tier costs is mostly the lit shader's
code, not the features' own passes: `--probe-grid` (every lit pixel reads
two probes) cost the old `high` 4.9 ms on `city_overview` and 1.6 ms on the
street paths, so the grid is `hyper`'s alone (a bare `--probe-grid` still
takes 1); `--no-ao`, `--no-shafts` and `--no-bloom` (which have no positive
form, so `low` always drops them) take 0.3-0.6 ms off the street paths.
`low` cannot reach 8 ms on `city_overview`: about 4.8 ms of that path is
the CPU's draw encoding (Metal has no indirect-count draw, so each
compacted slot is a CPU draw call; see [What is drawn](#what-is-drawn)) and 3.9 ms the
city's draw, which no tier switch changes without drawing less of the
city. That is left to the owner (draw less in `low`, or a higher `low`
budget); the budget stays 8 ms. Frame total medians, ms, from
`--bench --check-max --quality TIER` on an idle Apple M-series machine
(1920 x 1080, 720 frames a path; worst frames over the budget are named
in the bench JSON):

| Path | `low` | `medium` | `high` | `hyper` | `hyper` `--structures --tunnels` |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `street_walk` | 3.2 | 3.9 | 6.0 | 7.9 | 10.7 |
| `city_overview` | 10.7 | 10.9 | 15.1 | 20.0 | 22.5 |
| `kit_street` | 3.3 | 4.0 | 6.1 | 7.9 | 10.3 |
| `drive` | 3.2 | 3.8 | 6.0 | 7.7 | 10.2 |
| `bay` | 5.2 | 5.5 | 7.4 | 8.9 | 9.9 |
| `rain_street_walk` | 4.2 | 4.8 | 7.7 | 9.9 | 12.1 |
| `fog_elevated_sweep` | 5.3 | 5.7 | 8.3 | 10.8 | 11.4 |
| `night_kit_street` | 3.6 | 4.2 | 6.7 | 9.0 | 11.2 |
| `night_waterfront` | 4.5 | 4.9 | 6.7 | 8.2 | 8.5 |

The settings screen (`eo-ui` `settings.rs`) has a "Lighting and detail"
row with these tiers (Low, Medium, High, Hyper real) and a "Hyper real"
preset beside High, Medium and Low; each preset sets the tier of its name.
A separate World block holds "Grade-separated roads" (off, on, on with
tunnels: `--structures`, `--tunnels`), which no preset touches. In the
`--ui` window the stored record applies at start as if `--quality TIER`,
`--no-shadows` (when "Sun shadows" is off) and `--structures [--tunnels]`
were written first on the line, so any switch on the line wins. A change in
the menu reaches the running renderer for the sun's shadows and map size,
the sky (with its shafts and bloom), the surface occlusion and the photo
finish, turns lamp and headlight shadows, the lit fog
and the smooth clock on and off when the game started with them, and turns
the far shadows, ambient volume and probe grid off; facade clutter,
stand-ins, weathering decals, street life, shop windows, the attached
features and the compiled-out lighting coming on and the roads take effect
after restart,
as the rows' notes say. A fresh install stores Medium (the street look);
a file written before the row existed takes the tier of the preset its
rows match (else Medium). The captures and `--ui-bench` never read the file, and
`--no-settings-apply` keeps the window to the line alone.

### Scale of the ground plan

The street plan is drawn wider than the survey about the map origin, an
approved deviation from the California street standards recorded in
`eo-data/src/standards.rs`. `--ground-scale`, `--height-scale`,
`--people-scale` and `--vehicle-scale` override the factors for experiments,
and `--low-camera` / `--default-camera` choose the player camera height.
Experiments are not shipped defaults.

## Environment variables

A regular launch uses built-in defaults. To keep optional `EO_*` overrides
in one place, create `environment.json` beside the app's `settings.json`.
Use `%APPDATA%\Earth Online` on Windows and
`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/earth-online` (or `~/.config/earth-online`) on macOS and
Linux. If `EO_UI_SETTINGS` points to another settings file, use its folder.

```json
{
  "EO_CUMULUS": "1",
  "EO_NATIVE_CACHE": "/path/to/earth-online-cache"
}
```

Use JSON strings for values, including numbers. The desktop app reads the
file at startup in all modes. Shell variables take precedence over the
file, and command-line switches retain their existing precedence. Changes
take effect on the next launch. An absent file uses normal defaults.

| Variable | What |
| --- | --- |
| `EO_REPO_ROOT` | The repository root, if it cannot be found from the working directory |
| `EO_NATIVE_CACHE` | The cache folder (default `native/cache/`, inside the game's folder in a packaged build) |
| `EO_UI_SETTINGS` | Where the interface keeps its settings file (default: the user's application-data folder) |
| `EO_UI_FONT_DIR` | A font folder to use instead of the system font folder |
| `EO_TRACE_LOAD` | Print timings of the world load |
| `EO_PHOTOS_DIR` | Where photo mode saves its pictures (default: `photos` in the application-data folder the settings file is in) |
| `EO_CUMULUS` | `1` draws the fair-weather cumulus field under a clear sky (off by default; the overcast, fog and rain decks are drawn either way) |
| `EO_CAPTURE_HOLD_SKY` | `1` holds the sky's clock at `--world-time` for every pose of an `EO_CAPTURE_POSES` file, so the drifting clouds are the same in two runs (matched captures) |
| `EO_BENCH_POSES` | A pose file (the `EO_CAPTURE_POSES` format): each pose a held-camera bench path `pose-<name>`, timed like the others (`--segments pose-a,pose-b` runs only those) |
| `EO_CAPTURE_STREETS` | `N` with `--bench --capture-props2`: the citywide street audit instead of the props cards: up to N street-level views spread over the whole map (a grid of cells over the street plan, the longest run of a class taken in turn in each: arterial, collector, residential), seen from the wider footway at eye height, written as `capture-<label>-street-NN-<class>.png` with the poses file, so a change to the street is judged across the city; deterministic (the same plan gives the same views) |
| `EO_GRIME_STREET_V1` | `1` (a diagnostic) keeps the street weathering `--grime` drew before the road's cracks and the markings' wear were redrawn (the polygon crack network, the flaked paint, the scan's full holes), for matched A/B captures on one build |

The [optional assets](#optional-assets) are read from `external/` at the
repository root; the variables below point a set at another folder.

### Optional asset folders

Each set is read from its folder under `external/` (the repository is found
as [Where the data comes from](#where-the-data-comes-from) describes) unless
its variable names another folder; an empty variable counts as unset. When
the folder does not hold a copy that matches the set's manifest, that
feature is turned off, the log says so in one line, and the game runs
without it. `--no-external` stops the game reading `external/`, so only the
variables count.

| Variable | Default folder | What it holds | Read by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `EO_PEOPLE_ROOT` | `external/people` | Rocketbox avatars | The game |
| `EO_PEOPLE_MOTIONS_ROOT` | `external/people-motions` | Rocketbox motion clips | The game |
| `EO_PEOPLE_STYLES_ROOT` | `external/people-styles` | 100STYLE walking styles | The game |
| `EO_TEST_CHARACTERS_ROOT` | none | The local test demo's `public` directory: the demo avatars and motion packs (`assets/test-characters-manifest.json`) | The game with `--test-character` |
| `EO_CC0_ROOT` | `external/cc0` | Scanned CC0 texture sets (Poly Haven, ambientCG) | The game, `tools/fetch-cc0.mjs` |
| `EO_AUDIO_ROOT` | `external/audio` | Sound clips | The game |
